The Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, himself narrates the details of this second encounter, as reported in the hadith of Jabir ibn Abdullah Al-Ansari, may Allah be pleased with him: "While I was walking, I heard a voice from the sky. I looked up and saw the same angel who had visited me in the cave of Hira, sitting on a chair between the sky and the earth."

1. Seeing the Angel in His True Form

This time, Jibril did not come in the form of a man but appeared in his magnificent angelic form in which Allah created him. It was an awesome and majestic sight, intended by Allah to firmly establish the heart of His Prophet, and to fill his heart with certainty in the power of his source and the greatness of his sender. He saw him sitting on a great throne that filled the horizon, possessing six hundred wings, as the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, described him in another instance. It was a scene beyond description, embodying the greatness of the Creator in His creation.

The effect of this majestic sight was intense on the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him. He says: "I was terrified of him. I returned and said, 'Cover me, cover me! Wrap me, wrap me!'." He returned once again to his home, to his tranquility and source of security, Lady Khadijah, seeking warmth and cover from the intensity of the terror that had struck him.

2. The Revelation of the Command to Arise and Warn

In these moments of his human vulnerability, while he was wrapped in his garments, the heavenly call came. It was not just a revelation, but a command to rise and begin an entirely new phase. Allah revealed to him His words:

﴾O you who covers himself [with a garment], (1) Arise and warn, (2) And your Lord glorify, (3) And your clothing purify, (4) And uncleanliness avoid, (5) And do not confer favor to acquire more, (6) And for your Lord be patient. (7)﴿ [Al-Muddaththir: 1-7].
(﴿يَا أَيُّهَا الْمُدَّثِّرُ (1) قُمْ فَأَنذِرْ (2) وَرَبَّكَ فَكَبِّرْ (3) وَثِيَابَكَ فَطَهِّرْ (4) وَالرُّجْزَ فَاهْجُرْ (5) وَلَا تَمْنُن تَسْتَكْثِرُ (6) وَلِرَبِّكَ فَاصْبِرْ (7)﴾)

These verses were a roadmap and a complete methodology for the call to Allah:

  • ﴾O you who covers himself [with a garment]﴿: A gentle and affectionate call, addressing him in his human state, as if to say: O you who has covered himself in his garments from the intensity of the situation, arise, for this is not the time for sleep or warmth.
  • ﴾Arise and warn﴿: This is the essence of the commission. The time of personal prophethood has ended, and the era of the public message has begun. Arise from your bed, arise from your seclusion, arise from your stillness, to warn the people of Allah's punishment and call them to His Oneness.
  • ﴾And your Lord glorify﴿: This is the foundation of the call. The goal is to magnify Allah alone in the hearts and on the earth, so that nothing is held above His command, and no one is associated with Him.
  • ﴾And your clothing purify﴿: A command for both physical and spiritual purity. The caller to Allah must be pure in his outward appearance and his inner self, with clean clothes and a pure heart, to be a good example for those he invites.
  • ﴾And uncleanliness avoid﴿: A command to forsake all causes of punishment, foremost among them being polytheism and idols, and all sins and misdeeds.
  • ﴾And do not confer favor to acquire more﴿: A great ethical principle in the call. Do not give your gift, nor exert your effort, while seeking a worldly return or expecting people to repay your favor with more. Let your work be purely for Allah.
  • ﴾And for your Lord be patient﴿: A conclusion that prepares for the arduous path ahead. Arising to warn, glorifying the Lord, and avoiding uncleanliness will bring you enmity, harm, and hardship. The weapon with which you will face all of that is patience—not for any goal, but patience purely for the sake of your Generous Lord.

3. The Immediate Response: "The Era of Sleep has Passed"

The Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, understood the message immediately and realized that his quiet life had ended forever. He rose at once, shook off his cloak, and said his famous words to his wife: "The era of sleep has passed, O Khadijah."

That was the moment of the true rising. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, rose and remained standing for twenty-three years. He did not rest or settle; he did not live for himself or his family, but for the call of Allah. He rose carrying the heavy burden on his shoulders—the burden of the great trust, the burden of guiding humanity, the burden of struggle and striving in every field.

Thus, the command ﴾Arise and warn﴿ was not just the beginning of the call; it was the declaration of the birth of a nation and a commission for everyone who came after him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, to carry this concern, to fulfill this role, and to be a warner and a caller to Allah with insight.

Conclusion: The Types and Manners of Revelation

The first and second encounters with Jibril, peace be upon him, despite their awe and greatness, were not the only forms in which revelation descended upon the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him. The divine connection with the prophets was a multifaceted phenomenon, manifesting the wisdom and power of Allah in conveying His message in a manner befitting the situation and state. The Holy Quran summarizes these forms in a comprehensive verse. Allah, the Exalted, says:

﴾And it is not for any human being that Allah should speak to him except by revelation or from behind a partition or that He sends a messenger to reveal, by His permission, what He wills. Indeed, He is Most High and Wise.﴿ [Ash-Shura: 51].
(﴿وَمَا كَانَ لِبَشَرٍ أَن يُكَلِّمَهُ اللَّهُ إِلَّا وَحْيًا أَوْ مِن وَرَاءِ حِجَابٍ أَوْ يُرْسِلَ رَسُولًا فَيُوحِيَ بِإِذْنِهِ مَا يَشَاءُ ۚ إِنَّهُ عَلِيٌّ حَكِيمٌ﴾)

Based on this noble verse and what has been reported in the pure Prophetic Sunnah, we can detail the levels of revelation with which Allah distinguished His Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him:

  • First Level: Righteous Dreams during Sleep
    This was the first and lightest stage of revelation, serving as a prelude and training for the Prophet's, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, soul to receive the message. The Mother of the Believers, Aisha, may Allah be pleased with her, said: "The first thing with which the revelation to the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, began was the righteous dream in his sleep. He would not see a dream but that it would come true like the bright splitting of the dawn" (Agreed upon). The dreams of prophets are revelation and truth. An example is the dream of the intimate friend of Allah, Ibrahim, peace be upon him, about sacrificing his son Isma'il: ﴾He said, "O my son, indeed I have seen in a dream that I [must] sacrifice you..."﴿ [As-Saffat: 102].
  • Second Level: Inspiration and Casting into the Heart (Nafth fi'r-ru')
    This is when the angel casts the meaning into the Prophet's, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, heart in a subtle way, and the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, finds the knowledge necessary in his heart, with complete certainty that it is from Allah. In this regard, the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "The Holy Spirit (i.e., Jibril) inspired into my heart that no soul will die until it has completed its provision and its lifespan. So fear Allah and be moderate in seeking (your livelihood)" (Classified as authentic by Al-Albani).
  • Third Level: The Angel Coming in the Form of a Man
    This is the form that occurred in the first encounter in the cave and was repeated many times thereafter. Jibril, peace be upon him, would often come in the form of the handsome companion Dihyah al-Kalbi. Sometimes he would come in the form of a stranger whom the companions did not know, as in the famous hadith of Islam, Iman, and Ihsan, when he came to ask the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, about the pillars of the religion.
  • Fourth Level: The Angel Coming in His True Angelic Form
    This happened only twice throughout the entire period of the message. The first time was after the interruption of revelation when he saw him sitting on a throne between the sky and the earth. The second time was during the journey of the Night Ascension (Mi'raj) at the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary (Sidrat al-Muntaha), as Allah, the Exalted, said: ﴾And he certainly saw him in another descent, (13) At the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary. (14)﴿ [An-Najm: 13-14].
  • Fifth Level: Revelation Coming Like the Ringing of a Bell
    This was the most severe and heaviest type of revelation on the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him. When Al-Harith ibn Hisham asked him, "O Messenger of Allah, how does the revelation come to you?" he replied: "Sometimes it comes to me like the ringing of a bell, and that is the hardest on me. Then it ceases, and I have comprehended what was said" (Agreed upon). It was so intense that he would perspire profusely on a very cold day, and his body would become so heavy that his she-camel would kneel on the ground if the revelation came to him while he was riding it.
  • Sixth Level: Allah Speaking to Him Directly from Behind a Veil
    This great level was granted by Allah to some of His prophets, foremost among them Musa, peace be upon him: ﴾And Allah spoke to Musa with [direct] speech﴿ [An-Nisa: 164]. Our Prophet Muhammad, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, was granted this honor on the night of the Ascension (Mi'raj), when Allah enjoined upon him and his nation the five daily prayers.

This diversity in the modes of revelation is evidence of the greatness of the message and the weight of the trust, and of the divine wisdom in choosing the optimal method to deliver His word to the Seal of His Prophets and Messengers, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, who conveyed the message, fulfilled the trust, advised the nation, and strove in the cause of Allah as he ought to strive until certainty (death) came to him.

Introduction: The Key to Benefiting from the Prophetic Biography (Sirah)

All praise is due to Allah, and peace and blessings be upon the Messenger of Allah, our Prophet Muhammad (نبينا محمد), and upon his family and all his companions.

When we immerse ourselves in the fragrant ocean of the Prophetic Biography (Sirah), we are not merely embarking on a journey through time to narrate stories and follow historical events. To engage with the Sirah in this superficial manner is like one who possesses a magnificent treasure but is content to only look at it from afar, without extending a hand to draw from it. The true treasure and the ultimate goal lie in learning how to benefit from this blessed biography and transform it from mere historical knowledge into a practical, real-life methodology. The more a person delves into its study with the intention of contemplation and application, the more they will find in it an answer to every question and a solution to every dilemma. They will find how relationships are built, how nations are led, how souls are nurtured, and how hardships are faced with steadfastness and certainty. It is truly a complete roadmap for the righteous individual and society, charting the course for us in all aspects of life.

The ultimate objective of studying the Sirah is not just to receive and memorize information, but to possess the intellectual and spiritual tools that enable us to extract its benefits and fruits ourselves. Just as books of Quranic exegesis (Tafsir) are accessible and available to all, the greater challenge remains in learning how to contemplate the Quran and understand its profound messages that transcend the literal meaning of the words. The same applies to the Sirah; what is important is to learn how to engage with it as a mirror upon which we reflect our own reality and from which we derive solutions to our problems. The Sirah is the practical application of the Quran; it is the Quran walking on earth. If we want to understand how the verses of patience, mercy, justice, and wisdom are embodied, we have no choice but to look at the situations faced by the Prophet ﷺ.

This is the most crucial tool, which, if mastered, will launch us on an endless journey of understanding and application. At that point, we will not be mere transmitters of what commentators have said; rather, the door of understanding will be opened for us, and we will have our own insight and research that connects the events of the past with the challenges of the present. We will learn how to derive the divine universal laws (Sunan) from behind the events and how to understand the higher objectives of the Shari'ah through his actions and sayings, peace and blessings be upon him. Possessing this tool liberates us from blind imitation and grants us the insight to distinguish between the constant and the variable, between principles and branches.

With this methodology, we move beyond mere listening and momentary emotional influence to the broader horizons of real change and practical application in our daily lives. The Sirah becomes an inexhaustible source of inspiration that we draw upon in our personal decisions, in raising our children, in our professional dealings, and in our vision for the future of our Ummah (nation). It is an invitation to live with the Sirah, not just to read about it, so that it becomes a compass that guides us and a fuel that provides us with strength and resolve in every step we take towards Allah.

Lessons from the Beginning of Revelation: The Nature of the Path to Allah

One of the greatest fruits we can extract when contemplating the events at the beginning of the revelation is understanding the nature of the path to Allah. This understanding does not come from a single, isolated event, but from connecting the events to see the complete picture. When we combine two incidents that occurred at the start of the prophethood, the reality of this path becomes crystal clear, without any ambiguity:

  1. The Squeeze of Jibril (جبريل عليه السلام) on the Prophet ﷺ in the Cave of Hira: This intense embrace was not a mere passing event; it carried a practical message about the hardships the Prophet ﷺ would face on his journey.
  2. The Explicit Statement of Waraqah ibn Nawfal (ورقة بن نوفل): When he said to the Prophet ﷺ, "...Oh, I wish I were a young man! I wish I were alive when your people will drive you out." Waraqah was not distorting the truth or flattering him; he was informing him of an established universal law: "No man has ever come with something like what you have brought except that he was met with hostility."

When we place the practical situation (the squeeze) alongside the explicit statement (the expulsion and hostility), the nature of the path becomes clear: it is a path fraught with trials and hardships. The squeeze from Jibril was not meant to be understood only in that moment; Waraqah's statement came to complete the picture, saying clearly: "As long as you are on this path, may Allah be your aid, for you will surely face adversities."

Trials are the Way of the Prophets and Their Followers

This reality is not unique to Prophet Muhammad ﷺ alone; it is the way of Allah (Sunnah) with all messengers and their followers throughout the ages. The Holy Quran confirms this rule in many places, as if Allah is telling His Prophet and us after him: Do not be surprised, for this is the path.

Allah Almighty says:

{Nothing is said to you, [O Muhammad], except what was said to the messengers before you.} [Fussilat: 43]

He also says:

{And if they deny you, [O Muhammad] - so were messengers denied before you.} [Fatir: 4]

And Allah commanded His Prophet to say:

{Say, "I am not something original among the messengers..."} [Al-Ahqaf: 9]
, meaning that what is happening to me has happened to them, and it will continue with everyone who follows this path.

Therefore, what we see today of the trials faced by Muslims everywhere—whether at the level of the Ummah, as in Palestine and elsewhere, or at the individual level—is nothing but the fulfillment of this divine law. If the best of creation ﷺ was told on the very first day, "Your people will drive you out," then no one after him should be surprised if they face hostility or harm for the sake of their religion.

Personal Application: Prepare Yourself for Patience

This universal law is not limited to the level of the Ummah; it applies to every individual who embarks on the path of religious commitment. If the Ummah is tested, then the individual, by extension, will also be tested. Therefore, anyone who resolves to adhere to the religion of Allah must prepare themselves to face opposition and hostility, so that the shocks are not surprising.

When a young person decides to become committed, they begin to differ from their people and family in matters they have become accustomed to, even if they are Muslims. For instance, they might decide to:

  • Stop watching films and series.
  • A young man may grow his beard, or a woman may wear the Niqab.
  • Commit to praying in congregation at the mosque.
  • Seek Islamic knowledge and attend lessons.
  • Stop forbidden handshakes or attending mixed-gender weddings.

The moment they begin this opposition, they become a "stranger" in the eyes of others, and that is when the path of the prophets begins: the path of hostility. They may face explicit enmity, and in some cases, it may even lead to them being expelled from their home.

If something like this happens to you, remember that the Prophet ﷺ was driven out from the land most beloved to him. Remember that this path was never paved with roses. Whoever thinks so has not understood the laws of Allah regarding Da'wah (the call) and change.

The Mercy of Allah in Trials

However, it is from the mercy of Allah Almighty that trials come in proportion to one's faith, as stated in the authentic hadith where the Prophet ﷺ said:

"A man is tested according to his level of faith. If his faith is solid, his trials are intensified, and if there is weakness in his faith, he is tested according to his level of faith."

Because we live in a time of weakness, our trials are often lighter and easier. They might just be hurtful words, pressure from family, or minor threats. If a person cannot bear these simple trials, how can they aspire to walk this great path? Commitment is not just about good company, feasts, and trips; these are things that help one endure the real hardships of the path.

Therefore, these events at the beginning of the revelation were a form of psychological preparation for the Prophet ﷺ and for every caller after him: Prepare yourself mentally, for the path has hardship and trials, and this is the price of Paradise.

   

Recap: The Nature of the Path to Allah

  • A Path of Trials: The squeeze of Jibril and Waraqah's warning establish that the prophetic path is one of hardship and hostility.
  • A Universal Law: All prophets and their followers face trials; it is a divine sunnah.
  • Personal Application: Anyone who becomes religiously committed should expect opposition and prepare for it with patience.
  • Proportional Trials: Allah's mercy is that trials are proportionate to one's faith.

Qualities of a True Caller: Character Before Words

Amidst the fear and awe that the Prophet ﷺ felt after the revelation came for the first time, we find in the stance of Lady Khadijah (السيدة خديجة رضي الله عنها) a profound lesson in the principles and solid foundation of Da'wah. Her consolation was not merely emotional words; it was a powerful logical proof based on the noble character of the Prophet ﷺ as evidence that Allah would never forsake him. This stance establishes a golden rule: a true caller (Da'i) is one who calls with their character and conduct before their tongue.

Khadijah's Testimony: Character is the Divine Guarantee

When the Prophet ﷺ returned to Khadijah, his heart trembling, saying, "Cover me, cover me," and after he told her what had happened, her response was one of the greatest things ever said in this situation. She spoke her immortal words, as narrated by Imam al-Bukhari:

"Never! By Allah, Allah will never disgrace you. You keep good relations with your kith and kin, you help the poor and the destitute, you serve your guests generously, and you assist the deserving who are afflicted with calamities."

With her deep understanding, Khadijah linked the Prophet's ﷺ actions and noble qualities to Allah's protection and care for him. She was saying that anyone with such character—maintaining family ties, helping the weak, earning for the poor, honoring guests, and aiding the afflicted—could never be forsaken by Allah. This testimony shows that the personality and character of the caller are the foundation of their call and their true capital.

Who is the Caller (Da'i)?

The caller whose call bears fruit is not just someone with good logic or an eloquent speaker who memorizes and delivers sermons. These are superficial skills that many may possess. But the true caller is an integrated personality who calls to Allah with their entire being:

  • With their conduct and dealings: How they interact with people in their daily life.
  • With their character: How they translate the principles they preach into practical reality.
  • With their relationships: Their relationship with their family, neighbors, and the community around them.

This is the correct "seed" upon which the caller must be founded. If this character is the foundation, people will accept any words they say and in any way they present them, because they do not see mere words, but a living embodiment of the Quran. However, if the caller is eloquent but the effect of that is not seen in their dealings and behavior, their call will have little impact and may even repel people.

Evidence from the Quran and Sunnah on the Centrality of Character

The Quran and the Sunnah have emphasized that character is the essence of the Islamic message and the pillar of the call to Allah.

  • Allah's Testimony for His Prophet: The greatest testimony in honor of the Prophet ﷺ is that of his Lord regarding his good character, where Allah Almighty says: {And indeed, you are of a great moral character.} [Al-Qalam: 4] This verse places character at the highest level.
  • Character as the Reason People Gather: Allah Almighty explained that leniency and good character are the reason people rally around a caller, addressing His Prophet ﷺ: {So by mercy from Allah, you were lenient with them. And if you had been rude [in speech] and harsh in heart, they would have disbanded from about you.} [Aal 'Imran: 159]
  • The Purpose of the Mission: The Prophet ﷺ himself summarized the purpose of his mission in terms of character, saying in an authentic hadith:
    "I was sent only to perfect righteous character." (Narrated by Ahmad)
    This clarifies that perfecting noble morals is a primary objective of the message.
  • Character is the Heaviest on the Scale: The Prophet ﷺ explained the immense reward for good character on the Day of Judgment, saying:
    "Nothing is heavier on the Scale than good character." (Narrated by Abu Dawud and al-Tirmidhi)

The conclusion drawn from the stance of Lady Khadijah, supported by the clear text of the Quran and the authentic Sunnah, is that the call to Allah begins with self-building and purifying the soul with virtuous morals. If the caller is righteous in themselves, their call will be righteous and accepted by people, because they see in them the sincerity that connects words with deeds.

Recap: Character Before Words

  • Khadijah's Proof: Her reassurance to the Prophet ﷺ was based on a logical argument: Allah would never forsake someone with such a noble character.
  • The True Caller: A successful Da'i calls with their entire being—their actions, character, and relationships—not just their words.
  • Character as the Foundation: The Quran and Sunnah affirm that character is the essence of the message, the reason people gather, and the heaviest deed on the Scale.
  • Da'wah Begins with Self-Building: The first step in calling others is to be a living embodiment of the message yourself.

The Concept of "Wahy" (Revelation) in the Quran and Sunnah: Between the Linguistic and Shar'i Meaning

A precise understanding of the term "Wahy" (الوحي) is a key to understanding how heavenly messages were revealed and the difference between them and other forms of inspiration. The word is used in the Quran with multiple meanings, and it is essential to differentiate between its linguistic meaning and its technical (Shar'i) meaning.

First: The Linguistic Meaning of Wahy (Swift, Hidden Communication)

In the Arabic language, the root of Wahy indicates communicating something secretly and quickly. Anything you convey to another for them to know with speed and concealment is Wahy. Based on this broad meaning, the word is used in the Holy Quran in various contexts, including:

  1. Instinct and Natural Inspiration for Creatures:
    This is the innate guidance that Allah places in His creation to perform their functions. An example is His saying:
    {And your Lord inspired (awha) the bee, saying: "Take for yourself homes in the mountains and in the trees and in what they construct."} [An-Nahl: 68]
    Allah did not send Jibril to the bee; rather, He inspired it and instilled in its nature how to build its homes and gather nectar. This includes all innate guidance for creatures, like the inspiration for a newborn to suckle at its mother's breast.
  2. Divine Inspiration for the Awliya (Friends of Allah) and the Righteous:
    This is when something is placed in the heart that brings it tranquility. It is a form of guidance and affirmation that Allah singles out for some of His righteous servants. Examples include:
    • Revelation to the Mother of Moses: Allah says:
      {And We inspired (awhayna) the mother of Moses, "Suckle him; but when you fear for him, cast him into the river..."} [Al-Qasas: 7]
      The mother of Moses was not a prophetess, but it was a powerful inspiration and a casting of truth into her heart that guided her on what to do.
    • Revelation to the Disciples: Allah says:
      {And [remember] when I inspired (awhaytu) to the disciples, "Believe in Me and in My Messenger." They said, "We have believed..."} [Al-Ma'idah: 111]
      This was an inspiration and guidance from Allah for them to believe.
  3. The Whispering and Temptation of Devils:
    Because whispering is done secretly and quickly, the Quran uses the term Wahy in its linguistic sense. Allah says:
    {And indeed, the devils inspire (yuhuna) their allies to dispute with you.} [Al-An'am: 121]
    The devil casts doubts and falsehoods into the hearts of his allies in a hidden manner.
  4. A Quick Signal or Gesture:
    Wahy can also mean a non-verbal gesture, as in the story of Zakariyya (عليه السلام):
    {So he came out to his people from the prayer chamber and signaled (awha) to them to exalt [Allah] in the morning and afternoon.} [Maryam: 11]
    Meaning, he gestured and motioned quickly to them to do so.

Second: The Shar'i (Technical) Meaning of Wahy (Specific to Prophets)

In the Shar'i context, when the term "Wahy" is used, it refers to its technical meaning: "The speech of Allah Almighty revealed to one of His prophets."

This type of Wahy is the primary source of religion and has unique characteristics that distinguish it from everything else, the most important of which is complete infallibility (`Ismah - العصمة).

The Shar'i Wahy is protected from error and cannot be confused by the Prophet ﷺ with his personal thoughts, desires, or the whispers of Satan. It is pure truth from Allah Almighty, and Allah has guaranteed its protection at every stage.

The proof of its infallibility is Allah's statement about his Prophet Muhammad ﷺ: {Nor does he speak from [his own] inclination. * It is not but a revelation revealed.} [An-Najm: 3-4] This verse negates that his legislative speech stems from personal desire and confirms that it is pure revelation.

Even when it was confirmed in the Sahih collections that the Prophet ﷺ was affected by magic, this magic only impacted some of his worldly affairs and never affected the process of revelation or the conveying of the message, which affirms the divine protection of the message.

The Fundamental Difference between the Revelation of Prophets and the Inspiration of the Awliya

Confusing the revelation of prophethood with the inspiration of the Awliya (saints/friends of Allah) is one of the most dangerous pitfalls. The differences between them are fundamental and can be summarized in two points:

  1. Infallibility vs. Non-Infallibility:
    • Prophetic Revelation: Completely infallible; falsehood cannot approach it from before it or from behind it.
    • Inspiration of the Awliya: Not infallible. It is a thought that may be from Allah (which is the case for most of the Awliya), or it could be from the self and its desires, or from Satan. Therefore, it is prone to error.

    The best evidence for this is what happened to the masters of the Awliya in this Ummah, Abu Bakr (أبو بكر) and Umar (عمر رضي الله عنهما).

    Umar ibn al-Khattab (عمر بن الخطاب), about whom the Prophet ﷺ said: "Among the nations before you were people who were inspired (muhaddathun). If there is any of them in my Ummah, it is 'Umar ibn al-Khattab'" (Narrated by al-Bukhari), despite being "inspired," erred in his judgment at the Treaty of Hudaybiyyah when he strongly objected to its terms, while the correct position was with Abu Bakr, who agreed with the Prophet ﷺ.

    Similarly, Umar initially objected to Abu Bakr's decision to fight those who withheld Zakat, and the correct stance was with Abu Bakr.

    Conversely, Umar's inspiration was correct when he insisted that Abu Bakr should compile the Quran, while Abu Bakr was hesitant at first.

    If the masters of the Awliya like Umar and Abu Bakr could err in their inspiration and judgment, it is more likely for those below them to do so.

       
  2. A Source of Legislation vs. Being Judged by Legislation:
    • Prophetic Revelation: It is the primary source for legislation and beliefs. It establishes the religion and clarifies what is lawful (halal) and unlawful (haram).
    • Inspiration of the Awliya: It is never a source of legislation, nor can it be used as evidence for a new religious ruling. The religion was completed with the death of the Prophet ﷺ. Allah says:
      {This day I have perfected for you your religion and completed My favor upon you and have approved for you Islam as religion.} [Al-Ma'idah: 3]

    Therefore, the principle among Ahlus Sunnah wal Jama'ah is that inspiration, a righteous dream, or a spiritual unveiling that happens to a Wali is "judged upon, it is not a judge." This means it must be weighed against the scale of the Quran and Sunnah:

    • If it agrees with them, it is accepted as glad tidings and goodness, and can be taken for encouragement.
    • If it contradicts them, it is rejected and cast aside, and is considered either from the desires of the self or the deception of Satan.

    No one after the Prophet ﷺ is permitted to say, "My heart told me from my Lord" to legislate a new religion or to oppose the texts of the infallible revelation.

   

Recap: Understanding "Wahy" (Revelation)

  • Linguistic Meaning: Any swift, hidden communication (e.g., animal instinct, inspiration, demonic whispers).
  • Shar'i Meaning: Infallible communication from Allah to His prophets, which is the source of legislation.
  • Key Difference: Prophetic revelation is infallible and a source of law. The   _inspiration of saints (Awliya) is not infallible and must be judged by the Quran and Sunnah.

The Beginning of the Secret Call: The Methodology of Starting with the Nearest

After the clear divine command came to the Prophet ﷺ to begin the call and the warning in Allah's saying:

{O you who covers himself [with a garment], * Arise and warn} [Al-Muddathir: 1-2]
, a new and decisive phase began in the history of humanity. It was natural and logical, and by divine guidance, that this call would begin its first stage secretly and on a limited scale. Makkah at that time was the bastion of polytheism and the center of idol worship in the Arabian Peninsula. Any sudden public call to undermine this system would be met with overwhelming violence that could extinguish the light of the message at its inception.

Therefore, the wise prophetic methodology was to start with "the nearest, then the next nearest," a great principle in calling and change. A person begins with himself, then with his dependents, then with those around him. This method is the greatest proof of the caller's sincerity. The person whom his family knows in all his states—in his anger and pleasure, his travel and residence—is the hardest to convince if the caller is not truthful in his call and his life. The fact that his household members were the first to believe in him is the greatest testimony that his private life perfectly matched what he called to.

The Household: The First Fortress of the Call

The Prophet ﷺ set out to call to Allah, and the first circle he turned to was his own household, the people closest to him and most knowledgeable of his truthfulness and trustworthiness.

  1. Lady Khadijah bint Khuwaylid (السيدة خديجة بنت خويلد): The First Person to Ever Embrace Islam
    By consensus, Lady Khadijah (RA) was the first person to believe in the message and the very first to embrace Islam from among both men and women. She did not need proofs or miracles; her faith in the Prophet ﷺ and his truthfulness and character was enough for her to believe everything he brought. Her acceptance was not just belief; it was absolute support and complete reassurance. After his return from the Cave of Hira, she was his solace and refuge, and after he called her to Islam, she was the first to respond.
    The Prophet ﷺ cherished this great merit of hers throughout his life, frequently mentioning her and praising her, to the extent that Aisha (عائشة رضي الله عنها) said: "I was not jealous of any of the Prophet's ﷺ wives as I was of Khadijah, though I never saw her. But the Prophet ﷺ used to mention her often." He would say about her, "I was truly blessed with her love," and, "She believed in me when the people disbelieved, she affirmed my truthfulness when the people called me a liar, and she supported me with her wealth when the people withheld theirs..." Her acceptance of Islam is a source of pride for women of all times and proof that a woman can be the primary pillar of the greatest call.
  2. Ali ibn Abi Talib (علي بن أبي طالب): The First Among the Boys to Embrace Islam
    The second person to enter Islam was Ali ibn Abi Talib (RA), who was only ten years old at the time. Ali was living under the care and guardianship of the Prophet ﷺ. The reason for this, as the Sheikh mentioned, was that Abu Talib (أبو طالب) (the Prophet's uncle and Ali's father) had many children and little wealth. The Prophet ﷺ wanted to repay some of his uncle's kindness for raising him as a child, so he and his uncle al-Abbas (العباس) went to Abu Talib and offered to lighten his burden. The Prophet ﷺ took Ali, and al-Abbas took Ja'far.
    Ali grew up in the house of prophethood, witnessing the Prophet's ﷺ character and worship even before the mission began. When the revelation came, he saw the Prophet ﷺ and Khadijah praying and asked about it. The Prophet ﷺ invited him to Islam, and he accepted. Despite his young age,   _   his acceptance was based on awareness and understanding, for he was a bright and intelligent boy, and his upbringing in that pure household had prepared his heart to accept the truth immediately.
  3. Zayd ibn Harithah (زيد بن حارثة): The First Among the Freed Slaves to Embrace Islam
    Zayd ibn Harithah (RA) was considered a member of the household. He was the Prophet's ﷺ freed slave and his adopted son before Islam prohibited adoption. His story demonstrates the great character of the Prophet ﷺ, which was a direct reason for those closest to him to embrace Islam.
    Zayd was originally free but was kidnapped as a child and sold in the market of Ukaz. Hakim ibn Hizam bought him for Lady Khadijah, who in turn gifted him to the Prophet ﷺ. When his family learned of his whereabouts, his father and uncle came to Makkah with a large ransom to free him.
    When the Prophet ﷺ offered Zayd the choice between returning with his father as a free man or staying with him, Zayd's answer surprised everyone. He said: "I am not one to choose anyone over you. You are to me in the place of a father and an uncle." Zayd had seen such good companionship and noble character from the Prophet ﷺ that he preferred to remain with him as a slave over returning to his family as a free man.
    The Prophet ﷺ was so moved by this great stance that he took Zayd to the Ka'bah and announced before the Quraysh: "O you who are present, bear witness that Zayd is my son; he inherits from me, and I from him." From that day, he was called "Zayd ibn Muhammad." So when the Prophet ﷺ brought him the message of Islam, he did not hesitate for a moment. How could he hesitate when he had chosen his companion over his own family?
  4. The Daughters of the Prophet ﷺ: Zaynab, Ruqayyah, Umm Kulthum, and Fatimah (زينب ورقية وأم كلثوم وفاطمة)
    As the Sheikh mentioned, the books of Sirah do not detail the exact moment of the Prophet's ﷺ daughters' conversion, but it is taken for granted that they embraced Islam in the very first days of the call. It is inconceivable that their mother, Khadijah, the first to believe, would not be the first to call her daughters to this great good. They grew up in the house of prophethood and drank from the spring of Muhammadan character, so their acceptance of Islam was a natural and innate matter. They were the fruit of that goodly tree whose root is firm and whose branches are in the sky.
   

Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq: From the Wisdom of Da'wah to its Fruits

After the light of Islam shone in the Prophet's household, it was destined to extend to the person closest to the Prophet's ﷺ heart, his dear companion, Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq (أبو بكر الصديق رضي الله عنه). Abu Bakr represents a unique model; he was not just one of the first to embrace Islam, but he was the first to transform his faith into a powerful force of Da'wah that yielded immense good for the entire Ummah.

1. The Conversion of Al-Siddiq: The Response of a Pure Nature to the Truth

Biographers unanimously agree that Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq was the first free man to embrace Islam. But the most important feature of his conversion was not just his precedence, but his immediate, unhesitating response. The Prophet ﷺ expressed this by saying what means: "I did not call anyone to Islam except that there was some hesitation in them, except for Abu Bakr; he did not falter."

This immediate acceptance was not out of ignorance or naivety but was the logical and inevitable result of a lifelong relationship. Abu Bakr knew Muhammad ﷺ better than anyone. He knew his truthfulness and trustworthiness, and he knew he was not a poet or a soothsayer and could never lie. When his companion, upon whom he had never experienced a lie, came to him and said, "I am the Messenger of Allah," Abu Bakr's mind, heart, and sound nature told him: The one who never lied to people will not lie about Allah. His affirmation was a testament to the character of the Prophet ﷺ before it was a testament to the message itself.

2. The Secret to His Da'wah's Success: His Capital of Character and Social Standing

As soon as Abu Bakr embraced Islam, he did not sit at home content with his own salvation. He immediately transformed into a caller to Allah. The secret to the brilliant success he achieved from the very first day lay in the "capital" he had built throughout his life before Islam. Biographers described him, as the Sheikh summarized:

"He was an affable and beloved man, easy-going, of good character and known for his kindness. The men of his tribe were familiar with him and would sit with him for his knowledge, his trade, and his pleasant company."

The secret to his success can be detailed in the following points:

  • Noble Character: He was known for his good manners and pleasant disposition, which made him beloved by the people. Hearts naturally incline towards those who are kind to them.
  • Social Status: He was not a marginalized man but a chief among the leaders of Quraysh, a successful merchant, and an expert in the genealogies and histories of the Arabs. Thus, his words carried weight and value in his society.
  • A Wide Social Network: His home was like a forum where the men of the tribe would gather, which gave him a strong network of relationships that he invested in calling to goodness.

Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq possessed all the qualities of a successful caller even before the Prophet ﷺ was sent. When Islam came, he directed all these qualities to serve the new religion. His call was like rain falling on fertile land, bringing forth all kinds of beautiful plants by its Lord's permission.

3. The Immediate Fruits of His Da'wah: Forging Great Men

The response of people to Abu Bakr's call was swift and blessed, as if Allah had saved this honor for him as a reward for his sincerity and devotion. In the very first days of his call, a constellation of the best men of the Ummah embraced Islam at his hands, including five of the ten who were promised Paradise:

  • Uthman ibn Affan (عثمان بن عفان): The modest and generous one, possessor of the two lights, and the third Rightly-Guided Caliph.
  • Al-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam (الزبير بن العوام): The disciple of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his cousin.
  • Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf (عبد الرحمن بن عوف): The trustworthy merchant and one of the wealthiest companions who spent his wealth for the sake of Allah.
  • Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas (سعد بن أبي وقاص): The maternal uncle of the Prophet ﷺ, the first to shoot an arrow for the sake of Allah, and one whose prayers were answered.
  • Talhah ibn Ubaydillah (طلحة بن عبيد الله): Whom the Prophet ﷺ called "Talhah of Goodness," and who was one of the fiercest defenders of the Prophet ﷺ on the day of Uhud until his hand was paralyzed.

Contemplating these names makes us realize the magnitude of the achievement of Al-Siddiq. All the deeds of these great men, all their conquests, all their spending and giving, are poured into the scale of good deeds of Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq because he was the reason for their guidance. This embodies the saying of the Prophet ﷺ, as in Sahih Muslim:

"Whoever calls to guidance will have a reward similar to that of those who follow him, without that detracting from their reward in the slightest."

Evidence of His Status and Role

The status of Abu Bakr (RA) was not a matter of opinion but was affirmed by the texts of the revelation:

  • In the Holy Quran: Allah singled him out with the honor of companionship in the most difficult of situations, saying:
    {...when he said to his companion, "Do not grieve; indeed, Allah is with us."} [At-Tawbah: 40]
    Many commentators also believe that the verse
    {But the righteous one will avoid it - * [He] who gives his wealth to purify himself} [Al-Layl: 17-18]
    was revealed about Abu Bakr when he used to buy Muslim slaves from their disbelieving masters and free them, such as Bilal ibn Rabah (بلال بن رباح).
  • In the Prophetic Sunnah: The hadiths about his virtues are countless. Among them is the saying of the Prophet ﷺ:
    "If I were to take a Khalil (close friend) from my Ummah, I would have taken Abu Bakr, but it is the brotherhood of Islam and its affection." (Agreed upon)

The life of Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq in Da'wah teaches us that true faith does not stop at the boundaries of the heart but springs forth to become action, construction, and giving, and that the greatest means of calling to Allah is to be a righteous role model yourself, in whom people see the beauty of this religion.

   

Recap: Abu Bakr & the Fruits of Da'wah

  • Immediate Faith: Abu Bakr's unhesitating acceptance of Islam was a testament to the Prophet's ﷺ perfect character.
  • Character as Capital: His success in Da'wah was rooted in his beloved personality, social standing, and wide network of relationships.
  • Blessed Fruits: He was the direct cause for the conversion of five of the ten promised Paradise, showing the immense reward of guiding others.
  • Divine Affirmation: His status is confirmed in both the Quran and the Sunnah, highlighting his role as the Prophet's ﷺ companion and a great spender for Allah's cause.

Lessons from Abu Bakr's Da'wah: The Power of the Individual and a Methodology That Never Stops

The most remarkable aspect of Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq's launch into Da'wah is that it was not merely a historical event but the establishment of a practical methodology for every Muslim who wants to serve their religion. Al-Siddiq proved that sincere faith must transform into positive action and that a true caller does not wait for ideal conditions to begin.

1. The Power of Individual Da'wah: An Unstoppable Methodology 💪

At the beginning of the call, Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq worked with a methodology that was both simple and profound: individual Da'wah (one-to-one). He had no mosque to preach in, no pulpit, no media channel, and no thousands of followers on social media. All he possessed was his heart filled with faith, his truthful tongue, and his great moral capital. This teaches us fundamental lessons:

  • You Don't Need Special Resources: Individual Da'wah does not require material resources or official positions. Your weapon is your sincerity, your good character, and a kind word. You only need to be a righteous Muslim to be an effective caller in your circle. Al-Siddiq started with "zero resources" by material standards but reaped the greatest fruits.
  • No One Can Prevent It: Authorities can close mosques, ban public lectures, and block websites, but no power on earth can prevent you from speaking to your friend, your colleague at work, or your relative. It is a free call that is not subject to external restrictions. The greatest Quranic example of this is the call of Prophet Yusuf (Joseph - يوسف عليه السلام) in the most difficult of circumstances, where he called his two companions in prison to monotheism. He said:
    {O my two companions of prison, are separate lords better or Allah, the One, the Prevailing?} [Yusuf: 39]
    If Da'wah is possible in prison, it is possible everywhere.
  • Deeper Impact: Individual Da'wah often has a deeper impact than public speeches because it allows the caller to address the mind and heart directly, answer personal doubts, provide solutions tailored to the individual's situation, and build a relationship of trust and affection.

2. The Da'wah That Never Stops: Broader Than Pulpits and Mosques 🕌

Abu Bakr teaches us another lesson: the call to Allah is not confined to traditional means. While pulpits and mosques are important tools for public Da'wah, they are not the only ones. The mistake some fall into is waiting for these means to be available or stopping their work if they are restricted.

The Sheikh warned against callers being deceived by modern and mass media (like media and social networks) and neglecting individual work, which is the foundation. This work is the "strategic reserve" of Da'wah and the true incubator for sincere men and women. If all public means are cut off, this solid foundation remains, unaffected.

Every Muslim is a caller in their own way. Da'wah is not just words; it is:

  • A Call Through Character: Your trustworthiness in your trade, your honesty in your promises, your kindness to your parents, your goodness to your neighbors—all are a silent Da'wah that can be more eloquent than a thousand sermons. The Prophet ﷺ was a Quran walking on earth, and this is what we should strive for.
  • A Call Through Excellence: Your excellence in your work as a doctor, engineer, or laborer is a Da'wah in itself, reflecting the image of a serious and distinguished Muslim.
  • A Call Through Being a Role Model: For people to see Islam as a tangible reality in your daily interactions.

The Prophet ﷺ commanded every individual in the Ummah to convey according to their ability, saying in an agreed-upon hadith:

"Convey from me, even if it is one verse."
This is a command for every Muslim, not just scholars. He also explained the immense reward for guiding a single person, saying to Ali ibn Abi Talib (علي بن أبي طالب رضي الله عنه):
"By Allah, if Allah guides one man through you, it is better for you than having red camels." (Agreed upon)

The conclusion from Al-Siddiq's methodology is that true Da'wah is a continuous state, not a temporary job. It is an individual responsibility before being an institutional one, and its foundation is building trust through being a good role model before delivering sermons.

   

Recap: Lessons from Abu Bakr's Da'wah

  • Power of the Individual: One-to-one Da'wah is an unstoppable methodology that requires sincerity and good character, not special resources.
  • Unstoppable Da'wah: No authority can prevent individual Da'wah. It is the "strategic reserve" of the Ummah.
  • Da'wah is More Than Words: It is a call through one's character, excellence in one's work, and being a good role model.
  • A Universal Responsibility: Every Muslim is commanded to convey the message, even if it's a single verse.

The Prophetic Methodology of Change: From Building the Individual to Forging a Nation

The change that the Prophet ﷺ brought about in society was not sudden or random but a wise, divine methodology characterized by gradualism, patience, and deep-seated construction. This methodology remains the ideal path for anyone seeking reform in any time or place. The steps of this method can be summarized in four integrated stages:

Stage One: The General Call and Spreading Principles (Sowing the Seeds)

In this stage, the call was like sowing seeds in a vast field, where goodness is cast to everyone without distinction. This methodology was represented by:

  • Spreading the Religion to All: The Prophet ﷺ, Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq, and those who embraced Islam with them initially spoke to everyone they trusted and saw good in. The call was not limited to a specific group but knocked on every potential door. The goal was to spread the fundamental idea of monotheism and morals in society.
  • A Comprehensive Message: The message of the call at this stage was comprehensive, covering all the fundamentals of the religion:
    • Iman (Faith): Speaking about Allah, His oneness, His names and attributes, and the Last Day.
    • Islam (Submission): Introducing the basic acts of worship that connect the servant to their Lord.
    • Ihsan (Excellence): Focusing on purifying the soul, noble morals, and matters that soften the heart.

This stage is the "presentation" phase, where Islam is presented to all people. Through this general presentation, the first fruits begin to appear, and people are distinguished into those who respond and those who turn away.

Stage Two: Individual Da'wah and Focusing on the Elite (Nurturing the Sprout)

From among those who heard the general call, a group emerged whose hearts responded to the truth. Here, the second and most important stage begins: focusing on distinguished individuals. This is the stage of "crafting" and concentrated nurturing, characterized by:

  • Selection and Focus: The goal was not quantity but quality. The Prophet ﷺ focused on those who showed readiness and acceptance, giving them special attention. This focus ensures the building of a solid base that can be relied upon in the future.
  • Deep Construction: Individual Da'wah allows the caller (the nurturer) to build the individual in an integrated manner, instilling in them the correct creed, teaching them worship, purifying their character, and connecting them firmly to Allah.
  • Secrecy and Protection: This stage was necessarily secret to protect this nascent sprout from the tyranny of the era of ignorance (Jahiliyyah) and to give it enough time to grow and become strong.

This is the stage that resulted in the conversion of a constellation of figures like Abu Bakr, Uthman, Al-Zubayr, and other early pioneers. Each one of them was a project for an entire nation and required special focus and effort.

Stage Three: Collective Action and Forming the Nucleus (Forming the Trunk)

After a group of individuals had been strongly built, the next stage was to bring these individuals together into a single, cooperative entity. The work was no longer individual and isolated but became organized, collective action. The most prominent example of this stage is the "House of Al-Arqam ibn Abi Al-Arqam" (دار الأرقم بن أبي الأرقم).

  • Forming the Group: In the House of Al-Arqam, the Prophet ﷺ did not just teach each individual separately; he brought them together so they could get to know each other, their hearts could unite, and they could feel the meaning of brotherhood in Allah. This is the practical application of Allah's saying:
    {The believers are but brothers.} [Al-Hujurat: 10]
  • Cooperation in Righteousness and Piety: When this group gathers, the energies of the individuals begin to integrate. The wealthy one spends their wealth, the knowledgeable one teaches, and the strong one protects. This is a manifestation of Allah's saying: {And cooperate in righteousness and piety, but do not cooperate in sin and aggression.} [Al-Ma'idah: 2]
  • The School of Prophethood: The House of Al-Arqam was the first school in Islam, where the companions received the Quran fresh and pure directly from the mouth of the Prophet ﷺ, learned wisdom and character from him, and were trained in patience and steadfastness.

In this way, the group of scattered individuals was transformed into a solid nucleus, a cohesive group, and a single rank, as Allah later described them:

{Indeed, Allah loves those who fight in His cause in a row as though they are a [single] structure joined firmly.} [As-Saff: 4]

Stage Four: Public Influence and Empowerment (The Tree Bearing Fruit)

After this group grew strong and became capable of facing society, the final stage arrived: moving from secrecy to publicity and beginning to influence society on a wider scale.

  • Proclaiming the Call: The divine command
    {Then   _   declare what you are commanded and turn away from the polytheists.} [Al-Hijr: 94]
    was the signal to begin this stage. Islam was no longer just a religion practiced in secret but a public call challenging falsehood in its own heartland.
  • Influence by Example: The first Muslims, with what they learned in the school of prophethood, became living models of Islam. People saw their truthfulness, trustworthiness, and patience, and were influenced by them, entering the religion of Allah.
  • Empowerment: This stage is what paves the way for empowerment and victory. Because of this solid foundation, Islam was able to remain firm in the face of persecution in Makkah and then find fertile ground in Madinah to build its state. The efforts of Mus'ab ibn Umayr in Madinah, which were an application of the same methodology, prepared the society to receive the Prophet ﷺ and establish the first state in Islam.

This is the wise prophetic gradualism: starting with the individual, then forming the group, then influencing society, leading to the empowerment of Allah's religion on earth. It is a divine methodology that does not rush results but focuses on perfecting the foundation, because a strong foundation is what withstands the storms.

   

Recap: The Prophetic Methodology of Change

  1. Sowing Seeds: A general, comprehensive call to all.
  2. Nurturing the Sprout: Secret, individual Da'wah focusing on building a quality elite.
  3. Forming the Trunk: Collective action and brotherhood in a secret center (House of Al-Arqam) to form a solid nucleus.
  4. Bearing Fruit: Moving to public proclamation and societal influence, leading to empowerment.

The Early Muslims and the House of Al-Arqam: The Nucleus of the Ummah and the Fortress of Da'wah

With the dawn of Islam in Makkah, the call began to spread quietly and steadily, moving beyond the Prophet's ﷺ household and closest friends to reach pure hearts that were searching for the truth. This first constellation of Muslims, known as the "As-Sabiqun al-Awwalun" (the first and foremost), formed the solid foundation upon which the great edifice of Islam was built.

1. A Constellation of the First and Foremost

Allah Almighty praised these early pioneers with great praise and promised them the highest ranks, saying:

{And the first forerunners [in the faith] among the Muhajireen and the Ansar and those who followed them with good conduct - Allah is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him, and He has prepared for them gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein they will abide forever. That is the great attainment.} [At-Tawbah: 100]

This group represented a diverse cross-section of Makkan society, indicating the universality of the message and its ability to address all people:

  • From the Leaders and Nobles of Quraysh: Such as Uthman ibn Affan (عثمان بن عفان), Al-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam (الزبير بن العوام), Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf (عبد الرحمن بن عوف), Talhah ibn Ubaydillah (طلحة بن عبيد الله), Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas (سعد بن أبي وقاص) (all of whom are among the ten promised Paradise), Abu Ubaydah ibn al-Jarrah (أبو عبيدة بن الجراح) (the trustee of this Ummah), and Sa'id ibn Zayd (سعيد بن زيد).
  • From the Weak and the Freed Slaves: Who found their dignity and humanity in Islam, led by Bilal ibn Rabah (بلال بن رباح), Ammar ibn Yasir (عمار بن ياسر) and his parents Yasir and Sumayyah (first female martyr in Islam), Khabbab ibn al-Aratt (خباب بن الأرت), Suhayb al-Rumi (صهيب الرومي), and Abdullah ibn Mas'ud (عبد الله بن مسعود).
  • From the Virtuous Women: Such as Fatimah bint al-Khattab (فاطمة بنت الخطاب) (sister of Umar ibn al-Khattab and wife of Sa'id ibn Zayd), whose conversion played a role in her brother's later conversion, Asma bint Abi Bakr (أسماء بنت أبي بكر), and Umm Ayman (أم أيمن), the Prophet's ﷺ nursemaid.

2. The Need for a Secret Center: The Incident of Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas

As the number of Muslims grew, even though they were few, it became difficult to completely hide their rituals, especially the prayer, which requires gathering and a visible posture. Some companions used to go to the remote valleys and ravines of Makkah, away from sight, to pray together.

On one of these days, while Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas (سعد بن أبي وقاص رضي الله عنه) was with a group of companions praying in a ravine, a group of polytheists surprised them. The polytheists did not just see them; they began to mock them and criticize their prayer, which provoked the Muslims.

The situation escalated from mockery to a physical altercation. Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas took the jawbone of a camel and struck one of the polytheists with it, injuring his head and causing it to bleed. Thus, this was the first blood shed for the sake of Allah in Islam, and it was in defense of the right to worship Allah.

This incident served as an alarm bell. The Prophet ﷺ, with his profound wisdom, realized that a direct confrontation at this early stage could lead to the crushing of the call while it was still in its infancy. The material power was not balanced, and the ultimate goal was to nurture the first nucleus and deepen their faith, not to enter into losing confrontations. From here, the urgent need arose for a safe and highly secret place to serve as a center for the call and a school for education.

3. The House of Al-Arqam: A Brilliant Strategic Choice

The Prophet's ﷺ choice fell upon the house of Al-Arqam ibn Abi Al-Arqam (دار الأرقم بن أبي الأرقم) to be the first incubator for the call. This choice was not random but was a brilliant strategic decision for several reasons:

  • The House's Location: The house was near the hill of Safa, in a location relatively isolated from the homes of the Qurayshi leaders and their gathering centers around the Ka'bah, which reduced the likelihood of being discovered.
  • The Owner's Personality: Al-Arqam ibn Abi Al-Arqam was a young man (around sixteen years old). He was not one of the well-known senior companions, so eyes were not on his house. If Quraysh wanted to search for the Muslims' gathering place, they would have searched the houses of the elders like Abu Bakr, and it would never have occurred to them that the secret headquarters would be in the house of a young boy.
  • Al-Arqam's Tribal Affiliation: Al-Arqam was from the tribe of Banu Makhzum, the same tribe as the staunchest enemy of Islam at that time, Abu Jahl (أبو جهل). This affiliation provided unexpected security cover. If news of the meeting had leaked, the tribal loyalty of Banu Makhzum might have driven them to keep it quiet so that it would not be said that Muhammad ﷺ was using one of their homes as a center for his call, which would be a disgrace to them in the face of other tribes, especially Banu Hashim.

The Function of the House of Al-Arqam:
It was not just a shelter but the first educational institution in Islam, where the Prophet ﷺ did the following:

  • Teaching the Revelation: He would recite to the companions what was revealed to him of the Quran. They received it directly from him, and it penetrated their hearts and was reflected in their behavior.
  • Nurturing and Purification: He purified their souls and educated them on the meanings of faith, character, patience, and steadfastness.
  • Forging the Muslim Community: In this place, the master and the slave, the rich and the poor, the strong and the weak, gathered under one banner: "There is no god but Allah." The distinctions of the era of ignorance melted away, and the first nucleus of the Ummah based on brotherhood in Allah was formed, in fulfillment of Allah's saying: {The believers are but brothers.} [Al-Hujurat: 10]

Thus, thanks to this brilliant choice, the Prophet ﷺ was able, over three years, to build a unique generation, an elite group of people who were the cornerstone upon which the state of Islam was later built.

   

Recap: The First Muslim Community

  • The First Converts: A diverse group from all levels of society, proving the call's universality.
  • The Need for Secrecy: An early clash showed the danger of public worship, necessitating a secret center.
  • The House of Al-Arqam: A brilliant strategic choice for a headquarters due to its location and Al-Arqam's youth and tribal affiliation, which provided security.
  • The First Islamic School: It served as a center for teaching the Quran, nurturing souls, and forging the first cohesive Muslim community.

The Pillars of Education at the Dawn of Islam: The Methodology for Building the First Generation

The House of Al-Arqam was not merely a hideout; it was an integrated educational incubator and a comprehensive curriculum, supervised by the first teacher ﷺ. The education in this foundational phase focused on four integrated pillars, which served as the columns upon which the edifice of the unique generation of companions was built.

Pillar 1: Prayer (Salah) - The Spiritual Connection and Daily Fuel ⛽

Prayer was the first practical act of worship enjoined upon Muslims after monotheism. It is the pillar that represents the direct connection between the servant and their Lord and the primary source of spiritual strength. In this phase, the obligatory prayer consisted of:

  • The Night Prayer (Qiyam al-Layl): The night prayer was obligatory upon the Prophet ﷺ and his companions at the beginning of the mission, as mentioned in the opening of Surah Al-Muzzammil:
    {O you who wraps himself [in clothing], * Arise [to pray] the night, except for a little - * Half of it - or subtract from it a little * Or add to it, and recite the Qur'an with measured recitation.} [Al-Muzzammil: 1-4]
    The wisdom behind its obligation at the start was that this act of worship in the depth of the night builds sincerity in the heart, strengthens resolve, sharpens determination, and prepares the soul to bear the burdens of the call and its hardships during the day.
  • Two Prayers During the Day: There were also two obligatory prayers: two rak'ahs before sunrise and two rak'ahs before sunset. This is inferred from Allah's saying: {So be patient over what they say and exalt [Allah] with praise of your Lord before the rising of the sun and before its setting...} [Taha: 130]

This remained the obligatory prayer until the journey of Isra and Mi'raj, when the five daily prayers were prescribed, and the obligation of the night prayer was abrogated for the Ummah, though it remained for the Prophet ﷺ, with some scholarly disagreement. Starting with prayer teaches us that the foundation of change is to reform the relationship with Allah, for it is prayer that "prohibits immorality and wrongdoing."

Pillar 2: The Quran - The Intellectual Curriculum and Moral Constitution 📖

The Quran was the primary educational material in the school of the House of Al-Arqam. It was not just a text to be recited; it was:

  • The Foundation of Guidance: It was the light that dispelled the darkness of the pre-Islamic era from the minds and hearts of the companions, presenting them with a new vision of the universe, life, and humanity. Allah says:
    {Indeed, this Qur'an guides to that which is most suitable...} [Al-Isra: 9]
  • The Source of Change: The verses of the Quran were revealed to change their concepts, purify their morals, build their personalities, and transform them into different human beings with divine values.
  • The Provision of Steadfastness: The stories of previous prophets and Allah's promise of victory to the believers increased their firmness on the truth in the face of persecution. Allah says:
    {And each [story] We relate to you from the news of the messengers is that by which We make firm your heart.} [Hud: 120]

The companions received the Quran directly from the mouth of the Prophet ﷺ, living its meanings and events, which made it a part of their spiritual and intellectual makeup.

Pillar 3: Righteous Companionship (Suhbah) - The Nurturing and Supportive Environment 🤝

The Prophet ﷺ understood that a Muslim individual could not stand firm alone in a hostile society. Therefore, the House of Al-Arqam was a place to form the first cohesive Muslim community. Righteous companionship was the environment that:

  • Protected and Motivated: In it, a Muslim found brothers who shared the same goal, who would strengthen his resolve when he weakened, and encourage him towards good.
  • Forged True Brotherhood: In this place, class and ethnic distinctions dissolved, and everyone became brothers in Allah. Bilal the Abyssinian (بلال الحبشي) sat next to Abu Bakr the Qurayshi, with no superiority of one over the other except in piety.
  • Provided Psychological and Material Support: They would enjoin each other to truth and patience, the rich would console the poor, and the strong would support the weak.

This pillar is a practical application of Allah's command to His Prophet: {And keep yourself patient [by being] with those who call upon their Lord in the morning and the evening, seeking His countenance. And let not your eyes pass beyond them...} [Al-Kahf: 28], and an affirmation of the Prophet's ﷺ saying: "A man is upon the religion of his close friend, so let each of you look at whom he befriends." (Narrated by Abu Dawud and al-Tirmidhi)

Pillar 4: The Good Role Model (Qudwah) - The Practical Methodology and Living Application ⭐

This pillar is what unites all the other pillars. The Prophet ﷺ was not just a teacher delivering lessons; he was Islam embodied in a person. The companions learned more from him by seeing and observing than by hearing:

  • They saw how he prayed and learned humility.
  • They heard how he recited the Quran and learned contemplation.
  • They watched how he treated them and his enemies and learned character.
  • They felt his patience and steadfastness and drew strength from it.

The presence of the Prophet ﷺ among them was the guarantee of the correct application of the methodology. He was the absolute role model who could not be done without. Allah says: {There has certainly been for you in the Messenger of Allah an excellent pattern for anyone whose hope is in Allah and the Last Day and [who] remembers Allah often.} [Al-Ahzab: 21]

Practical Application: The Best Start on the Path of Commitment

These four pillars are not just history to be narrated; they are a practical methodology for every new person on the path of religious commitment. If a young man or woman wants to start on a correct and firm footing, they must focus on these fundamentals:

  • Fix Your Prayer: Make it your priority, perform it on time, and try to increase your voluntary prayers and night prayers, even if with just two rak'ahs.
  • Have a Daily Portion of the Quran: Do not abandon the Quran. Read every day, even if it is just one page, with contemplation and understanding.
  • Seek Righteous Company: Join a group of friends who will help you in obedience to Allah and distance you from His disobedience.
  • Take a Righteous Role Model: Follow a devout scholar or a sincere caller from whose knowledge and character you can learn.

Whoever focuses on these pillars will find that change occurs naturally in their life: the prayer will restrain them, the Quran will guide them, the company will encourage them, and the role model will inspire them.

   

Recap: The Four Pillars of Early Islamic Education

  1. Prayer (Salah): The spiritual connection and source of strength.
  2. The Quran: The intellectual curriculum and moral constitution.
  3. Righteous Companionship (Suhbah): The supportive environment for growth and protection.
  4. The Good Role Model (Qudwah): The Prophet ﷺ as the living application of the message.

Conclusion of the Secret Phase: Completion of the Nucleus and Preparation for Public Proclamation

After three years of secret Da'wah and focused nurturing in the House of Al-Arqam, the efforts of the Prophet ﷺ culminated in the formation of the solid nucleus of the Muslim community. This group was not just a collection of individuals who had believed; they were a generation built on firm foundations of creed, worship, brotherhood, and adherence. By the grace of Allah and then through the nurturing of His Messenger ﷺ, they became capable of shouldering the burdens of the next phase: the phase of confrontation and struggle.

The number of Muslims had reached a few dozen (narrations mention numbers ranging from forty to over a hundred), but their value was not in their number but in their quality. They were a chosen elite who had drunk Islam from its pure source, and their personalities were shaped under the watchful eye of the Prophet ﷺ. With this, the objective of the secret phase was complete, and the time had come for Islam to move from whispers in homes to an open declaration in the face of the world.

The Divine Command for a Partial Proclamation: "And warn your nearest kindred"

The divine command came to move the call to a new stage, a stage of partial public proclamation directed at the closest circle to the Prophet ﷺ: his clan of Banu Hashim. Allah Almighty said:

{And warn, [O Muhammad], your closest kindred. * And lower your wing to those who follow you of the believers. * And if they disobey you, then say, "Indeed, I am disassociated from what you are doing."} [Ash-Shu'ara: 214-216]
.

There was profound wisdom in this divine command:

  • Fulfilling the Responsibility: A person begins with their family, for they are the most deserving of their kindness and goodness, and calling them is the greatest form of goodness to them.
  • Testing Loyalty: This warning was a test for the family, to show who would stand with him and who would oppose him from among his closest relatives.
  • Seeking Protection: In a tribal society like Makkah, the protection of the clan was the first line of defense for an individual. If the Prophet ﷺ could secure the protection of his clan (even if they did not believe in him), it would provide a necessary cover to face the rest of the clans of Quraysh.

The First Attempt: Abu Lahab Spoils the Gathering

In compliance with his Lord's command, the Prophet ﷺ took the first step. He prepared a meal and invited the members of Banu Hashim and those with them from Banu al-Muttalib, who were about forty-five men. After they had eaten and drunk, the Prophet ﷺ wanted to speak and call them to Allah.

But his uncle, Abd al-Uzza ibn Abd al-Muttalib, known as Abu Lahab (أبو لهب), was watching the situation with vigilance. With his characteristic malice, he realized what his nephew intended to do and preemptively spoke to cut him off and spoil his call before it could even begin. Abu Lahab shouted at the attendees with words that mocked the Prophet ﷺ and scattered the group. The assembly dispersed before the Prophet ﷺ could present his message.

_

Here, the wisdom of the Prophet ﷺ was manifest. He did not cause a scene or enter into a futile argument. He saw that the atmosphere was no longer suitable and that the opportunity was lost, so he remained silent and waited for another time.

The Second Attempt: The Clear Declaration and Drawing the Lines of Confrontation

The Prophet ﷺ did not despair; he repeated the attempt. He invited them to a meal a second time, and this time, after they finished eating, he immediately addressed them before giving Abu Lahab a chance. He stood and delivered his eloquent and concise speech that defined the path ahead:

"Indeed, a guide does not lie to his people. By Allah, besides Whom there is no other god, I am the Messenger of Allah to you specifically and to all people generally. By Allah, you will surely die just as you sleep, and you will be resurrected just as you awaken, and you will be held accountable for what you do. And it is either Paradise forever or Hellfire forever."

The words were powerful and clear, and at that moment, the ranks within the single family were distinguished:

  • The Stance of Abu Lahab (أبو لهب) (The Enemy from Within): Once again, Abu Lahab was the first to respond with ugliness and hostility, saying: "May you perish for the rest of the day! Is this why you have gathered us?" (In other narrations, he said similar words on another occasion). He began to incite his uncles against him, saying: "Seize him before the Arabs unite against him, and we have to hand him over." In response, the words of Allah Almighty were revealed about him:
    {May the hands of Abu Lahab be ruined, and ruined is he. * His wealth will not avail him or that which he gained...} [Al-Masad: 1-2]
    , marking the first explicit declaration of war from the heavens against the enemies of the call.
  • The Stance of Abu Talib (أبو طالب) (The Protector and Supporter): Although Abu Talib did not embrace Islam, the honor of kinship and blood drove him to fulfill his role as the chief of Banu Hashim. He said to the Prophet ﷺ: "Proceed with what you have been commanded. By Allah, I will surely protect and defend you."

This stance from Abu Talib was the greatest political victory of this stage. The Prophet ﷺ had secured the tribal protection that would prevent Quraysh from killing him, which gave him the green light to move to the next and most dangerous stage: proclaiming the call to all the people of Makkah from atop the hill of Safa.

And so, the years of secret preparation and building came to an end, and the years of open confrontation, patience, and tribulation began.

Introduction: The Prophetic Biography (Seerah).. A Methodology for Life, Not Merely History

It is a mistake to think that the noble Prophetic Biography (Seerah) is merely a historical account of bygone events or stories told for entertainment and passing time. In its essence, it is far deeper than that; it is an integrated methodology for life, an inexhaustible, sweet-water river from which we still seek light, learn manners and morals, draw inspiration for our ideas, and strengthen our beliefs. Everything an individual and a society needs for renaissance and reform can be found within the folds of the life of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him.

This is not just the biography of a great man; it is the practical application of divine revelation on earth. Allah, the Exalted, did not command us merely to read, but to emulate and follow his example. He said in His Noble Book:

“There has certainly been for you in the Messenger of Allah an excellent pattern for anyone whose hope is in Allah and the Last Day and [who] remembers Allah often.” (Surah Al-Ahzab: 21)
. This "excellent pattern" (uswah hasanah) means that his life, in all its details, is the perfect model to be followed in every aspect of life.

Therefore, when the Mother of the Believers, Aisha (عائشة), may Allah be pleased with her, was asked about the character of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, she replied with comprehensive and definitive words: "His character was the Qur'an." He was, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, a walking Qur'an on earth. In his Seerah, we see a living interpretation of Allah's verses, from which we learn how to be patient in times of trial, how to be grateful in times of ease, how to show mercy even to enemies, and how to exercise wisdom in da'wah (calling to Islam) and politics.

The Seerah is the complete methodology that teaches us how to build ourselves from within before changing our reality on the outside. It teaches us how to plant the pure creed of monotheism (Tawhid) in our hearts and how to purify our souls with the sublime morals for which his Lord testified, saying:

“And indeed, you are of a great moral character.” (Surah Al-Qalam: 4)
. From it, we learn strategies for change, the jurisprudence of priorities, and how to deal with different types of people.

Studying the Seerah is not a journey into the past, but an extraction of hidden gems that illuminate our present and future. The idea is not merely to know the events, but to derive principles, draw lessons, and transform them into a practical methodology. This is what we will strive for in these lines, following in his footsteps, peace and blessings be upon him, to understand how the greatest change in human history began.

The Prophetic Methodology for Change: A Gradual and Well-Structured Roadmap

The call to Islam in its early days was not merely an emotional reaction or a random revolution. It was an organized movement of change proceeding according to a divine methodology with clear features, characterized by gradualism, wisdom, and consideration of reality. By tracing the steps of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, in Mecca, we can extract a complete roadmap composed of logical stages, each paving the way for the next.

Stage One: Planting the Seeds.. Public Proclamation and Individual Invitation

The first step was to spread the fundamental principles of the religion as widely as possible. In a closed-off, ignorant (Jahili) society, it was necessary to create an intellectual and spiritual shock by presenting the grand concepts: monotheism (Tawhid), belief in the resurrection, morals, and purification of the soul. This was done through two parallel channels:
