  1. Public Proclamation: Utilizing any opportunity or gathering to speak about the essence of the message, just as the prophets before him did, in accordance with the Almighty's saying:
    “Invite to the way of your Lord with wisdom and good instruction, and argue with them in a way that is best.” (Surah An-Nahl: 125)
    .
  2. Individual Invitation (Da'wah): In the face of restrictions and a hostile environment, this channel was the most effective. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, would approach individuals in whom he saw goodness and sound intellect, presenting the call to them personally. The greatest example of this is the conversion of As-Siddiq, Abu Bakr (أبو بكر), may Allah be pleased with him, who in turn immediately became a caller to Islam. A constellation of the best companions, such as Uthman ibn Affan (عثمان بن عفان), Az-Zubayr ibn Al-Awwam (الزبير بن العوام), and Talhah ibn Ubaydillah (طلحة بن عبيد الله), accepted Islam at his hands.

At this stage, the focus was not on a single aspect of the religion. Rather, all the meanings of Islam, Iman (faith), and Ihsan (excellence) were propagated: from creed and worship to morals and the deeds of the heart, to establish a comprehensive understanding of the religion.

Stage Two: Nurturing the Sapling.. Focusing on the Respondents

As responses began to emerge, the call moved to a new, more focused stage. While the general call continued, special attention was given to those who had accepted the message. They were not left to their own devices; instead, a process of "concentrated individual da'wah" began—a stage of building and formation. At this stage, distinguished and influential elements from among the respondents were selected and given greater attention, with the aim of creating the initial nucleus that would carry the burdens of the da'wah later on. This is the way of Allah in bringing about change. Allah Almighty describes the Messenger's mission with the believers, saying:

“reciting to them His verses and purifying them and teaching them the Book and wisdom.” (Surah Al-Jumu'ah: 2)
. This is a comprehensive process of nurturing that requires focus and follow-up.

Stage Three: Building the Pillars.. Forging a Solid Core in the House of Al-Arqam

This stage served as an intensive training camp for the elite selected from the previous stage. Any great cause needs men who are like pillars (awtad), firmly rooted in the ground, unshaken by tribulations. This stage was embodied in the House of Al-Arqam ibn Abi Al-Arqam (دار الأرقم بن أبي الأرقم), which was not just a secret hiding place, but the first university of faith in Islam. In this blessed place, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, would secretly meet with his companions, nurturing them with the meanings of the Qur'an, purifying their souls, and building their characters on the foundation of firm faith and absolute sacrifice. He was forging them into the foundation upon which the entire edifice of Islam would be built. These are the ones known as

“The first forerunners [in the faith] among the Muhajireen and the Ansar.” (Surah At-Tawbah: 100)
, who earned the honor of establishing the foundation and bore the initial hardships.

Stage Four: Constructing the Edifice.. Transitioning to Collective Action

After building the "pillars" and forming a solid, conscious, and cohesive core, it became possible to move to a new stage that could not be achieved through individual efforts. This is the stage of organized collective action, where this group begins to carry out tasks that require combined efforts and cooperation. It is the practical application of Allah's saying:

“And cooperate in righteousness and piety.” (Surah Al-Ma'idah: 2)
. Some reformist and transformative goals in any society cannot be accomplished by an individual, no matter his capabilities; they require a harmonious team that works like a solid structure, as described by the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him:
"The believer to the believer is like a building; each part strengthens the other" (Agreed upon)
.

Stage Five: Empowerment (At-Tamkeen).. A Divine Grant, Not Human Calculation

This stage comes as a culmination of the previous stages, but it is not subject to precise human calculations. When will comprehensive change occur? When will the people of truth be empowered? This is a matter known only to Allah, and it comes as a divine grant when the means are prepared and Allah wills it. The period of weakness may be long, or the opportunity may come suddenly from where it is least expected.

The best example of this is what happened with the Prophet's call in Mecca. While its people were rejecting and resisting, Allah prepared the way in Medina (المدينة المنورة). The Battle of Bu'ath (حرب بعاث) between the tribes of Aws (الأوس) and Khazraj (الخزرج) had led to the death of most of their senior leaders, leaving leadership in the hands of the youth, who were more receptive to the truth. A delegation from Medina came during the Hajj season and met the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, which led to the Pledge of Al-Aqabah that changed the course of history.

Empowerment is Allah's promise to the sincere believers:

“Allah has promised those who have believed among you and done righteous deeds that He will surely grant them succession [to authority] upon the earth.” (Surah An-Nur: 55)
. Our role is to strive with sincerity and dedication in the stages we control, and to leave the results to the Disposer of all affairs, the Almighty.

Recap: The Prophetic Methodology for Change

  • Stage 1 (Sowing Seeds): Spreading the core principles through public proclamation and individual invitation.
  • Stage 2 (Nurturing): Focusing on those who responded with concentrated individual nurturing.
  • Stage 3 (Building Pillars): Forging a solid, elite core through intensive training (House of Al-Arqam).
  • Stage 4 (Collective Action): Transitioning to organized group work to achieve larger goals.
  • Stage 5 (Empowerment): A divine grant that comes after fulfilling the prerequisites, not a result of human calculation.

The Limits of Responsibility in Da'wah: Your Effort is Your Success, Not Your Results

In the midst of the relentless pursuit of change and reform, a caller (da'i) may fall prey to one of the most dangerous diseases of the heart: linking the value of their work to visible, material results. Their enthusiasm might lead to frustration if they do not see a quick response, or their ego might lead them to measure success by the number of followers or the extent of their influence. Here, the Seerah and the Qur'anic methodology provide us with a precise and soul-reassuring balance that clearly defines the limits of our responsibility, freeing us from the burden of results that are not in our hands.

Your Responsibility Ends with Conveying the Message: Freeing the Heart from the Burden of Results

One of the heaviest burdens a caller can carry, and one of the most dangerous traps they can fall into, is linking the value of their effort and the fruit of their call to tangible results: the number of people guided, or the extent of visible change. This logic, though it may seem to stem from keenness and love for good, contradicts the essence of the divine methodology and opens the door wide to despair or arrogance. Here, the Qur'an and the Sunnah present us with a great truth that is not just a principle of da'wah, but a creed that liberates the heart and grants the soul unparalleled tranquility: You are responsible for conveying the message (Al-Balagh), and Allah alone is responsible for guidance (Al-Hidayah).

It is a clear divine division: you are responsible for the effort, and He is responsible for the outcome. You are responsible for the striving, and He is responsible for the result. Your responsibility is concentrated entirely in the first stage of da'wah: to convey the message clearly and to spread the word with the best wisdom and good instruction you can muster. As for what comes after—the opening of hearts, the response of souls, the formation of groups, and the achievement of empowerment—all of that is a pure grace and grant from Allah, which He bestows when and how He wills. It is not an inevitable fruit of your limited human effort.

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, who was the most eager of all creation for the guidance of humanity, was so overwhelmed with sorrow and grief at his people's rejection that he was on the verge of destroying himself. His Lord addressed him in the Book, not to blame him for his eagerness, but to remind him of the limits of his mission and to relieve him of this heavy burden:

“Then perhaps you would kill yourself through grief over them, [O Muhammad], if they do not believe in this message, out of sorrow.” (Surah Al-Kahf: 6)
. Then came the decisive guidance that sets the record straight:
“Not upon you, [O Muhammad], is their guidance, but Allah guides whom He wills.” (Surah Al-Baqarah: 272)
. And in another verse:
“Indeed, [O Muhammad], you do not guide whom you love, but Allah guides whom He wills.” (Surah Al-Qasas: 56)
. If this was addressed to the most beloved of creation to Allah, what then of those below him?

The role of the caller is like that of a sincere farmer. He plows the land (by preparing the hearts), sows the seed (by delivering the word), and waters it (with follow-up and reminders). But the moment the seed splits open, the plant grows, and the fruit appears is a pure divine miracle that no human possesses. Your task is to plant; the One who brings forth the crop and makes it bear fruit is Allah alone. This understanding frees the caller from the "tyranny of numbers" that has become a disease of our time and protects their heart from deadly despair.

Even if no one responds to you, even if you remain alone on your path, and even if you face rejection and aversion from every side, in the sight of Allah, you are among the most successful as long as you have done what was required of you. Your reward with Allah is guaranteed and complete simply for fulfilling the duty of conveying the message.

The greatest proof of this truth comes to us in a majestic scene from the Day of Judgment, narrated by the truthful and trusted one, peace and blessings be upon him, who said:

"The nations were displayed before me, and I saw a prophet with a small group (ruhayt), a prophet with one or two men, and a prophet with no one with him..." (Narrated by Al-Bukhari)
.

Stop and reflect! A prophet of Allah, the best and most chosen of His creation, will stand alone on the Day of Judgment, with no one from his nation having believed in him. Did this prophet fail? God forbid! If success were measured by worldly standards, this would be a catastrophic failure. But in the divine balance, this prophet is among the most successful because he fulfilled the duty assigned to him perfectly and was patient with denial and rejection until he met his Lord. Allah Almighty will not ask him, "How many followed you?" but will ask him, as mentioned in a narration, "Did you convey the message?"

This truth is the safety valve for the heart of every caller. It means that your true success is not in changing the world around you, but in remaining steadfast upon the truth within yourself. Your success is to meet Allah having fulfilled the trust, conveyed the message, and advised the nation, whether people responded or turned away. So continue on your path with a tranquil heart, plant goodness in every land, and do not ask about the time of harvest, for that knowledge is with your Lord.

“Your duty is only to convey the message, and upon Us is the account.” (Surah Ar-Ra'd: 40)
.

Success Measured by Effort, Not by Numbers

In an era dominated by materialism, where everything is measured by numbers and statistics, it is easy to fall into the trap of evaluating calls to Islam based on the "number of followers" or the "size of the audience." But the divine scale is entirely different. Allah does not look at the quantity of results, but at the quality of the work, the sincerity of the effort, and the burning desire in the heart for the guidance of creation.

Let us reflect on the story of the Prophet Nuh (نوح) (Noah), peace be upon him, who is considered one of the Messengers of strong will, the best of the prophets. He called his people for nine hundred and fifty years, as the Qur'an tells us:

“He said, ‘My Lord, indeed I have called my people night and day, but my call has not increased them except in flight.’” (Surah Nuh: 5-6)
. Despite this extraordinary effort, the result was:
“And none had believed with him, except a few.” (Surah Hud: 40)
.

In contrast, we find the Prophet Yunus (يونس) (Jonah), peace be upon him, who, when he called his people, they all believed. Their number is described in the Qur'an as:

“And We sent him to [a people of] a hundred thousand or more. And they believed, so We gave them enjoyment for a time.” (Surah As-Saffat: 147-148)
.

Does this mean that Yunus was more successful than Nuh? Of course not, for Nuh holds a higher station with Allah. The lesson is not in the number of believers, but in the magnitude of the effort, sacrifice, and patience exerted for the sake of the call. The effort that Nuh exerted with that small number was far greater and more arduous than the effort exerted with the hundred thousand.

This truth frees us from the shackles of numbers and makes us focus on what we truly possess: sincerity, diligent work, and persistence in the call despite the scarcity of means or the severity of restrictions. If the fuel of faith remains ignited in your heart, and you continue to strive and do your utmost, then you are successful in the sight of Allah, even if not a single person commits to the path at your hands. So continue on your way, and do not worry about the results, for you will be rewarded in any case.

Recap: The Limits of Responsibility in Da'wah

  • Role is Conveyance, Not Guidance: Your duty is to convey the message (Al-Balagh); guidance (Al-Hidayah) is from Allah alone.
  • Success is in the Effort: Allah measures success by the quality of effort and sincerity, not by the quantity of results or followers.
  • Freedom from the Burden of Results: This principle frees the caller from despair over a lack of response and from arrogance over visible success.
  • Prophetic Examples: Nuh's great effort yielded few followers, while a prophet may have none, yet they are successful in Allah's sight.

Proclaiming the Da'wah: From the Inner Circle to the Public Announcement

After three years of secret da'wah, which resulted in the formation of a solid base of faith, the divine command came to move the call to a new, more audacious and challenging phase: the phase of public proclamation and confrontation. This transition was not random; it too followed a wise divine strategy, starting with the most important and then moving outward, establishing the groundwork for a comprehensive confrontation with society.

Step One: Warning the Closest Kinsmen.. Proclaiming the Truth in the Innermost Circle

After the initial foundations of the call were laid in secret and a solid nucleus of faith had formed, the divine directive came to move the message from a hidden whisper to a careful and calculated public proclamation. The command was not for an immediate, absolute proclamation all at once, but followed a divine strategy that began from the center and spread to the periphery. The first command was clear and specific in the Almighty's words:

“And warn, [O Muhammad], your closest kindred. And lower your wing to those who follow you of the believers.” (Surah Ash-Shu'ara: 214-215)
.

This command carries a deep jurisprudential understanding of da'wah. The beginning is always with the closest circle. This is not merely an emotional priority but a strategic and logical necessity. One's relatives are the most deserving of one's goodness, and their reform is the foundation for the reform of society. Furthermore, calling them is a test of the caller's sincerity; for one who does not dare to confront his own family with the truth, how will he confront the world with it? And one who does not start with himself and his household, as the Prophet, peace be upon him, said, "Start with yourself, then with those you are responsible for," his call to others will lack impact.

The First Attempt: The Feast of Truth and Abu Lahab's Sabotage

In compliance with this command, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, did not simply gather them to deliver a dry sermon. Instead, he prepared a meal for them and invited them to a feast. They were about forty-five men from the clan of Banu Hashim. This was a reflection of his perfect character and wisdom, as he sought to prepare their souls and open their hearts before addressing their minds.

However, in this gathering, there was one who lay in wait to harm the call: his uncle, Abd al-Uzza ibn Abd al-Muttalib, nicknamed Abu Lahab (أبو لهب). The Prophet had barely begun to speak after they had eaten and drunk when Abu Lahab preempted everyone with a decisive and poisonous word, cutting off his nephew. He said, "These are your uncles and cousins, so speak, but abandon your youthful follies, and know that your people do not have the strength to face all the Arabs..." He erected a fence of fear, portraying the call as a cause for a total war with all the Arabs that they could not withstand, and then concluded by discouraging them.

Here, the great wisdom of the Prophet, peace be upon him, was manifest. He realized with his insight that the atmosphere had been poisoned and that any word spoken now would be lost in the midst of the argument and preconceived rejection stirred up by Abu Lahab. He did not enter into a futile debate, nor did he try to impose his word in a charged atmosphere that would bear no fruit. Instead, he chose silence. He remained quiet and ended the meeting peacefully, deciding to postpone the confrontation for a better opportunity.

This teaches us a profound lesson in the art of da'wah: the point is not just to deliver the word, but to deliver it at the right time and in the right circumstances to achieve the maximum possible impact. Wisdom sometimes requires silence and postponement, waiting for a moment when the hearts are more prepared to receive. Assessing the "receptivity" of the place, time, and audience is at the core of a caller's jurisprudence.

The Second Attempt: The Decisive Word and Divergent Stances

The first failure did not deter the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him; rather, it increased his resolve. He invited them to a meal once again, and after they had eaten, he immediately addressed them before giving Abu Lahab another chance to spoil the moment. He stood before them and delivered his historic speech, laying down the foundations of his call:

He began by saying, "The scout does not lie to his people," a phrase with deep meaning in Arab culture. The scout (ra'id) is the vanguard of the tribe who explores for pastures and water, and the tribe's entire life depends on his truthfulness. It was as if he was saying, "You know me and trust me in your worldly affairs, so how can you not trust me when I inform you of what will save you in your afterlife?"

Then he proclaimed the truth, complete and concise: "By Allah, besides Whom there is no other god, I am the Messenger of Allah to you in particular and to all people in general. By Allah, you will die just as you sleep, and you will be resurrected just as you awaken. You will be held accountable for what you do, and it will be either Paradise forever or Hellfire forever."

The words were like lightning—clear, decisive, and unambiguous. Here, the stances within the family diverged sharply:

  • The Stance of Abu Talib (أبو طالب) (Tribal Protection): Abu Talib stood up, moved by kinship and tribal loyalty, and said his supportive words: "How we love to assist you and accept your advice... Proceed with what you have been commanded. By Allah, I will continue to protect and defend you." But he followed it with a statement that revealed the power of tradition: "However, my soul cannot bring itself to abandon the religion of Abd al-Muttalib." Abu Talib offered the Prophet a protective shield, but he did not offer him his heart.
  • The Stance of Abu Lahab (أبو لهب) (Open Hostility): Abu Lahab stood up directly against him and uttered his ugly words, immortalized by history: "May you perish for the rest of this day! Is this why you gathered us?" He had declared his open and complete enmity. The divine response was immediate and decisive. An entire surah was revealed, to be recited until the Day of Judgment, not only exposing his stance but condemning him and his wife to ruin:
    “May the hands of Abu Lahab be ruined, and ruined is he. His wealth will not avail him or that which he earned...” (Surah Al-Masad)
    .

Thus, this meeting ended with all stances revealed, the message conveyed, and the proof established upon the closest of kin. The path was now paved for launching the call to the wider circle: all of Quraysh.

Recap: Proclaiming the Da'wah to the Inner Circle

  • Divine Command: Began with the directive to "warn your closest kindred."
  • Strategic Wisdom: Starting with family tests sincerity and seeks tribal protection.
  • First Attempt: A meal was prepared, but Abu Lahab sabotaged the meeting, teaching the wisdom of choosing the right moment.
  • Second Attempt: A decisive speech led to clear divisions: Abu Talib offered protection based on kinship, while Abu Lahab declared open hostility.

The Public Proclamation on Mount Safa: Establishing the Conclusive Proof

After the proof had been established upon the closest circle, and after the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, had secured the relative protection of his uncle Abu Talib, the time came to take the call to its widest extent yet. The matter was no longer confined to individuals or the clan; the decisive divine command came to bring the conflict into the open and place the message in confrontation with the entire society:

“Then declare what you are commanded and turn away from the polytheists. Indeed, We are sufficient for you against the mockers.” (Surah Al-Hijr: 94-95)
. The word "declare" (fasda') carries the force of shattering and splitting, as if it were a command to break the wall of silence and smash the idols of outdated traditions on the heads of their followers.

Choosing the Stage and the Resounding Call

The choice of place, time, and method was not random. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, ascended Mount Safa (جبل الصفا), a famous rocky hill overlooking the Kaaba, representing a natural gathering point and an open stage from which everyone could see and hear. Then he let out a cry that the Arabs knew well, shouting at the top of his voice: "Ya Sabahah!" (يا صباحاه!).

This was no ordinary call. It was the equivalent of an alarm siren used to warn of an imminent danger, like an enemy raid at dawn. This cry alone was enough to stir panic and curiosity in the hearts of the people of Mecca. People left their work and markets and rushed to see what was happening. Those who could not come sent representatives to find out the news.

After they had gathered, he did not begin with the call directly. Instead, he began calling them by their names and clans, to make every individual and every group feel personally addressed: "O Banu Fihr, O Banu 'Adi, O Banu Ka'b ibn Lu'ay, O Banu 'Abd Manaf, O Banu 'Abd al-Muttalib..." He addressed them from the eldest ancestor down to his closest clan, leaving no one with room to say, "This does not concern me."

The Logical Proof: The Opponent's Acknowledgment of the Caller's Truthfulness

Here, at the height of anticipation, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, used a brilliant rhetorical technique. He did not present his message; he made them endorse it first. He did not say, "I am a prophet, so believe me." Instead, he posed a hypothetical question based on their lived experience, a question resting on the immense moral capital he had built among them over forty years:

"If I were to tell you that horsemen were in the valley, intending to raid you, would you believe me?"

It was a simple question on the surface, but profound in its purpose. It presented them with two choices: either they believe him based on what they knew of him, or they disbelieve him and contradict forty years of experience. Their collective testimony, from which no one dissented, came like a judicial verdict they issued against themselves: "Yes, we have never known you to be anything but truthful." In another narration: "We have never experienced a lie from you."

With this answer, all their logical weapons were disarmed before the battle even began. They had admitted with their own tongues that he was "The Truthful, The Trustworthy" (As-Sadiq Al-Ameen), and that his word was, to them, a certainty beyond doubt. He had closed off all future avenues of denial. Herein lies the greatness of the Qur'anic argument that was later revealed to affirm the same logic:

“I have lived among you a lifetime before it. Then will you not reason?” (Surah Yunus: 16)
. Meaning, my entire life among you is the greatest proof of my truthfulness.

Proclaiming the Message and Establishing Individual Responsibility

After extracting this decisive acknowledgment from them, he moved to the core of the message, linking their belief in him in worldly matters to believing him in the matter of the Hereafter, which is far greater: "Then I am a warner to you of a severe punishment to come." Meaning, I, the truthful and trustworthy one whom you trust, am warning you of a danger greater than an army raiding you—the danger of Hellfire and painful torment.

Then, in a historic, defining moment, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, demolished the most important pillar of pre-Islamic tribal society: the principle of tribal solidarity and protection by lineage. He made it clear that salvation on the Day of Judgment is not through one's clan or lineage, but through individual deeds and personal faith. He called out to his closest relatives, one by one, to declare this principle publicly:

"O Banu 'Abd al-Muttalib, save yourselves from the Fire... O 'Abbas ibn 'Abd al-Muttalib (عباس بن عبد المطلب), I can avail you nothing from Allah... O Safiyyah (صفية), aunt of the Messenger of Allah, I can avail you nothing from Allah... And O Fatimah (فاطمة), daughter of Muhammad, ask me for whatever you wish of my wealth, but I can avail you nothing from Allah."

These words were a declaration of the birth of a new society, founded on the basis of piety and individual responsibility, not on blood and lineage.

The stand on Mount Safa was the "founding statement" of the Islamic state, intellectually and ideologically. It was an official announcement that the stage of secrecy was over and that the all-out confrontation between faith and disbelief had begun. The proof had been established, the excuse was removed, and there was no longer room for hesitation. From that moment, the struggle that would change the face of history forever began.

Recap: The Proclamation on Mount Safa

  • Strategic Setting: Chose Mount Safa, a public stage, and used the "Ya Sabahah!" alarm call to gather all of society.
  • Logical Proof: Disarmed his opponents by first making them testify to his lifelong truthfulness and trustworthiness.
  • Core Message: Warned them of the Hereafter, a danger greater than any worldly raid.
  • New Principle: Demolished tribalism by establishing individual responsibility for salvation, declaring that lineage offers no protection from Allah.

Methods of Opposition and Confrontation: The Unchanging Patterns in the War Between Truth and Falsehood

When truth proclaims its voice, falsehood never remains silent. It launches a comprehensive and organized war, whose methods do not differ with time or place. These are fixed divine patterns (sunan) in the conflict between faith and disbelief, and they manifested in their clearest form in the confrontation of Quraysh with the call of the Prophet Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him. Their war was not random; it proceeded through escalating stages, each more severe than the last, as if it were an instruction manual used by every tyrant and every system hostile to the truth throughout the ages.

Stage One: Containment and Temptation.. An Attempt to Buy the Principle

When falsehood first encounters the voice of truth, its initial weapon is often the softest to the touch and the most cunning. It is the weapon of containment and bargaining. This approach stems from the short-sightedness of the people of falsehood, who measure the motives of others by their own purely materialistic standards. They cannot conceive that a person might be driven by a goal loftier than money, a higher position, or an immediate desire. They assume that every call for reform is but a mask for personal ambitions and that every principle has a price that can be paid.

Therefore, their first step was to try to "buy" the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, by offering him a "deal" that would secure his silence and grant him what they thought he sought of worldly possessions.

The Story of 'Utbah ibn Rabi'ah (عتبة بن ربيعة): The Offer of this World vs. the Revelation from the Heavens

The story of 'Utbah ibn Rabi'ah, a chief of Banu Abd Shams and one of the sagest and most cunning men of the Arabs, is the most prominent example of this stage. Quraysh did not send a rash man like Abu Jahl (أبو جهل), but chose a man known for his composure, wisdom, and negotiating skills, indicating that they took the matter seriously. One day, 'Utbah was sitting in his people's assembly while the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, was sitting alone in the mosque. 'Utbah said, "O people of Quraysh, shall I not go to Muhammad, speak to him, and offer him some proposals? Perhaps he will accept one of them, and we will give him whatever he wants, and he will leave us alone?" They said, "Yes, O Abu al-Walid, go and speak to him."

'Utbah went and sat next to the Prophet, peace be upon him, and began his conversation in a friendly tone aimed at softening his heart and opening a dialogue. He did not attack or accuse, but addressed him with a title of kinship: "O son of my brother, you are, as you know, of a noble status in the clan and a high place in lineage. You have brought a grave matter to your people, by which you have divided their community, belittled their intelligence, faulted their gods and their religion, and declared disbelievers of their forefathers. So listen to me, and I will present some matters for you to consider; perhaps you will accept some of them."

Then he began to detail the comprehensive package of temptations that he thought was irresistible:

  • The Offer of Absolute Wealth: "O son of my brother, if what you seek with this matter you have brought is wealth, we will collect enough of our wealth for you until you become the wealthiest of us all."
  • The Offer of Absolute Leadership: "If you seek honor, we will make you our chief, so that no matter is decided without you."
  • The Offer of Absolute Kingship: "If you seek a kingdom, we will make you our king."
  • The Offer of "Treatment" (a veiled insult): "And if this which comes to you is a spirit (ra'i, possession by a jinn) that you cannot repel from yourself, we will seek medicine for you and spend our wealth on it until we cure you."

These offers represented the pinnacle of what any human soul in that society could dream of. But they were facing a heavenly soul, a heart connected to the Creator of the universe. Here, the greatness of the Prophet, peace be upon him, shone through. He did not interrupt him, nor did he become enraged for his dignity when they hinted at madness. He listened in calm and serenity, letting him empty his bag of worldly offers. When he finished, he asked with refined manners and absolute confidence, "Are you finished, O Abu al-Walid?" He said, "Yes." He said, "Then listen to me."

The Prophet did not respond to his offers with counter-offers, nor did he enter into a debate about wealth or kingship. He transported him from the world of the earth to the world of the heavens, from human logic to divine revelation. He began reciting to him verses from the beginning of Surah Fussilat:

“Ha, Meem. A revelation from the Entirely Merciful, the Especially Merciful. A Book whose verses have been detailed—an Arabic Qur'an for a people who know...”

'Utbah, a master of eloquence, was hearing words the like of which he had never heard. Words that captivated the mind, described the creation of the heavens and the earth, narrated the fate of bygone nations, and spoke of resurrection and recompense. The Prophet continued his recitation while 'Utbah listened, resting his hands behind his back, until he reached the verse:

“But if they turn away, then say, ‘I have warned you of a thunderbolt like the thunderbolt [that struck] 'Aad and Thamud.’”
At this point, 'Utbah could not control himself. He stood up in panic, placed his hand over the Prophet's mouth, and implored him by the ties of kinship to stop, fearing that the thunderbolt would strike them right where they stood.

'Utbah returned to his people, and they saw the change in his face before he even spoke. They asked, "What is the news, O Abu al-Walid?" He replied with his immortal words: "The news is that I have heard a speech, by Allah, the like of which I have never heard. By Allah, it is not poetry, nor magic, nor soothsaying. O people of Quraysh, obey me and make this my responsibility. Leave this man and what he is engaged in; leave him alone. By Allah, the words I have heard will have a great outcome..."

The attempt at containment failed miserably. In fact, the magic turned against the magician, and their own envoy began to call on them to leave the Prophet alone, acknowledging the power of his message.

A Recurring Divine Pattern

This attempt was not unique. It was repeated in different forms. In another attempt, the chiefs of Quraysh gathered and made him a similar offer. When he refused, they said, "If you will not accept anything we have offered you, then you know that there are no people with a narrower land, less water, or harsher life than us. So ask your Lord, who sent you... to move these mountains from us... and to cause rivers to flow for us like the rivers of Syria and Iraq..."

His response was always one of absolute steadfastness on his principles, as manifested in his words to his uncle Abu Talib when he came to negotiate on behalf of Quraysh: "O uncle, by Allah, if they were to place the sun in my right hand and the moon in my left on the condition that I abandon this matter, I would not abandon it until Allah makes it victorious or I perish in the process."

This pattern of trying to contain the truth with worldly temptations is the same one faced by the prophets before him. Pharaoh tried to blackmail Moses (Musa, موسى), peace be upon him, emotionally by reminding him of his favor:

“Did we not raise you among us as a child?” (Surah Ash-Shu'ara: 18)
. The people of Shu'ayb (شعيب) said to him:
“We will surely drive you out, O Shu'ayb, and those who have believed with you from our city, or you must return to our religion.” (Surah Al-A'raf: 88)
. The bargain was either expulsion or abandoning their principles.

But the answer of the people of truth is always the same: what we have from Allah is better and more lasting, and our principles are not for sale or compromise. It is this steadfastness that forces falsehood to reveal its ugly face and move on to the next, more violent and overt stages of confrontation.

Stage Two: Demands for the Impossible and Sowing Doubt.. A War to Demonstrate Powerlessness

When the policy of temptation and compromise failed, and all offers of this world shattered against the mountain of prophetic steadfastness, Quraysh realized they were facing a man who could not be bought. Here, they moved to a new, more malicious stage: "intellectual intimidation" or a war of psychological attrition. If we cannot buy him, let us try to break him and show him to be powerless before his followers and the Arabs. The conflict shifted from trying to tempt him to trying to embarrass him, from bargaining to stubborn defiance, by demanding impossible material miracles. The purpose was not to seek certainty, but absolute incapacitation.

The List of Impossible Demands: Whims, Not Requests for Guidance

The Qur'an recorded these impossible demands with meticulous accuracy, exposing their superficial, materialistic thinking and their boundless obstinacy. They gathered around the Kaaba or came to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, and said, as the Qur'an narrates:

“And they say, ‘We will not believe you until you...” (Surah Al-Isra: 90)
. They set their conditions for belief, conditions that revealed their argumentative bankruptcy:

  1. Demands to Change the Earth's Nature: “...cause a spring to gush forth for us from the earth.” They wanted to change the arid nature of Mecca and turn it into a fertile land with flowing springs.
  2. Demands for Extraordinary Personal Wealth: “Or [until] you have a garden of palm trees and grapes and make rivers gush forth within them in abundance.” They said: If you are a prophet, why are you not the wealthiest of men, possessing gardens and orchards?
  3. Terrifying Cosmic Demands: “Or you cause the sky to fall upon us in fragments as you have claimed.” They were defiantly challenging him to bring about the promised punishment immediately.
  4. Demands to See the Unseen: “Or you bring Allah and the angels before [us] face to face.” They demanded to see Allah and the angels with their own eyes, a request that contradicts the very nature of faith in the unseen.
  5. Demands to Ascend to Heaven: “Or you have a house of gold or you ascend into the sky. And [even then], we will not believe in your ascension until you bring down to us a book we can read.” Even if he were to ascend to the sky before their very eyes, they attached another condition: that he return with a physical book they could read.

In another incident narrated by Imam Ahmad, the chiefs of Quraysh sat with the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, and said to him, "O Muhammad, ask your Lord to turn Mount Safa into gold for us, and we will believe in you." He said, "And you will do so?" They said, "Yes." So he prayed to his Lord, and Gabriel (Jibreel, جبريل), peace be upon him, came to him and said, "Your Lord conveys His greetings to you and says: If you wish, I will turn Safa into gold for them, but whoever among them disbelieves after that, I will punish him with a torment I have not afflicted upon anyone else in the worlds. And if you wish, I will open for them the door of repentance and mercy." The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, chose the door of repentance and mercy, saying, "Rather, the door of repentance and mercy."

The Divine Wisdom in Rejecting Whims

Some may ask: Why did Allah not respond to these demands to establish the proof against them? The answer lies in Allah's profound wisdom and His unchanging laws (sunan):

  1. The Law of Annihilation after Suggested Miracles: The Qur'an clearly explains this law. When a people suggest a specific sign and then disbelieve after it comes to them, the punishment is inevitable and immediate: complete destruction. Allah says:
    “And nothing has prevented Us from sending signs except that the former peoples denied them. And We gave Thamud the she-camel as a clear sign, but they wronged her.” (Surah Al-Isra: 59)
    . The people of Thamud requested a she-camel to emerge from a rock. It came as they requested, but they hamstrung her. The result was that Allah destroyed them to the last man. Thus, Allah's non-response to the demands of Quraysh was a mercy upon them, giving them respite so they might repent.
  2. The Greatest Miracle is the Qur'an: Allah revealed to this nation a miracle befitting the eternal and universal nature of its message: the Holy Qur'an. It is an intellectual, linguistic, and rhetorical miracle, enduring until the Day of Judgment, addressing minds and hearts. It is not a sensory miracle that ends once it is witnessed. Allah rebuked them for their heedlessness of this great sign in their hands, saying:
    “And is it not sufficient for them that We revealed to you the Book which is recited to them? Indeed in that is a mercy and a reminder for a people who believe.” (Surah Al-Ankabut: 51)
    . A sincere seeker of truth will find the Qur'an sufficient, but as for the one who follows his whims and is obstinate, all the signs in the universe will not suffice him.

The Prophetic Response: Defining the Mission and Glorifying the Lord

The divine directive to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, was to give a decisive and logical response that would put matters in their proper perspective. He did not argue with them about the details of their demands but answered with a reply that demolished their logic from its foundation:

“Say, ‘Exalted is my Lord! Was I ever but a man, a messenger?’” (Surah Al-Isra: 93)
.

This response carries two great meanings:

  • Glorifying the Lord (Subhana Rabbi): These cosmic miracles are the affair of Allah alone, not a toy in my hands to request whenever and however I wish. It is a glorification of Allah, exalting Him above being subject to human whims and suggestions.
  • Defining the Messenger's Mission (Was I ever but a man, a messenger?): I am not a god or a supernatural being; I am a human like you, and my mission is specific: I am a messenger conveying a message from Allah. My job is to proclaim, not to manufacture miracles on demand.

With this response, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, drew a clear line between the nature of true prophethood and the magic and sorcery that rely on marvels to dazzle people. He declared that the foundation of his call was proof, evidence, and good words, not acrobatic tricks. Thus, this stage also failed to break his will or tarnish his call; instead, it exposed their intellectual bankruptcy and blind obstinacy.

Stage Three: The Media War.. Ridicule, Defamation, and Rumors

When the offers of this world crashed against the rock of steadfastness, and the demands for the impossible were shattered by the logic of revelation, falsehood realized it was fighting a losing battle on the grounds of argument and reason. Here, it resorted to its filthiest and most widespread weapon, one with the greatest effect on the common people: the media and psychological war. This is the stage of character assassination, destruction of credibility, and tainting the idea before it reaches minds and hearts. This comprehensive campaign aimed to create a thick psychological barrier that would prevent people from even considering listening to the voice of truth. The Qurayshi campaign relied on three integrated and devilish axes.

1. Ridicule and Mockery: The Weapon to Shatter Prestige

Their first and simplest weapon was ridicule. Mockery requires no logic or evidence; it relies on winks, jibes, and laughter. It is a particularly effective weapon with the masses because it turns a serious issue into a subject of derision and strips its proponent of his prestige. The Qur'an recorded this practice with painful accuracy:

“Indeed, those who committed crimes used to laugh at those who believed. And when they passed by them, they would exchange derisive glances. And when they returned to their people, they would return jesting.” (Surah Al-Mutaffifin: 29-31)
.

The leaders of disbelief, like Abu Jahl, Al-Walid ibn Al-Mughirah (الوليد بن المغيرة), and Al-'As ibn Wa'il (العاص بن وائل), would stand on the roads. When they saw the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, and the poor Muslims with him, they would burst into mocking laughter, wink at each other, and then return to their gatherings to recount what they saw as a source of humor and entertainment.

The ridicule was not limited to laughter; it extended to insulting nicknames. They called the Prophet "Mudhammam" (the reviled one) instead of "Muhammad" (the praised one) to reverse the meaning. They would mimic his prayers and supplications. Imagine the impact of this daily psychological abuse on the noblest of creation, who had been known among them as the Truthful, the Trustworthy. His noble chest would tighten with this harm, until Allah revealed verses to comfort and strengthen him:

“And We already know that your breast is constrained by what they say. So exalt [Allah] with praise of your Lord and be of those who prostrate [to Him].” (Surah Al-Hijr: 97-98)
. The divine remedy for this harm was to seek refuge in Allah through glorification and prayer.

2. Organized Propaganda: Creating Labels and Unifying the Accusation

Quraysh realized that random mockery was not enough. The Hajj season was approaching, when delegations of Arabs would come from everywhere, and an organized media campaign was needed to "inoculate" these newcomers against Muhammad's message. They gathered in the Dar an-Nadwa (their parliament), headed by their media expert, Al-Walid ibn Al-Mughirah, to agree on a single accusation to level against him, so they would not contradict themselves before the Arabs and weaken their position.

Al-Walid began to review the options:

  • Shall we say he is a soothsayer (kahin)? They said: No, by Allah, he is not. We have seen soothsayers, and his speech is not their mumbling or rhyming prose.
  • Shall we say he is mad (majnun)? They said: He is not mad. We have seen madness and know it; his is not its choking, confusion, or whispering.
  • Shall we say he is a poet (sha'ir)? They said: He is not a poet. We know all forms of poetry—its different meters—and his speech is not poetry.

Here began Al-Walid's intellectual struggle, which the Qur'an depicted with cinematic precision:

“Indeed, he thought and deliberated. So may he be destroyed [for] how he deliberated. Then may he be destroyed [for] how he deliberated. Then he looked. Then he frowned and scowled. Then he turned back and was arrogant.” (Surah Al-Muddathir: 18-23)
. It is the scene of a man in a predicament, who knows the truth in his heart but is searching for any convincing lie to protect his status and his people's interests.

After this arduous mental labor, he could find only one charge, which, though it did not apply to him, was the most effective in repelling people:

“And said, ‘This is not but magic imitated [from others].’”
Why magic (sihr)? Because the accusation of magic carries an implicit warning and a direct threat: "Beware of approaching him, for he will separate a man from his wife, a son from his father, and a man from his clan." It is the accusation that creates a barrier of fear, preventing people from even listening and trying for themselves.

Indeed, the leaders of Quraysh, led by Abu Lahab, set out. They would sit on the roads leading to Mecca, and whenever they saw an approaching delegation, they would warn them, saying, "Beware of the boy of Banu Hashim, for he is a sorcerer who divides families."

3. Spreading Doubts and Lies: Tainting the Message and its Source

They did not stop at defaming the Messenger's character; they moved on to attacking the message itself and its source. They began to spread doubts and lies aimed at shaking confidence in the Qur'an:

  • The Doubt of a Human Source: They claimed that this Qur'an was not a revelation from heaven but was authored by Muhammad himself, or that someone was helping him. They circulated the rumor that it was
    “legends of the former peoples which he has written down, and they are dictated to him morning and afternoon.” (Surah Al-Furqan: 5)
    . Their lies went so far as to name a specific person, a Christian Roman blacksmith in Mecca named Jabr (جبر), whom they claimed was teaching him. The Qur'anic response was irrefutable:
    “And We certainly know that they say, ‘It is only a human being who teaches him.’ The tongue of the one they refer to is foreign, and this is a clear Arabic tongue.” (Surah An-Nahl: 103)
    . How could a man who could barely speak Arabic produce this Qur'an, which is a miracle of eloquence and clarity?
  • The Doubt of Plagiarism: They claimed he was borrowing his stories from the books of old, ignoring the fact that the Prophet, peace be upon him, was unlettered (ummi), unable to read or write, and had never sat with scholars or monks.
  • Direct Interference: When they saw that the magic of the Qur'an was captivating the hearts of those who listened, they resorted to a policy of deliberate disruption. Whenever they saw the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, reciting the Qur'an, they would raise their voices with singing, poetry, and idle talk to prevent his voice from reaching people's ears, implementing the command of their leaders:
    “And those who disbelieve say, ‘Do not listen to this Qur'an and speak noisily during [the recitation of] it that perhaps you will overcome.’” (Surah Fussilat: 26)
    .

Thus, this stage was a comprehensive media war, in which they used every tool of distortion and disinformation in a desperate attempt to extinguish the light of Allah. But Allah was watching them, and He revealed the Qur'an to expose their plans, refute their doubts, and strengthen the heart of His Prophet and the believers.

Stage Four: Physical Violence and Brutality.. The Final Language of the Powerless

When all the masks fall off, and all the soft weapons fail—from temptation and compromise, to impossible demands and defiance, to ridicule and defamation—falsehood is left with nothing but its true, ugly face and the only language it masters: physical violence. This is the stage of intellectual and moral bankruptcy, the language of the powerless who have lost all argument and logic and have nothing left but brute force to silence the voice that exposes and threatens their existence.

Quraysh knew that directly harming the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, or senior companions from the nobility of Quraysh like Abu Bakr As-Siddiq, would mean igniting a civil war and breaking tribal customs, which was unforgivable. The Prophet was under the protection of his uncle Abu Talib and the clan of Banu Hashim. Therefore, they directed all their hatred and brutality toward the group they thought had no one to protect them, no clan to defend them: the oppressed believers, including slaves, allies, and the poor. By torturing them, they aimed to achieve two goals: to break the will of these individuals and force them to apostatize, and to make them an example to terrorize anyone else who thought of entering this new religion.

Here, in the scorching alleys of Mecca and under its blazing sun, the most magnificent pages of patience and steadfastness in human history were written with the tears and blood of these heroes.

Bilal ibn Rabah (بلال بن رباح): The Anthem of Monotheism Under the Lashes of Torture

Bilal was an Abyssinian slave belonging to one of the worst tyrants of Quraysh, Umayyah ibn Khalaf (أمية بن خلف). When his master learned of his conversion to Islam, he went mad and began chapters of torture that are difficult for the human mind to conceive. He would take him out at midday, when the desert sand turned to embers, strip him of his clothes, and throw him on his bare back onto the burning sand. Then he would order a massive rock to be placed on his chest until he could barely breathe and his ribs were about to shatter.

The goal was clear and direct: "You will remain like this until you die or you disbelieve in Muhammad and worship Al-Lat and Al-Uzza." But the faith that had settled in Bilal's heart was stronger than the rock and hotter than the sand. While his body melted in pain, his soul soared in the heavens of monotheism, and his tongue uttered only one word, which became an eternal anthem for the free: "Ahadun... Ahad." (One... One). It was not just a word; it was a creedal and political declaration that demolished the entire world of polytheism. Every "Ahad" he uttered was like a thunderbolt on Umayyah's head, reminding him of his powerlessness against the strength of faith.

The torture did not end there. Umayyah would tie a rope around his neck and hand him over to the boys and foolish men of Mecca to be dragged through its streets, while he would say nothing more than "Ahadun... Ahad." One day, Abu Bakr As-Siddiq passed by while he was being tortured, so he bought him with his own money and freed him for the sake of Allah. Umar ibn Al-Khattab (عمر بن الخطاب) later made his famous statement: "Abu Bakr is our master, and he freed our master."

The Family of Yasir (آل ياسر): The First Martyred Family in Islam

Yasir, his wife Sumayyah (سمية), and their son Ammar (عمار) were among the first to embrace Islam. They were allies of the Banu Makhzum clan, the tribe of the "Pharaoh of this nation," Abu Jahl. Abu Jahl personally took on the task of torturing this peaceful family. He would take them out to the burning grounds of Mecca and dress them in iron armor to intensify the heat of the sun.

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, would pass by them, possessing no material power to prevent the harm, and his heart would break with pain. But he offered them the greatest weapon: reassurance and glad tidings. He would say to them, "Patience, O family of Yasir, for your appointed place is Paradise." These words were like cool rain on burning hearts, renewing their resolve and connecting them to the eternal reward.

On one of the days of torture, while Sumayyah remained firm in her faith, refusing to give Abu Jahl what he wanted, the tyrant flew into a rage. In a moment of absolute brutality, he stabbed her with a spear in her private parts, and she fell instantly, becoming the first martyr in Islam. Her husband, Yasir, followed her, martyred under torture.

As for Ammar, he was subjected to unbearable torture until they extracted from him a word of disbelief with his tongue, while his heart remained full of faith. He came to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, weeping and afraid. The Prophet asked him, "How do you find your heart?" He replied, "Content with faith." The Prophet said, "O Ammar, if they return [to torturing you], then you return [to saying what they want]." And the verse of Allah was revealed about him, becoming a concession and a mercy for the entire nation until the Day of Judgment:

“...except for one who is forced [to renounce his religion] while his heart is secure in faith.” (Surah An-Nahl: 106)
.

Khabbab ibn Al-Aratt (خباب بن الأرت): A School of Bodily Sacrifice

Khabbab was a blacksmith, so his enemies used the tools of his trade to torture him. They would heat iron skewers until they were red hot, then uncover his back and brand him with them. They would light a fire, throw him onto it, and nothing would extinguish the flames except the melting fat of his back as it trickled down. Many years later, during the caliphate of Umar ibn Al-Khattab, when he uncovered his back, it looked as if it were afflicted with leprosy from the severity of the torture marks.

Other Examples of Resilience

These were not the only ones; the list of heroes was long:

  • Suhaib Ar-Rumi (صُهَيْب الرُّومِيّ) was tortured until he lost consciousness and did not know what he was saying.
  • 'Amir ibn Fuhayrah (عامر بن فهيرة) was tortured so severely that Abu Bakr bought and freed him.
  • Zinnirah (زنيرة), a female slave, was tortured until she lost her sight. The polytheists said, "Al-Lat and Al-Uzza have struck her." She replied, "They lie, by the House of Allah! It was not Al-Lat and Al-Uzza..." And Allah restored her sight.

Even those who had some protection from their clans were not entirely safe. 'Uthman ibn 'Affan (عثمان بن عفان) was wrapped in a mat of palm leaves by his uncle, who would then light a fire underneath to fill it with smoke. Mus'ab ibn 'Umayr (مصعب بن عمير), the pampered youth of Mecca, was imprisoned by his mother, who deprived him of everything until his skin became rough and cracked.

This stage was the harshest test, the sieve that separated the truthful from the pretender. These heroes proved with their blood and their pain that faith is not a word to be spoken, but a reality that takes root in the heart, for which all pain and sacrifice become insignificant. And with their legendary patience, they laid the solid foundation upon which the towering edifice of Islam was built.

Recap: The Escalating Methods of Opposition

  • Stage 1 (Temptation): Attempting to buy the message with offers of wealth, leadership, and kingship, which failed against prophetic steadfastness.
  • Stage 2 (Defiance): Demanding impossible material miracles to embarrass the Prophet and prove him powerless.
  • Stage 3 (Media War): A campaign of ridicule, character assassination (labeling him a "sorcerer"), and spreading lies to create a barrier of fear and doubt.
  • Stage 4 (Violence): Resorting to brutal physical torture of the most vulnerable believers when all other methods failed.

Conclusion: Patience and Steadfastness.. The Principles of the Path and the Methodology of Victory

Closing the pages of the Seerah on its Meccan period is not like closing any other history book. It is more like graduating from a complete school, from which one emerges with solid convictions, profound insight, and a heart filled with certainty. What we have learned are not merely stories of the heroism of the first generation, but an understanding of the divine methodology of victory and a deep comprehension of the divine laws (sunan) that govern the journey of truth in its eternal struggle against falsehood. At the heart of this methodology stand two fundamental values: patience as a strategic weapon, and steadfastness as the condition for arrival.

The Philosophy of Patience: From a Moral Virtue to a Strategic Weapon

A superficial mind might think of patience as merely a passive virtue: bending to the storm until it passes, a listless waiting for relief, a synonym for weakness and helplessness. But the Qur'an and the Seerah present us with a completely different understanding, one that elevates patience from the realm of purely individual ethics to being an effective strategic weapon, a tremendous positive force, and an integrated methodology of action. Patience in the methodology of the prophets is not surrender; it is positive resilience. It is not weakness; it is strength in its highest form. It is an act of worship through which a servant draws closer to his Lord in the darkest of circumstances.

1. Patience as a Weapon that Psychologically Defeats the Enemy

The ultimate goal of an oppressor's brutality is not merely to inflict physical pain; his supreme objective is to break the spirit, shatter the will, and see his opponent collapse, crying for help or renouncing his principles. When a believer meets all this harm with beautiful patience and firm steadfastness, he disarms his enemy of his greatest weapon: the pleasure of subjugation and the satisfaction of his vindictive desires. The patient one becomes, in the eyes of his tormentor, a mountain unshaken by winds, and the oppressor becomes like an angry child striking a solid rock with his fist, only to hurt his own pride and burn in the fire of his rage.

It is a war of psychological attrition, won by the one whose patience is longer and more beautiful. Let us consider the story of Pharaoh's magicians, who at the beginning of the day were the tyrant's aides, and by its end, righteous martyrs. When they believed, and Pharaoh threatened them with the most horrific forms of death:

“I will surely cut off your hands and your feet on opposite sides, and I will crucify you on the trunks of palm trees,” (Surah Ta-Ha: 71)
, they did not beg or retreat. Instead, they responded with a patience and certainty that shook Pharaoh's throne:
“They said, ‘Never will we prefer you over what has come to us of clear proofs and [over] He who created us. So decree whatever you are to decree. You can only decree for this worldly life.’” (Surah Ta-Ha: 72)
. They had achieved a spiritual victory before their souls were taken, and they declared Pharaoh's complete psychological defeat. This is why Allah promised the patient His special company:
“Indeed, Allah is with the patient.” (Surah Al-Baqarah: 153)
—a company of victory, support, and strength.

2. Patience as a Divine Tool for Nurturing and Sifting

The years of persecution in Mecca were not just a period of random suffering; they were an intentional divine "smelting furnace." Hardships sift the ranks, weed out the pretenders, and reveal the true mettle of men. A great cause that will change the face of the world cannot be carried by a group of weak-hearted individuals or seekers of worldly interests. A severe test is necessary to separate the wicked from the good, after which only the pure gold capable of bearing the burdens of the message remains.

The Almighty explains this unchanging law:

“Do the people think that they will be left to say, ‘We believe’ and they will not be tried? But We have certainly tried those before them, and Allah will surely make evident those who are truthful, and He will surely make evident the liars.” (Surah Al-Ankabut: 2-3)
. Trial and tribulation are the detectors of truthfulness, and through them, a solid foundation is built that will not be shaken by the first storm. This is Allah's way of preparing leaders and building nations:
“And We made from among them leaders guiding by Our command when they were patient and [when] they were certain of Our signs.” (Surah As-Sajdah: 24)
. Thus, Allah made patience and certainty two essential conditions for leadership in the religion.

3. Patience as the Highest Level of Servitude and Trust in Allah

Patience in the face of harm is not just endurance; it is the highest degree of servitude and absolute trust in Allah's wisdom and timing. When the harm against the companions reached its peak, Khabbab ibn Al-Aratt came to the Prophet, peace be upon him, who was resting on his cloak in the shade of the Kaaba. In understandable human despair, they said, "Will you not seek victory for us? Will you not pray to Allah for us?"

The Prophet's response was not a prayer for the immediate destruction of the disbelievers but a profound lesson in the jurisprudence of patience, linking it to the history of the prophets before them. His noble face turned red, not out of anger at their complaint, but out of frustration with the spirit of haste that contradicts the wisdom of divine gradualism. He said: "Among the nations before you, a man would be taken and a pit would be dug for him, and he would be placed in it. Then a saw would be brought and placed on his head, and he would be sawn into two halves, yet that would not turn him away from his religion. He would be combed with iron combs between his flesh and bones, yet that would not turn him away from his religion..."

After painting this horrific picture of the patience of their predecessors, he presented them with a bright image of the promised future to affirm that this patience was not in vain: "...By Allah, this matter will be completed until a rider travels from Sana'a to Hadhramaut, fearing nothing but Allah, or a wolf for his sheep. But you are in a hurry." (Narrated by Al-Bukhari).

The lesson was clear: victory is surely coming, and security will be established, but the price of this secure future is your patience today in this painful reality. It is the servitude of submission to Allah's timing and the trust that after this dark night comes a true dawn.

4. Patience with the Hardship of the Path and the Length of the Call

Patience is not limited to enduring the harm of enemies; it extends to include patience with the hardship of the path itself, its long duration, and the scarcity of respondents. The best example is the chief of the Messengers, Nuh (Noah), peace be upon him, who called his people for

“a thousand years less fifty,” (Surah Al-Ankabut: 14)
. He called them night and day, secretly and openly, but their only response was that they
“put their fingers in their ears, covered themselves with their garments, persisted, and were arrogant with [great] arrogance.” (Surah Nuh: 7)
. Despite all this extraordinary effort, the result was
“And none had believed with him, except a few.” (Surah Hud: 40)
.

This is patience with the scarcity of results, patience with people's aversion, and steadfastness on the principle despite the absence of any glimmer of hope on the horizon. This is the patience for which its people will be given their reward without account, as Allah has promised:

“Indeed, the patient will be given their reward without account.” (Surah Az-Zumar: 10)
.

Thus, we see that patience is not just a moral quality but a complete philosophy, a multi-dimensional weapon, a tool of nurturing, a condition for victory, and a noble act of worship. It is the fuel that moves the caravan of faith through the desert of tribulations until it reaches the oasis of empowerment.

The Certainty of Steadfastness: Understanding the Divine Laws of Conflict

The greatest fruit reaped by one who contemplates the Prophetic Biography is not mere historical knowledge, but the attainment of a state of "certainty" (yaqeen). The certainty that what happens on the journey of da'wah is not chaos or a series of random events, but the unfolding of fixed divine laws (sunan) that do not change or alter. Understanding these laws transforms the caller from a person stumbling in the dark, surprised by events and improvising reactions, into a leader who reads the roadmap clearly and walks the path with confidence and insight, no matter the obstacles.

1. The Law of Inevitable Confrontation: The Clash is a Decree, Not a Choice

The first of these laws that must be firmly established in the certainty of every believer is that the clash between truth and falsehood is not an avoidable option but a foregone conclusion and an inherent feature of this worldly life. From the moment Iblis (Satan) refused to prostrate to Adam, the great battle of existence began. The mere existence of truth is in itself a provocation to falsehood, and the mere call to light is a disturbance to the people of darkness. Truth, by its nature, exposes and uncovers; it lays bare the falsity of falsehood and shatters its idols, whether they are idols of stone or idols of ideas, authority, and desires.

The Almighty states this cosmic law:

“And if it were not for Allah checking some people by means of others, the earth would have been corrupted.” (Surah Al-Baqarah: 251)
. This "checking" or "pushing back" is the law of life, and through it, Allah preserves the earth from absolute corruption. Whoever thinks he can carry the call for change and walk a path strewn with roses, to be welcomed by everyone, has understood nothing of the Qur'an and the Seerah and is living in an illusion that will inevitably crash against the rock of reality. The Qur'an is clear that the battle is not just for existence, but for ideological eradication:
“And never will the Jews or the Christians approve of you until you follow their religion.” (Surah Al-Baqarah: 120)
. The ultimate goal of falsehood is not just to silence you, but to make you one of them.

The story of the People of the Ditch (Ashab al-Ukhdud) in Surah Al-Buruj is the absolute embodiment of this law. The young boy and the believers committed no crime other than saying, "We believe in the Lord of the worlds," but the mere existence of their faith was a challenge to the authority of the tyrannical king. He found no solution but to light a fire for them. This is always the logic of falsehood: "Either you are with me, or you do not exist at all."

2. The Law of Recurring Methods: History Repeats Itself

One of the greatest things that gives the heart tranquility and removes feelings of loneliness and alienation is the realization that the methods of the people of falsehood in opposition and confrontation are the same throughout the ages. They repeat with astonishing precision, as if they are copying each other's plans. That is why the Qur'an is filled with the stories of the prophets and their peoples—not for entertainment, but to be a methodology for strengthening faith and building awareness.

  • Ridicule: This was the first weapon used by the people of Nuh against him as he built the ark in the desert:
    “And every time a group of the eminent of his people passed by him, they ridiculed him.” (Surah Hud: 38)
    . It is the same ridicule that the Prophet Muhammad and his companions faced in Mecca.
  • The Accusation of Magic: This was the accusation leveled by Pharaoh against Moses:
    “He said to the eminent around him, ‘Indeed, this is a learned magician.’” (Surah Ash-Shu'ara: 34)
    . It is the same accusation that Quraysh settled on in Dar an-Nadwa.
  • The Threat of Expulsion and Exile: This is what Shu'ayb faced from his people:
    “We will surely drive you out, O Shu'ayb, and those who have believed with you from our city.” (Surah Al-A'raf: 88)
    . This is what Quraysh planned against the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, which ended with the Hijrah (migration).
  • Violence and Murder: This is how the people of Abraham (Ibrahim, إبراهيم) tried to silence him:
    “They said, ‘Burn him and support your gods.’” (Surah Al-Anbiya: 68)
    . This is what Quraysh practiced against the oppressed companions.

Allah Almighty says to His Prophet:

“And each [story] We relate to you from the news of the messengers is that by which We make your heart firm.” (Surah Hud: 120)
. These stories are not just historical information; they are a divine "medicine of steadfastness." They tell you: "You are not alone on this path. The best of creation walked it before you, and they suffered what you suffer, and perhaps even more. So be patient as they were patient."

3. The Law of Divine Victory: The True Promise of Allah

Just as trial and confrontation are a law, so too is victory for the people of truth a divine law and a definite promise that is never broken. But it is a victory that has its conditions, its timing, and its wisdom. It does not come easily, nor is it granted to the impatient. It is the fruit of patience and steadfastness after the most severe stages of being "shaken" and tested.

The Qur'an depicts this critical moment that precedes the dawn in the most magnificent way:

“Or do you think that you will enter Paradise while such [trial] has not yet come to you as came to those who passed on before you? They were afflicted by poverty and hardship and were shaken so violently that [even] the Messenger and those who believed with him said, ‘When is the help of Allah?’ Unquestionably, the help of Allah is near.” (Surah Al-Baqarah: 214)
. It is the moment when distress reaches its peak, and souls are violently shaken, until even the Messengers themselves reach the height of feeling the need for divine aid and ask, "When is the help of Allah?" And at that very moment, the healing and sufficient answer comes: "Unquestionably, the help of Allah is near."

Victory does not have only one form. It may be a victory through argument and evidence, or the salvation of the believers and the destruction of the deniers as happened with Nuh and Musa, or empowerment on earth and the establishment of a state as happened with our Prophet Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him. The important thing is that Allah's promise of victory is absolute:

“Indeed, We will support Our messengers and those who believe during the life of this world and on the Day when the witnesses will stand.” (Surah Ghafir: 51)
.

In the end, the Seerah teaches us that the path is long and arduous, and its costs are high, but its fruit is worth every sacrifice. It teaches us that our role is not to guarantee results, but to remain steadfast on the methodology. It teaches us to plant the seed with sincerity, water it with the tears of patience and the sweat of effort, and then leave the matter of its growth to the Lord of the heavens. For the final outcome is for the righteous, and Allah does not fail in His promise.

Introduction: When Harm Reaches Its Peak... The Wisdom of a Decision and the Beginning of a New Path

When the conflict between truth and falsehood reaches its zenith, when all means of dialogue and compromise are exhausted, and when the language of confrontation turns to physical brutality and direct harm, some might think the road has reached a dead end. But the Prophet's biography teaches us that the darkest moments are those that precede the dawn, and that the peak of a trial is the very thing that gives birth to a blessing from its womb.

After the Islamic call in Mecca had drunk from the bitter cups of persecution—from mockery and defamation to the brutal torture inflicted upon the vulnerable—the machine of falsehood did not stop there. Its audacity escalated to target the person of the Prophet ﷺ himself in his most sacred moments and among those closest to him. At this point, it was no longer a mere test of patience but had become an existential threat to the very seed of the message.

In this decisive moment, the end was not submission or despair, but rather the beginning of an entirely new chapter in the history of Islam. From the heart of this suffering, the wisdom of a Prophetic decision emerged that would change the rules of the game, moving the call from a stage of defensive resilience in a narrow geographical spot to a stage of strategic launch towards a broader horizon. The decision to migrate to Abyssinia was not merely an escape from harm; it was the first practical step towards the universality of the message, an intelligent search for a safe haven where the souls of the believers could be preserved, the dignity of the religion protected, and a rear base established that would play a pivotal role in the future of Islam.

1. Escalation of the Confrontation: When the Harm Targets the Prophet ﷺ Personally

After Quraysh failed to break the will of the vulnerable believers, they realized that targeting the followers alone would not extinguish the light of the message as long as its source, the Prophet ﷺ, stood firm like a towering mountain. Thus, their malicious strategy shifted to a more dangerous and audacious phase: attempting to break the Prophet's personal prestige and drive him to despair by targeting what he held most sacred: his worship, his family, and his dignity. The harm moved from the periphery to the very heart in a desperate attempt to silence the voice that had shaken their entity.

Psychological and Familial Warfare: Striking the Innermost Circle

Quraysh did not dare to directly kill the Prophet ﷺ due to his protection under the Banu Hashim, so they resorted to what is more painful to the soul: psychological and familial warfare targeting his closest and most beloved people.

The Harm of Umm Jamil and the Protection of the Angels:

The enmity did not only come from outside; hatred also emanated from within the house of the Prophet's own uncle, Abu Lahab. His wife, Arwa bint Harb, nicknamed "Umm Jamil" and described by the Quran as

﴿the carrier of firewood﴾
, was one of his most fervent haters. After the revelation of Surah Al-Masad, which exposed her and her husband, she flew into a rage, picked up a "fihr" (a large stone), and went out looking for the Prophet ﷺ to strike him with it.

The Prophet ﷺ was sitting by the Kaaba with Abu Bakr Al-Siddiq when Umm Jamil approached, seeing only Abu Bakr. She stood before him and said, "O Abu Bakr, where is your companion? I have heard that he satirizes me. By Allah, if I had found him, I would have smashed his mouth with this stone." Then she left. Abu Bakr said, "O Messenger of Allah, do you not think she saw you?" The Prophet ﷺ replied, "She did not see me. Allah veiled her sight from me." In another narration, he said, "There was an angel shielding me from her with his wings." It was direct divine protection, showing that whoever is in Allah's company cannot be reached by the hands of aggressors, even if they are steps away.

The Familial Stab: Forcing the Divorce of His Daughters to Deepen the Humiliation:

In a malicious attempt to break the Prophet's ﷺ heart and make him feel isolated even within his own extended family, Abu Lahab resorted to a cruel weapon. His two sons, Utbah and Utaybah, had married the daughters of the Messenger of Allah, Ruqayyah and Umm Kulthum, before the advent of Islam. Abu Lahab and his wife ordered their sons to divorce them, for no reason other than to hurt their father. He told them, "My head is unlawful to yours if you do not divorce Muhammad's daughters."

This act was a treacherous blow, aimed not only at hurting the Prophet's feelings but also at making his daughters feel humiliated and rejected, demonstrating that association with him had become a source of shame from which even the closest families were disassociating. The two sons complied with their father's command and divorced the daughters of the noblest of creation, in a scene reflecting the depth of hatred harbored by his closest relatives.

Direct Physical Assault: Violating the Sanctity of Worship

The audacity escalated to the point of direct physical assault on the Prophet ﷺ in the most sacred place and the most noble posture, in an attempt to humiliate him in public and violate the sanctity of his prayer to his Lord.

The Incident of the Camel's Intestines: The Peak of Viciousness and the Heroism of Fatimah:

Abdullah ibn Mas'ud (may Allah be pleased with him) narrates this painful scene, saying: While the Prophet ﷺ was praying near the Kaaba, and Abu Jahl and his companions were sitting, one of them said to the others, "Who among you will go to the carcass of the camel of so-and-so, take its intestines, and place them on Muhammad's shoulders when he prostrates?" The most wretched of the people, Uqbah ibn Abi Mu'ayt, volunteered, brought it, and placed it between the Prophet's ﷺ shoulders while he was in prostration.

Ibn Mas'ud said, "He did not raise his head, and I was watching. If I had any power, I would have thrown it off his back." The Prophet remained in prostration, steadfast, patient, not cutting his connection with his Lord despite this disgusting abuse, while they laughed hysterically, shaking so much that they leaned on one another.

None of the men dared to come to his aid until someone went and informed Fatimah (may Allah be pleased with her), who was then a "juwayriyyah" (a young girl). She came running, threw the filth off him, and then turned to them, cursing and rebuking them with the tongue of truth that fears no blame in the path of Allah. When the Prophet ﷺ finished his prayer, he raised his voice and supplicated against them. It was known that when a supplication was made against them in their city, they believed it would be answered. He prayed: "O Allah, deal with Quraysh" three times, then he named them: "O Allah, deal with Abu Jahl, and deal with Utbah ibn Rabi'ah, Shaybah ibn Rabi'ah, Al-Walid ibn Utbah, Umayyah ibn Khalaf, and Uqbah ibn Abi Mu'ayt." Ibn Mas'ud says, "By Him who sent Muhammad with the truth, I saw those he named slain on the day of Badr."

Abu Jahl's Attempt: A Miraculous Divine Protection:

The "Pharaoh of this nation," Abu Jahl, was not deterred. He swore by Al-Lat and Al-Uzza, saying, "If I see Muhammad praying at the Kaaba, I will surely tread on his neck and rub his face in the dust."
