  2. Individual Chivalry (Humanitarian Smuggling): The Jahiliyyah was not devoid of men, even among disbelievers, moved by honor.
    • Hakim bin Hizam (حكيم بن حزام) (Khadijah's nephew) used to carry wheat to his aunt secretly. Abu Jahl saw him once, grabbed him, and said: "Are you taking food to Banu Hashim? By Allah, you shall not leave until I expose you in Mecca." Then Abu Al-Bakhtari bin Hisham (أبو البختري بن هشام) (a disbeliever) came and said to Abu Jahl: "Woe to you! It is food belonging to his aunt that was with him and he is sending it to her; do you prevent him from bringing her her food? Let the man go." He then took a camel's jawbone and struck Abu Jahl, wounding him.
    • Hisham bin 'Amr (هشام بن عمرو) used to bring a camel loaded with food, dates, and clothes to the entrance of the Shi'b at night, then strike it and shoo it so it would enter upon Banu Hashim.

The Lesson Learned: The comparison reveals that the enmity of the "First Jahiliyyah" was governed by a moral ceiling and tribal customs preventing total extermination or the denial of water and medicine. In contrast, the "Contemporary Jahiliyyah" has dropped all masks; hospitals are bombed, water is denied, and millions are besieged for years without a contemporary "Abu Al-Bakhtari" coming out to strike the oppressor's hand.

The lesson of the Shi'b teaches us that steadfastness on principle is costly, that siege may gnaw at bodies but builds the solidity of souls, and that victory always comes after hearts reach the throats, and after the ranks are distinguished, so the Muslim knows his enemy from the friend bound to him by the "bond of creed" or even the "bond of chivalry."

3. The Art of "Lobbying": How Was the Unjust Scroll Broken by Smart Planning?

Lifting the siege on Banu Hashim was not a random event, nor a free gift from Quraysh, but the fruit of a tight "political and social movement" led by one man who smartly managed to turn "silent refusal" in the souls of individuals into a "collective scream" that shook the pillars of injustice. It is the story of "The Five Men" (Al-Nafar Al-Khamsa) who taught us that an organized minority defeats a heedless majority.

The Architect of the Annulment: Hisham bin 'Amr... When Honor Moves

The story began with a man named Hisham bin 'Amr bin Rabi'ah (هشام بن عمرو بن ربيعة) (from Banu Amir bin Lu'ayy). This man upheld kinship ties and refused injustice, and he used to visit Banu Hashim secretly with food. But he realized that "individual relief work" would not solve the crisis, so he decided to move to break the siege from its roots.

Hisham did not go to scream alone in the assembly of Quraysh; rather, he began secret shuttle tours to build an "alliance" (lobby).

Round One: Provoking Emotion (With Zuhair bin Abi Umayyah)

Hisham went first to Zuhair bin Abi Umayyah Al-Makhzumi (زهير بن أبي أمية المخزومي), whose mother was Atikah bint Abdul-Muttalib (the Prophet's aunt). He entered upon him and said in a blaming, provoking tone: "O Zuhair, are you content to eat food, drink drink, and marry women, while your maternal uncles are where you know they are? (i.e., in hunger and nakedness). By Allah, if they were the uncles of Abu Al-Hakam bin Hisham (Abu Jahl) and you invited him to what he invited you to regarding them, he would never have answered you."

Here Zuhair's pride was stirred, and he said with helplessness: "Woe to you, O Hisham! What can I do when I am a single man? By Allah, if there were another man with me, I would stand up to annul it."

Hisham said confidently: "I have found a man."

He asked: "Who is he?" He said: "Me."

Zuhair said: "Find us a third man."

Round Two: Provoking Historical Shame (With Al-Mut'im bin Adi)

They went to Al-Mut'im bin Adi (المطعم بن عدي) (from Banu Nawfal). Hisham addressed him in the language of interests and reputation: "O Mut'im, are you content that two clans of Banu Abd Manaf (Banu Hashim and Banu Al-Muttalib) perish while you witness it? By Allah, if you enable them to do this (i.e., let Quraysh eradicate them), you will find them coming for you quickly (i.e., your turn will come)."

Al-Mut'im said: "Woe to you! What can I do? I am but one man."

He said: "I have found a second." He asked: "Who?" He said: "Me."

Al-Mut'im said: "Find us a third." He said: "I have done so, Zuhair bin Abi Umayyah."

He said: "Find us a fourth."

Round Three and Four: Completing the Circle

Hisham went to Abu Al-Bakhtari bin Hisham (أبو البختري بن هشام), and said to him what he said to Al-Mut'im, and he agreed. Then he went to Zam'ah bin Al-Aswad bin Al-Muttalib (زمعة بن الأسود بن المطلب), and spoke to him about his kinship and rights, and he agreed.

Thus, the circle of the "Five Revolutionaries" was complete. They met at night at "Hajun of Mecca" (a high place in upper Mecca), and agreed and pledged to rise up to annul the scroll. They drew up the plan precisely: "Let none of us show support for the other at the beginning; rather, we will follow each other in speaking until we form general pressure." They agreed that Zuhair bin Abi Umayyah would start because he was closest in lineage (son of Atikah).

The Day of Decision: The Political Theater in the Assembly of Quraysh

In the morning, the people went to their meeting places. Zuhair stood up, circumambulated the Kaaba seven times, then turned to the people and called out at the top of his voice: "O people of Mecca, do we eat food and wear clothes while Banu Hashim are perishing, with no buying or selling allowed with them? By Allah, I will not sit until this severing, unjust scroll is torn up."

The enemy of Allah, Abu Jahl, sprang up from the side of the Mosque: "You lie! By Allah, it shall not be torn."

Here, the execution of the plan began:

  1. Zam'ah bin Al-Aswad stood up and said: "You are the greater liar, by Allah. We were not pleased with its writing when it was written."
  2. Abu Al-Bakhtari jumped in and said: "Zam'ah has spoken the truth. We do not accept what is written in it, nor do we acknowledge it."
  3. Al-Mut'im bin Adi intervened: "You both spoke the truth, and whoever said otherwise lied. We disavow it to Allah and what is written in it."
  4. Hisham bin 'Amr concluded it with support.

Abu Jahl stood stunned before this "sudden consensus" and realized with his satanic intelligence what had happened, saying his famous phrase describing conspiracies: "This is a matter decided by night, consulted upon in a place other than this."

The Divine Miracle: The Termite Precedes Humans

While the people were arguing, Allah had taken charge of defending His Prophet ﷺ in another way. Revelation came down to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was in the Shi'b, informing him: "That Allah has sent upon the scroll of Quraysh the termite (woodworm), and it has eaten everything in it of injustice, severing of ties, and oppression, and left nothing but the mention of Allah Almighty ('In Your Name, O Allah')."

The Prophet ﷺ informed his uncle Abu Talib of the news. Abu Talib said eagerly: "Did your Lord tell you this?" He said: "Yes." He said: "By Allah, your Lord has not lied to you. Rise and let us enter upon the people."

Abu Talib and those with him went out to the assembly of Quraysh, while the argument was ongoing between the Five and Abu Jahl. Abu Talib stood and said with the confidence of one trusting in the revelation of Heaven:

"O gathering of Quraysh, my nephew has told me—and he has never lied to me—that Allah has sent the termite upon your scroll, and it has eaten everything in it of injustice and severing of ties, and left everything in which the name of Allah is mentioned. So bring your scroll; if he is truthful, you will desist, and if he is lying, I will hand my nephew over to you."

They said: "You have been fair."

They took down the scroll from inside the Kaaba, eyes fixed, hearts pounding. When they opened it, they found it exactly as the Truthful and Trustworthy ﷺ had said—blank and torn except for "In Your Name, O Allah" (Bismika Allahumma).

Here, he who disbelieved was dumbfounded, the voice of truth rose, the scroll was torn physically and morally, and Banu Hashim emerged from a siege that lasted three years, raising their heads with the victory of Allah.

Lessons in the Jurisprudence of Change:

  1. The Role of the Initiating Individual: Had Hisham bin 'Amr not moved and gone between houses, the group would not have gathered. Change does not wait for miracles; it creates the reasons for them.
  2. Investing in Intersections: These five were disbelievers, but they intersected with Islam in the area of "Justice and Chivalry." This teaches us the permissibility of seeking help from non-Muslims in humanitarian issues and lifting injustice, and the importance of building "lobbying" groups to serve the Ummah's causes.
  3. Tight Planning: The distribution of roles (who starts, who seconds, who concludes) was sufficient to break the prestige of tyranny and show that the refusal was "public opinion" and not just a dissenting voice.

4. The Tragedy of "Tribal Loyalty" Without Faith: The Struggle of the Final Moments on the Deathbed

Six months after Banu Hashim left the Shi'b (in Rajab or Ramadan of the 10th year of Prophethood), Abu Talib fell ill with his fatal sickness. He was over eighty, exhausted by the siege, hunger, and old age. The Prophet ﷺ realized this was the last chance to save his uncle who had surrounded him and defended him for forty years, so he rushed to him to fight the last and most dangerous Dawah battle of his life.

The Final Scene: (Say It, So I May Argue for You With It Before Allah)

The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered upon his uncle and found Abu Jahl bin Hisham (أبو جهل بن هشام) and Abdullah bin Abi Umayyah (عبد الله بن أبي أمية) (brother of Umm Salamah, before his conversion) with him. The Prophet ﷺ sat near his uncle’s head, and with the tone of a compassionate lover, he said: "O uncle, say: There is no god but Allah (La ilaha illa Allah), a word by which I will testify for you before Allah" (and in a narration: argue for you with it before Allah).

The Prophet’s ﷺ words were clear: He was not asking for new support, nor money, nor fighting, but one word that would save him from eternity in the Fire.

But the "human devils" sitting beside him realized the danger of this word. So Abu Jahl and Abdullah bin Abi Umayyah said: "O Abu Talib, do you turn away from the religion of Abdul-Muttalib?" (i.e., Will you leave the religion of your father and grandfather and bring shame upon yourself as you breathe your last?).

The Prophet ﷺ kept repeating his offer, and they kept repeating their statement, while Abu Talib hesitated. He looked at his nephew and saw truth and mercy, and looked at his companions and saw "Tribal Legacy" and "Family Reputation." In the end, his wretchedness prevailed, and "Jahiliyyah Tribalism" won over the "Call of Fitrah." He said as his last words to them: "He is on the religion of Abdul-Muttalib," and he refused to say: La ilaha illa Allah.

The Prophet’s ﷺ Loyalty and the Divine Prohibition

Abu Talib died, and the Prophet ﷺ grieved deeply. With a heart full of loyalty and pain, he said: "By Allah, I will ask forgiveness for you as long as I am not forbidden from doing so."

The Prophet ﷺ continued to ask forgiveness for his uncle until Allah Almighty revealed a Quran putting a decisive line between human emotion and Divine Truth. The verse in Surah At-Tawbah was revealed:

“It is not for the Prophet and those who have believed to ask forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they were relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are companions of Hellfire.” [At-Tawbah: 113].

Also, a verse in Surah Al-Qasas was revealed concerning Abu Talib, consoling the Prophet ﷺ and affirming that guidance is not owned by anyone, not even the Master of the progeny of Adam:

“Indeed, [O Muhammad], you do not guide whom you like, but Allah guides whom He wills. And He is most knowing of the [rightly] guided.” [Al-Qasas: 56].

Special Intercession: "In Shallow Water of Fire"

Allah did not forget Abu Talib's protection of His Prophet, but Allah’s justice refuses that a polytheist enters Paradise. In Sahih Muslim, Al-Abbas bin Abdul-Muttalib said to the Prophet ﷺ: "O Messenger of Allah, have you benefited Abu Talib with anything? For he used to protect you and get angry for you."

He ﷺ said: "Yes, he is in a shallow place (Dahdah) of Fire reaching his ankles, from which his brain boils. Were it not for me, he would have been in the lowest depth of the Fire." In another narration: "The person with the lightest punishment among the people of the Fire is Abu Talib; he wears two sandals from which his brain boils."

Imagine this terrifying scene: The lightest punishment is "boiling of the brain" from the heat of sandals! So how about the one in the lowest depths? It is a message to anyone who is deluded by serving Islam without believing in it, or relies on relation to the righteous without deeds.

Analyzing the Tragedy: Why Did Abu Talib Not Accept Islam?

Abu Talib did not doubt the truthfulness of the Prophet ﷺ. He said in his famous Lamiyah poem:
And I have known that the religion of Muhammad... is among the best religions of creation.
Were it not for blame or fear of abuse... you would have found me allowing it, clear about it.

So, the obstacle was not "intellectual," but "psychological and social."

  1. Idol of "Social Status": Abu Talib was an obeyed master. He saw following Muhammad ﷺ as a concession of his leadership and submission to his nephew, so he preferred fleeting worldly leadership over lasting Hereafter bliss.
  2. Pressure of "Public Opinion": The phrase "Were it not for blame or fear of abuse" reveals the chronic disease. He feared the women of Quraysh would taunt him saying he panicked at death and left the religion of his fathers. Abu Talib died a victim of "what people say."
  3. Bad Company: The presence of Abu Jahl and Abdullah bin Abi Umayyah at that critical moment was catastrophic. A bad friend may not harm you in your daily life, but he might be the cause of your bad ending and eternal destruction.

The Harsh Lesson: Abu Talib's tragedy teaches us that knowing the "Truth" alone is not enough for faith; there must be "submission of the heart" and breaking the barrier of arrogance. It teaches us that salvation is an individual responsibility; your lineage will not help you even if you are the Prophet's uncle, nor will your service to the religion help if the motive is not Monotheism (Tawhid) and sincerity to Allah. It is a call to review our "hidden idols": Do we leave the truth out of fear of people? And do we prioritize "Social Prestige" over the pleasure of Allah?

5. Departure of the "Internal Army": Khadijah... The Irreplaceable Solace and the Glad Tidings of the Pearl Palace

The Prophet ﷺ had barely recovered from the shock of losing his uncle Abu Talib (the external support) when he was struck in the month of Ramadan of the 10th year of Prophethood by a calamity that shook his human being; the departure of his wife, beloved, and first advisor, Sayyidah Khadijah bint Khuwaylid (خديجة بنت خويلد), may Allah be pleased with her, at the age of sixty-five. With her death, the Prophet ﷺ lost the "secure internal front" to which he sought refuge from the storms of polytheism, making this year deservedly titled the "Year of Sorrow" ('Am al-Huzn).

Testimony of Loyalty: "No, by Allah, Allah did not replace her with better than her"

Khadijah was not just a woman in the Prophet's ﷺ life; she was a "nation" in the form of a woman. This meaning is evident in the authentic Hadith narrated by Aisha (عائشة) may Allah be pleased with her—who was the most beloved of his wives after Khadijah—when she became jealous one day due to his frequent mention of Khadijah. She said: "O Messenger of Allah, what do you mention of an old woman among the old women of Quraysh, red-mouthed (toothless from old age), who died long ago, and Allah has replaced her with better than her (meaning herself)?"

Here the Prophet ﷺ became intensely angry and replied with a decisive response immortalizing Khadijah’s virtues until the Hour:

"No, by Allah! Allah did not replace her with better than her: She believed in me when people disbelieved, she trusted me when people belied me, she supported me with her wealth when people denied me, and Allah blessed me with her children when He deprived me of the children of other women."

The Prophet ﷺ summarized in these four phrases the role of the mission-oriented wife: (Faith, Validation and Psychological Support, Financial Support, and Family Care).

Glad Tidings from Above Seven Heavens: (A House of Qasab)

Heaven knew Khadijah’s worth before the people of Earth did. In the agreed-upon Hadith, Gabriel (Jibreel), peace be upon him, came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:

"O Messenger of Allah, this is Khadijah coming to you with a vessel containing condiment or food or drink. When she comes to you, convey to her peace from her Lord and from me, and give her glad tidings of a house in Paradise made of Qasab (hollow pearl), wherein there is no noise (Sakhab) and no fatigue (Nasab)."

Reflect on the precision of the Divine Reward (the reward is of the same nature as the deed):

  1. "Of Qasab" (Hollow Pearl): For the purity of her intention and the clarity of her inner self.
  2. "No noise in it" (No shouting or clamor): Because she never raised her voice at the Prophet ﷺ, nor did she make his house an arena for argument and bickering; rather, she provided him with calm and tranquility.
  3. "And no fatigue" (No tiredness): Because she removed the fatigue of the Dawah from him, and sufficed him the worries of the house and children, never burdening him with what he could not bear, so Allah rewarded her with eternal rest containing no tiredness.

Rare Loyalty: "O Allah, Hala!"

The Prophet's ﷺ love for Khadijah did not cease with her death. Whenever he slaughtered a sheep, he would cut it into pieces and send them to Khadijah’s friends out of loyalty to her memory.

In a touching situation, Hala bint Khuwaylid (هالة بنت خويلد) (Khadijah’s sister) asked permission to enter upon him one day, and her voice sounded exactly like Khadijah’s voice. When the Prophet ﷺ heard her voice, he was startled (his heart shook) and said eagerly: "O Allah, Hala!", remembering the days of Khadijah and her scent.

Analyzing the Role: The Woman is a "Support," Not a "Peer"

Khadijah's biography corrects the misconceptions about marriage in our era:

  1. Excellence in the Field of "Containment": Khadijah did not try to compete with the Prophet ﷺ in leadership; rather, she excelled in the field of "Emotional Containment." She was the embrace that received the Prophet ﷺ while he was trembling from the Cave of Hira saying: "Cover me, cover me," so she covered him and said her immortal words: "Nay! By Allah, Allah will never disgrace you; for you uphold ties of kinship, speak the truth, bear the burden of the weak, earn for the destitute, host the guest, and help in the vicissitudes of truth." She practiced the role of "Psychotherapist" par excellence, turning fear into confidence.
  2. The Only Wife: The Prophet ﷺ lived with her for twenty-five years (from age 25 to 50), which are the years of his youth and maturity, and did not marry another woman while she was alive. This is practical proof that she "filled his eyes, heart, and life." She was the wife, the beloved, the mother, the advisor, and the financial supporter, so he found no need for another in her presence.
  3. Exceptional Motherhood: Allah blessed her with all the Prophet's ﷺ children (Al-Qasim, Abdullah, Zainab, Ruqayyah, Umm Kulthum, and Fatimah) except Ibrahim. She was the excellent nurturing mother who produced for the Ummah the "Mistress of the Women of the Worlds," Fatimah Az-Zahra.

The Lesson Learned: Khadijah’s departure teaches us that behind every great man is a great woman who does not butt heads with him but supports him. It teaches us that homes are not built on rivalry and fighting for rights, but are built on affection, mercy, and complementarity. Khadijah was a "complete army" in the form of a woman, and that is why her loss was a fracture that could only be healed by Allah's success and stabilization.

6. The Jurisprudence of "Overcoming Grief": When Pain Does Not Stop the Wheel of Life and Mission

Although the tenth year of Prophethood was named the "Year of Sorrow" due to the severity of the affliction of losing the external support (Abu Talib) and the internal support (Khadijah), the Prophetic biography offers us an eloquent lesson in "Psychological Resilience" and the art of crisis management. The Prophet ﷺ did not withdraw from life, nor did he stop at the limits of weeping; rather, he quickly resumed the path of Dawah and community building, confirming that "sadness in the heart" does not mean "paralysis of the limbs."

The Women’s Initiative: Khawlah bint Hakim

The Prophet ﷺ did not wait long. In the month of Shawwal of the same year (i.e., one month after Khadijah’s death), the wise Companion Khawlah bint Hakim (خولة بنت حكيم) (wife of Uthman bin Maz'un) came to him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, will you not marry?"

He said: "Who?"

She said: "If you wish a virgin (Bikr), and if you wish a previously married woman (Thayyib)."

He said: "Who is the virgin and who is the previously married?"

She said: "As for the virgin, she is the daughter of the most beloved of Allah’s creation to you, Aisha bint Abi Bakr (عائشة بنت أبي بكر), and as for the previously married, she is Sawda bint Zam'a (سودة بنت زمعة), who has believed in you and followed you."

He said: "Mention them to me."

Marriage of "Care and Loyalty": Sayyidah Sawda bint Zam'a

The Prophetic household included the Prophet’s young daughters (Umm Kulthum and Fatimah), and they were in dire need of a mother to care for them. Therefore, the Prophet ﷺ began by marrying Sawda bint Zam'a Al-Amiriyyah (in Shawwal, year 10 of Prophethood).

  • Who is Sawda? She is the widow of the emigrant "As-Sakran bin 'Amr," who returned from Abyssinia and her husband died, so her family (who were disbelievers) feared they might tempt her away from her religion. She was an older woman, heavy in body, not possessing beauty that men would covet, but she possessed a sound mind and solid faith.
  • Wisdom of the Marriage: It was not a marriage of desire, but a marriage of "care and mercy." The Prophet ﷺ wanted to heal her brokenness, preserve her faith, and have her take over the care of his daughters. She was indeed an obedient, cheerful wife, who said to the Prophet ﷺ in her old age: "O Messenger of Allah, I have gifted my day to Aisha," preferring his pleasure and out of love for him.

Marriage of "Future and Alliance": Sayyidah Aisha bint Abi Bakr

In the same month (Shawwal), the Prophet ﷺ concluded the marriage contract with Aisha bint Abi Bakr As-Siddiq, when she was six years old, and consummated the marriage in Medina when she was nine.

  • Wisdom of the Marriage: Strengthening the bond with his friend and first vizier Abu Bakr, and preparing the "Bearer of the Banner of Knowledge" for the future. Aisha, with her sharp intelligence and young age, was qualified to memorize the details of the Prophetic life and transmit them to the Ummah, which indeed happened, as she was among those who narrated the most Hadith.
  • The True Dream: It is narrated in the Sahihayn from Aisha that she said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "You were shown to me in a dream for three nights. The angel brought you in a piece of silk and said: 'This is your wife.' I uncovered your face, and behold, it was you. I said: 'If this is from Allah, He will bring it to pass.'" So it was a marriage by Divine direction.

The Doubt Regarding "Marriage Age": Judging History by Standards of the Present

Some raise doubts about the Prophet’s ﷺ marriage to Aisha when she was young (9 years). The response is from several angles:

  1. Environmental Maturity: Puberty varies according to the environment; in hot regions (like Mecca), girls mature physically very early (between 8 and 10 years) unlike in cold regions. The marriage of a nine or ten-year-old girl was a common and natural matter among the Arabs, Romans, and Persians.
  2. Enemy Acknowledgement: Abu Jahl and others were waiting for any slip from the Prophet ﷺ. Had this marriage been a "defect" or "abnormality" in their custom, they would have defamed him for it, but they remained silent because they knew it was natural in their time and environment.
  3. Aisha's Happiness: Aisha lived with the Prophet ﷺ for nine years as the most beloved person to him, and she used to boast saying: "I am the only virgin the Messenger of Allah married." She narrated the details of their life with love and longing, and did not mention a single letter suggesting she was a child whose childhood was stolen as they claim; rather, she was mature in mind and body, a jurist and a scholar.

Preparation for Ta'if: Escaping Forward

The Prophet ﷺ did not content himself with arranging his household. In the same month (Shawwal), he went out walking on foot to Ta'if (which is about 90 km from Mecca), accompanied by his freed slave Zaid bin Harithah (زيد بن حارثة), seeking support from the tribe of "Thaqif."

This rapid exit after the death of his uncle and wife, his marriage to Sawda, and his contract with Aisha, confirms the principle of "Continuity." The leader does not stop, the Dawah does not die with the death of individuals, and the best cure for grief is immersing oneself in work and opening new horizons for hope.

1. Exiting into the Unknown: The Harsh Journey to Ta'if and the Search for "New Land"

In Shawwal of the tenth year of the Prophethood, approximately one month after the death of Lady Khadija (السيدة خديجة) and his uncle Abu Talib (أبي طالب), the Prophet ﷺ found himself alone in Mecca without a backer, his back exposed to the arrows of Quraish (قريش), which no longer missed their mark. The foolish among them became emboldened against him, inflicting upon him what they would not have dared during his uncle's life, to the point where one of them scattered dirt upon his noble head ﷺ. He entered his home with dirt on his head, and one of his daughters rose to wash him while weeping. He said to her with the steadfastness of mountains: "Do not weep, my daughter, for Allah is the Protector of your father."

The Crisis of Missionary "Oxygen Deprivation" in Mecca

Mecca had turned into a "large prison" for the Call (Da'wah). The earth, vast as it is, had narrowed, and hearts were locked with bolts and keys. It was not merely emotional grief over the loss of loved ones, but a strategic siege threatening the very existence of Islam. The Prophet ﷺ realized with his leadership intuition that remaining in Mecca without political and social protection meant the slow death of the Call, or the depletion of energy in futile side battles. It was necessary to search for a "new lung" through which the Message could breathe, and "fertile ground" that would accept the seedlings of Monotheism (Tawheed) after the land of Mecca had become a desert.

Why Ta'if? (A Reading in Geopolitics)

The choice of "Ta'if" (الطائف) was not random; it was a carefully calculated decision:

  1. Strategic Weight: Ta'if represented the "Second Pole" in the Arabian Peninsula after Mecca. It was inhabited by the powerful tribe of Thaqif (ثقيف), which rivaled Quraish in sovereignty, wealth, and numbers.
  2. The Rival Religious Center: Ta'if was the stronghold of the idol "Al-Lat" (اللات), the greatest idol of the Arabs after Hubal. Thus, opening Ta'if meant a decisive blow to polytheism in its second home.
  3. Relative Proximity: Despite its distance (about 90-100 km), it was closer to Mecca compared to Medina or Abyssinia, making it a logical choice for seeking immediate support.

The Journey of Hardship: Climbing the Peaks Barefoot

The Prophet ﷺ decided to leave, but how? Leaving on a camel or mount would attract the attention of Quraish’s spies, and they would immediately understand it was a "strategic journey" to forge external alliances, which could expose him to assassination or arrest before leaving the sanctuary boundaries.

Therefore, the Prophet ﷺ made a heroic decision fraught with danger and hardship: walking on foot.

The Prophet ﷺ set out with his sole companion and loyal freedman, Zaid bin Harithah (زيد بن حارثة), disguised from eyes. It was not a leisurely stroll in a flat valley, but a journey of "arduous ascent." Mecca lies in a low valley (about 300 meters above sea level), while Ta'if sits on the peaks of the Sarawat Mountains (rising about 1800 to 2500 meters).

They had to cover a distance of nearly one hundred kilometers, climbing rugged mountain paths, crossing Wadi Nakhla, and then ascending the towering Mount Kara, or what is known as "Aqabat Al-Hada," roads not usually taken except by strong beasts of burden. Imagine a Prophet in his fifties, burdened with sorrows, walking on stones and burning sands, climbing towering mountains step by step, his feet swelling, exhausted by thirst and the heat of the sun, all to find an ear that would listen to "La ilaha illa Allah" (There is no god but Allah).

Zaid bin Harithah: Companion of the Path and Secret

The choice of Zaid (زيد) was not in vain. He did not choose Abu Bakr, Umar, or Hamza, because the absence of these prominent figures would immediately arouse suspicion in the assemblies of Quraish. As for Zaid, he was the freedman and formerly adopted son; their shadow together was familiar and did not raise suspicion. Zaid in this journey was the guard, the comforter, the servant, and the human shield ready to receive harm on behalf of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.

"Psychological Resilience": The Greatest Lesson in Crisis Management

What boggles the mind about the Ta'if journey is its timing. It came at the peak of the Prophet's ﷺ human brokenness. Any ordinary human who loses his wife (his dwelling) and his uncle (his support) in one month, and is subjected to direct insults, would naturally enter a state of "depression" or "withdrawal" (Trauma Freeze), isolating himself and locking his door to weep over his loss.

But the Prophet ﷺ offered humanity a lesson in "Psychological Resilience." He separated with amazing skill between legitimate "heartfelt grief" and unacceptable "practical cessation." He did not allow pain to paralyze his movement but turned the energy of pain into "fuel for action."

The Prophet ﷺ taught us in this journey that life does not stop with the death of anyone, that missions do not die with the departure of supporters, and that a true leader wipes his tears with one hand and builds his future with the other. He went out to Ta'if carrying a bleeding wound in his heart, but carrying a greater concern for the Ummah in his chest, evoking the words of the Almighty:

“And be patient, [O Muhammad], and your patience is not but through Allah. And do not grieve over them and do not be in distress over what they conspire.” [An-Nahl: 127].

This exit was a silent declaration that a believer may grieve, and may weaken humanly, but he does not break, does not surrender, and does not lay down the weapon of Da'wah until he meets Allah while on the field of action, not in the corners of despair.

2. The Shock of Rejection and the Corridor of "Humiliation": When Benevolence is Met with Ingratitude

After an arduous journey that drained the body, the Prophet ﷺ and his companion Zaid (زيد) arrived at the outskirts of Ta'if, that city fortified by its high walls and lush gardens. Hopes drove him to find in Thaqif (ثقيف) what he had lost in Quraish (قريش), and for this mountain city to be the "Medina" that would embrace Islam. But what awaited him was not merely a political rejection, but one of the harshest chapters of psychological and physical humiliation in the history of Prophethood.

Meeting the Masters: The Arrogance of Falsehood and the Mockery of Tyrants

With the intelligence of a leader, the Prophet ﷺ did not begin by inviting the common people in the streets; rather, he went directly to the "heads of the hierarchy." He approached the masters and nobles of Thaqif, who were three brothers, sons of Amr bin Umayr (عمرو بن عمير): Abd Yalil (عبد ياليل), Mas'ud (مسعود), and Habib (حبيب). The Prophet ﷺ sat with them, presented Islam with an open heart, and asked them to protect him and support the religion of Allah until he conveyed his Lord's message, placing the honor of this world and the Hereafter in their hands.

But the responses came like thunderbolts, revealing sick psyches that combined arrogance, ignorance, and insolence:

  • The first said with sarcastic mockery striking at the core of the creed: "He acts as if he is tearing the cloths of the Kaaba if Allah has sent you!" He meant by this oath that it is impossible for a person like him to be a messenger, as if sending him was a crime worthy of violating the sanctity of the Holy House.
  • The second said with a look of classist superiority, despising the material and human state of the Prophet ﷺ: "Did Allah not find anyone else to send other than you?!" This is the same Jahili (ignorant) logic immortalized by the Quran in their saying:
    “And they said, 'Why was this Quran not sent down upon a great man from [one of] the two cities?'” [Az-Zukhruf: 31]
    , where they measure truth by standards of wealth and status.
  • As for the third, he said with cunning and malice: "By Allah, I will never speak to you. If you are a Messenger from Allah as you say, you are too great for me to reply to; and if you are lying against Allah, it is not befitting for me to speak to you." Thus, he closed the door of dialogue completely with a flimsy excuse.

Ten Days of Steadfastness Amidst the Storm

Despite this severe "diplomatic shock," the Prophet ﷺ did not despair. Narrations mention that he stayed in Ta'if for ten days, trying to reach the common people, knocking on doors, and sitting in markets, yet seeing nothing but frowning faces and hard hearts that inherited arrogance generation after generation. Their nobles would pass by him and say: "Get out of our land," fearing he would "corrupt" their youth and slaves. When he despaired of them ﷺ, he asked them for a final request reflecting his high security awareness: "Since you have done what you have done, keep this secret for me." (Meaning, do not inform Quraish of my coming to you and your rejection of me, so they do not gloat and become emboldened against me). But their meanness refused anything but to expose him; indeed, they incited every fool in the city against him.

The Corridor of "Humiliation": Human Hell

When the Prophet ﷺ decided to leave, they did not let him depart in peace. The leaders of Thaqif executed a vile plan; they incited their fools, slaves, and children against him, ordering the formation of two long rows (a human queue) on both sides of the road through which the Prophet ﷺ would pass.

Imagine the scene: The Prophet of Mercy, who came to bring them out of darkness into light, forced to pass through this degrading human corridor. And the tragedy began...

Shouts and obscene insults arose, and sinful hands began throwing stones at him. The throwing was not random; they deliberately aimed the stones at his "heels" (the back of the feet) to make him fall and paralyze his movement.

Noble blood flowed from the Prophet's ﷺ feet until it dyed his sandals, and the blood mixed with dirt and pebbles. Whenever the pain overwhelmed him and he sat on the ground, they forced him to stand up to continue stoning him while laughing and shouting.

Zaid bin Harithah: The Shield of Love

In this difficult situation, the highest meanings of love and sacrifice were manifested in the person of Zaid bin Harithah (زيد بن حارثة). He did not possess a sword to fight an army, but he possessed his body. Zaid would jump right and left, shielding the Prophet ﷺ with his body, receiving stones on his chest, back, and head, screaming at them: "Do you do this to the Messenger of Allah?!"

The stone bombardment continued until Zaid's head was cut in many places, blood flowing from them, but he did not care about his pain; every wound in his body was easy to bear as long as he protected the body of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They continued to chase them with stones for a distance of nearly three miles (about 5 kilometers) until they forced them into a walled garden (Ha'it) outside Ta'if, compelling them to enter it for shelter.

The Philosophy of Affliction: The Tax of Nobility and Honor

What happened in Ta'if raises a profound question: Why does Allah forsake His Prophet and Beloved before these fools? Why did the earth not split to swallow them before they threw a single stone?

The answer lies in the "Sunnah of Affliction" which is the destiny of the chosen ones. The more a servant's faith and nobility increase, the more his trial increases, so that his heart may be purified and his rank elevated. The humiliation the Prophet ﷺ was subjected to is "humiliation in the scales of humans," but it is a "badge of honor in the scales of Allah."

This scene teaches us that the path of Da'wah is not paved with roses, and that the caller may be humiliated, insulted, and beaten, and this does not diminish his worth with Allah by an atom's weight; rather, it increases his elevation. He ﷺ says in the authentic Hadith: "The people most severely tested are the Prophets, then the next best and the next best. A man is tested according to his religion."

The blood of his noble feet in Ta'if was the "ink" with which the history of patience was written, and these harsh moments were the painful labor pains that preceded the birth of "Al-Isra' wal-Mi'raj" and empowerment on earth. The Prophet ﷺ did not break; rather, he emerged from Ta'if physically wounded, but sound in creed, lofty in will, knowing that what is with Allah remains, and the [good] outcome is for the righteous.

3. In the "Garden of Reproach": The Supplication of Weakness and the Philosophy of "Divine Satisfaction"

After the noise of the fools ceased, and the stones of treachery disappeared behind the hills, the Prophet ﷺ and his companion Zaid (زيد) reached a walled garden owned by Utbah (عتبة) and Shaybah (شيبة), the sons of Rabia (ربيعة). The distance he had covered under the hail of stones exceeded three miles, enough distance to drain every bit of physical energy, but not enough to extinguish the flame of certainty in his noble heart.

The Strange Paradox: Safety in the Enemy's Den

Fate willed that the only available refuge in that harsh desert was a garden belonging to the fiercest enemies of the Call in Mecca. Utbah and Shaybah, the masters of Abd Shams, who had long harbored hostility toward the Prophet ﷺ, were at their summer resort in Ta'if.

The Prophet ﷺ entered the garden and sat in the shade of a grapevine (Hublah), leaning against its trunk, the blood not yet dry on his sandals. The sons of Rabia looked at him and saw a man from their own people (Quraish) in a pitiable state of exhaustion and wounds.

Here, "Arab chivalry" and "tribal zeal" stirred within them. Their action did not stem from faith or sympathy with the Message, but from tribal superiority; how dare the "fools of Thaqif" insult a "master from the sons of Abd Manaf"?

This scene reveals to us the complexities of the human psyche in the Jahiliyyah; disbelief may coexist with chivalry, and an enemy may protect his enemy not out of love, but to preserve social and tribal "prestige." They remained silent, did not expel him, and even sent him food, in a moment of "humanitarian truce" ordained by Allah for His Prophet to catch his breath before the Great Supplication.

The Eternal Supplication: The Constitution of Pure Servitude

In that pivotal moment, where stillness wrapped the place except for the breaths of pain, and where earthly means were completely cut off (the uncle died, the wife died, Mecca expelled him, Ta'if stoned him), the Prophet ﷺ turned with his heart and limbs to the "Causer of Causes." He did not raise his hand to curse his people, nor did he ask for kingship or urgent military victory; rather, he uttered words that are the "icon of servitude" in human history.

It was narrated that he ﷺ said:

"O Allah, to You I complain of the weakness of my strength, the paucity of my resources, and my humiliation before the people. O Most Merciful of the merciful, You are the Lord of the oppressed, and You are my Lord. To whom do You entrust me? To a distant one who greets me with a scowl? Or to an enemy to whom You have given control over my affairs? If You are not angry with me, I do not care, but Your protection (well-being) is broader for me. I seek refuge in the Light of Your Face by which darkness is illuminated, and the affairs of this world and the Hereafter are set right, lest Your anger descend upon me, or Your wrath settle upon me. To You is the reproach until You are pleased, and there is no power and no might except with You."

Text Analysis: Dissecting Pain and Certainty

  1. Complaint "to" Allah, not "of" Allah: He began by saying "To You I complain," and did not say "I complain about what You did to me." Complaint to Allah is worship and humility, while complaint of Allah is objection and discontent. He acknowledged his human weakness (weakness of my strength), the exhaustion of his options (paucity of my resources), and the loss of his social status among the disbelievers (humiliation before the people).
  2. The Bitter Existential Question: "To whom do You entrust me?". This question reflects the peak of "estrangement" from creation. Do You leave me to strangers (a distant one who greets me with a scowl)? Or to powerful enemies (an enemy to whom You have given control over my affairs)? It is the feeling of a "cosmic orphan" who finds no shelter except in the embrace of Divine Care.
  3. Psychological Balance (Asking for Well-being): Despite his courage and contentment, he added: "But Your protection (well-being) is broader for me." This is a great lesson; the believer does not seek affliction, does not wish to meet the enemy, and does not enjoy pain masochistically, but asks Allah for well-being (Afiyah), yet if affliction descends, he is patient.

Philosophy of "Contentment": (If You are not angry with me, I do not care)

This sentence is the "core of the matter" and the peak of the supplication. It sets a new standard for success and failure in life, a standard that obliterates all material measures.

  • The Meaning: O Lord, it does not matter if I am stoned, it does not matter if my blood flows, it does not matter if all people deny me, it does not matter if I go hungry or am chased away... All this is "noise on the outside." What matters is the "inside," what matters is Your view of me. Are You pleased with me? If this affliction is not a punishment from You, and not an expulsion from Your mercy, then I do not care about anything that happens, and I will bear it with an open heart.
  • Turning the Sentence into a "Life Motto": If we made the slogan "If You are not angry with me, I do not care" a way of life, most of our psychological problems would disappear:
    • In material loss: Lost my job or money? (If You are not angry with me, I do not care).
    • In relationships: People let me down or ruined my reputation? (The important thing is that You are not displeased with me).
    • In illness: My health is gone? (Your well-being is broader, but if it is Your decree, then contentment is the medicine of the soul).

Seeking Refuge in the Light: Doctrinal Depth

The Prophet ﷺ concluded his supplication by seeking refuge in an intrinsic attribute of Allah: "I seek refuge in the Light of Your Face by which darkness is illuminated." Evoking the "Light of the Face" here has profound significance; just as sensory darkness (darkness of night and the road) is dispelled by the light of the sun, the darkness of the soul (worry, sadness, fear, oppression) is only dispelled by the Light of Allah's Face. He is asking for a "spiritual illumination" that extracts his heart from the dark tunnel of Ta'if into the space of contentment and tranquility.

Conclusion: In the Garden of Reproach, the Prophet ﷺ taught us that "resilience" does not mean lack of complaint, but means complaining to the Right Entity (Allah). He taught us that a believer's body may be broken, but his spirit remains towering as long as it is connected to the Sky, and that the pinnacle of happiness is not in the applause of the masses, but in the tranquility of the conscience that whispers: "O Lord, the important thing is that You are pleased."

4. Glimmers of Light in the Darkness: Addas the Christian and the Option of "Destroying the Towns"

In those dark moments, where all doors seemed closed, Allah willed to open a small "window of light" for His Prophet ﷺ, to confirm to him that his message is not lost, and that living hearts pick up the Truth even if its bearer is a stranger and oppressed. In the heart of the enemy's garden, and by the order of the garden owners themselves (Utbah and Shaybah), fates moved to arrange a brief meeting that would change the destiny of a man and comfort the heart of a Prophet.

Addas: The Stranger Who Recognized the Truth

The sons of Rabia called a Christian slave of theirs named "Addas" (عداس) and said to him: "Take a bunch of these grapes, place it on this platter, then go with it to that man and tell him to eat from it."

Addas was a man from the people of "Nineveh" (in Iraq), brought by fate to be a servant in Mecca. He carried the platter and placed it before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, saying: "Eat."

The Prophet ﷺ extended his hand, and before putting a grape into his mouth, he said in a calm and audible voice: "Bismillah" (In the name of Allah).

Addas’s movement stopped, and his eyes froze on the face of the Prophet ﷺ. This word was the "code" that unlocked his heart. He said eagerly: "By Allah, these words are not spoken by the people of this land!" (The people of Ta'if and Mecca did not know the Basmalah; rather, they said "In the name of Al-Lat and Al-Uzza").

The Prophet ﷺ replied with the wisdom of a caller picking up the thread: "And from which land are you, O Addas? And what is your religion?"

He said: "I am a Christian, and I am a man from the people of Nineveh."

The Prophet ﷺ said, evoking the shared history of messages: "From the village of the righteous man Yunus bin Matta (Jonah, son of Matthew)?"

Here Addas's astonishment increased, for the people here knew nothing but trade and idols, so where would this strange man know the name of a prophet who lived centuries ago in the distant land of Iraq?

Addas said: "And what makes you know who Yunus bin Matta is?"

The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said with the certainty of the confident: "That is my brother; he was a Prophet and I am a Prophet."

Addas did not need more than that. His "sound instinct" (Fitrah) met with the "Light of Prophethood." He did not ask for a miracle, nor did he argue about doctrines. In a rare moment of honesty, Addas threw himself upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, kissing his head, his hands, and his feet (those very feet that stones had bloodied moments ago), as if wiping away the traces of harm with the tears of repentance and faith.

The sons of Rabia watched the scene in amazement, and one said to the other with regret: "As for your slave, he has corrupted him for you!" When Addas returned, they scolded him saying: "Woe to you, Addas! Why do you kiss that man's head, hands, and feet?"

He answered them with the answer of the confident: "O my master, there is nothing on earth better than this; he told me of a matter that none but a Prophet would know."

The Offer of the "Angel of Mountains": The Moment of Testing Revenge

After this touching situation, the Prophet ﷺ left the garden returning, distressed and not recovering from his grief until he was in the area of "Qarn al-Tha'alib" (known today as Qarn al-Manazil or Al-Sayl Al-Kabir). There, the heavenly encounter occurred that placed all of humanity in one pan of the scale, and the mercy of Muhammad ﷺ in the other.

The Prophet ﷺ raised his head, and behold, a cloud shaded him. He looked and saw Gabriel (Jibreel) peace be upon him within it. He called out to him and said: "Allah has heard the statement of your people to you, and how they replied to you, and He has sent to you the Angel of the Mountains to order him to do whatever you wish regarding them."

Then the Angel of the Mountains called out to him, greeted him, and said: "O Muhammad, if you wish, I will bring together the Al-Akhshabayn (The Two Mountains) upon them?"

(Al-Akhshaban are the two mountains of Mecca: Abu Qubais and Quayqia'an, or the mountains surrounding Ta'if). The offer was clear and tempting: One command, one word, and the city that humiliated you is crushed, the people who denied you and bloodied your feet are exterminated, and disbelief ends in moments via a devastating geological earthquake. It is the "Samson Option" (upon me and my enemies), or the option of Noah peace be upon him when he said:

{My Lord, do not leave upon the earth from among the disbelievers an inhabitant.}

The Gifted Mercy: (Nay, I Hope...)

In this moment, where blood boils in the veins from pain and injustice, and where the human soul yearns for revenge and the restoration of dignity, the greatness of the "Final Prophethood" manifested.

The Prophet ﷺ said with a heart filled with hope in Allah and compassion for the servants:

"Nay, I hope that Allah will bring forth from their loins those who worship Allah alone, associating nothing with Him."

Contemplate the greatness of this response:

  1. Future Vision: He did not look at the "Criminal Fathers" who deserved punishment, but looked at the "Future Children" who had not yet been born. He saw in the loins of the disbelievers a potential seed of faith, so he decided to be patient with the harm of the fathers for the sake of the life of the sons.
  2. Prioritizing the Interest of the Call: Revenge heals the chest momentarily, but it ends the Da'wah and closes the file of an entire people. Patience, however, keeps the door open for guidance. His intuition ﷺ proved true; from the loins of these people came Ikrimah bin Abi Jahl, Khalid bin Al-Walid, Safwan bin Umayyah, and others who became leaders of Islam.
  3. Pardon when Able: True pardon is that which happens when you have the power to crush your enemy (you have the Angel of the Mountains), not when you are weak.

This stance is the practical application of the Almighty's saying:

“And We have not sent you, [O Muhammad], except as a mercy to the worlds.” [Al-Anbiya: 107].
His dream and mercy saved an entire city from annihilation, and proved to the world that his message is "Revival" not "Extermination," and Guidance not Revenge.

5. The Hidden Victory: Islam of the Jinn and Returning under the Protection of a "Polytheist"

After the Prophet ﷺ refused the destruction of Ta'if, he continued the journey back to Mecca, but stopped in the area of "Wadi Nakhla" (a valley between Mecca and Ta'if). There, while thinking he was alone except for his Lord and his companion Zaid, Allah was arranging a cosmic surprise for him, opening a missionary front he had not imagined, to prove to him that even if the earth narrows, Allah's Kingdom is vast, and that his message has transcended the boundaries of the human world to reach the world of the Jinn.

Night of the Jinn: The Quran Pierces the Veils

In the depth of the night, the Prophet ﷺ stood praying and reciting the Quran with his melodious, humble voice. During that time, Allah directed a group of Jinn towards him (it is said they were seven or nine from the Jinn of Nusaybin or Yemen). This group passed by and heard a recitation they had never heard the likes of before. They stopped, astounded, and said to one another: "Listen silently."

Silence fell upon them as they listened to the verses of Allah flowing like light, piercing the depths of their hearts. As soon as the Prophet ﷺ finished his recitation, they immediately transformed from "listeners" to "callers and warners." They turned back to their people rushing, carrying the glad tidings of the new Faith.

The Quran immortalized this great scene in Surahs "Al-Ahqaf" and "Al-Jinn":

“And [mention, O Muhammad], when We directed to you a few of the jinn, listening to the Quran. And when they attended it, they said, 'Listen quietly.' And when it was concluded, they turned back to their people as warners.” [Al-Ahqaf: 29].
“Say, [O Muhammad], "It has been revealed to me that a group of the jinn listened and said, 'Indeed, we have heard an amazing Quran. * It guides to the right course, and we have believed in it. And we will never associate with our Lord anyone.'” [Al-Jinn: 1-2].

Implications of the Event:

  1. Universality of the Call: The Prophet ﷺ was not sent only to humans, but to the two weighty beings (Humans and Jinn).
  2. Preserved Reward: The Prophet ﷺ went out seeking the Islam of "Thaqif" and they refused, so Allah compensated him with the Islam of the "Jinn." This is a lesson for every caller and reformer: You may exert effort in a place and see no fruit, so Allah drives the fruit to you from another place you did not expect, because "Allah does not waste the reward of those who do good."
  3. Hidden Victory: Victory may be invisible in human calculations (no one from Ta'if converted), but it is a strategic victory by opening new worlds for the Call.

Return to Mecca: Legal Politics and the Art of "Protection" (Jawar)

The Prophet ﷺ approached Mecca, but he realized with his security and political sense that he could not enter just like that. He had left Mecca "angry" or semi-expelled, and his return now after the failure of the Ta'if journey would make him an easy target for Quraish; either murder, humiliation, or imprisonment.

Here, the Prophet ﷺ did not wait for a miracle to drop him by parachute into the middle of the Sanctuary; rather, he practiced "Political Realism" in its highest forms. He decided to enter Mecca under the protection of the tribal "Law of Neighboring/Protection" (Jawar) prevalent at the time.

He sent a man from Khuza'ah (Urayqit) to "Al-Akhnas bin Shariq" (الأخنس بن شريق) asking for his protection, but Al-Akhnas excused himself saying he was an "ally" and an ally cannot grant protection against a principal.

Then he sent to "Suhayl bin Amr" (سهيل بن عمرو), but he excused himself with a tribal reason (that Bani Amir does not grant protection against Bani Ka'b).

The third time, he sent to "Al-Mut'im bin Adi" (المطعم بن عدي) (Chief of Bani Nawfal). Al-Mut'im, despite his disbelief, was a chivalrous man, possessing nobility, and was one of the five who had previously broken the scroll of the boycott.

Al-Mut'im agreed immediately and said to the messenger: "Yes, let him come and enter under my protection."

The Armed Protection Procession

Al-Mut'im bin Adi ordered his sons and his people to put on their weapons and stand at the four corners of the Kaaba. Then he called out to Quraish: "O people of Quraish, I have granted protection to Muhammad, so let no one among you harm him."

The Prophet ﷺ entered Mecca, accompanied by Zaid bin Harithah, and headed to the Sacred Mosque. Al-Mut'im bin Adi was mounted on his beast announcing his protection, and his sons surrounded the Prophet ﷺ with their drawn swords.

Abu Jahl saw him, choked on his saliva, and said to Al-Mut'im: "A protector or a follower?" (Meaning, have you become a Muslim following him or just a protector?).

Al-Mut'im said: "Rather, a protector."

Abu Jahl said, submitting to tribal custom: "We grant protection to whom you have granted protection."

The Prophet ﷺ circumambulated the Kaaba and prayed two units (Rak'ahs), then left for his house under the guard of Al-Mut'im bin Adi's swords.

A Lesson in the "Jurisprudence of Vulnerability":

This situation teaches us that "Disbelief is one religion" in creed, but it is "not one" in morals and stances. There is a belligerent, malicious disbeliever (like Abu Jahl), and there is a fair, chivalrous disbeliever (like Al-Mut'im).

It teaches us the permissibility of "seeking help from a polytheist" to ward off harm or bring about benefit, as long as that does not touch the constants of religion or result in humiliation. The Prophet ﷺ accepted the protection of a polytheist to protect the "Caller," so that the "Call" could continue. The Prophet ﷺ preserved this favor for Al-Mut'im, eulogizing him and mentioning him with goodness even after his death, saying regarding the captives of Badr: "If Al-Mut'im bin Adi were alive and spoke to me regarding these filthy ones, I would have let them go for his sake."

6. Al-Isra' and Mi'raj: The Journey of "Promotion" and Soothing the Heart

After the raging storm of Ta'if, and after the doors of the earth were locked in the face of the Call (Mecca refused, and Ta'if stoned), Divine Honor came to open the doors of the Sky. The incident of "Al-Isra' wal-Mi'raj" (The Night Journey and Ascension) was not merely a sensory miracle breaking the laws of nature; it was a "healing journey" for the soul, a "promotion" of status, and a cosmic declaration that this Prophet who was expelled from his land is the Master of the people of Heaven and Earth, and that his Call, besieged in the ravines of Mecca, will reach the horizons and transcend the boundaries of time and space.

Timing: The Gift is Born from the Womb of Ordeal

Historians differ in determining its exact date, but it is most likely that it was after the journey to Ta'if, and before the Migration (Hijrah) by a year or so (in the tenth, eleventh, or twelfth year of Prophethood). It came in the "Year of Sorrow" (Aam al-Huzn), to serve as a "Divine Soothing" for the wounded heart. It was as if the situation was saying: "If the people of earth have shunned you, the people of heaven celebrate you; and if the kings of earth have locked their doors, the King of Kings has opened for you the doors of the High Heavens."

The Second Splitting of the Chest: The Process of "Adornment" after "Purification"

The journey began with a deeply symbolic event. While the Prophet ﷺ was sleeping in the Hatim (or Hijr) next to the Kaaba, Gabriel peace be upon him came to him and split his noble chest for the second time (the first was in his childhood with Halima Al-Sa'diyyah).

  • The First Time (Childhood): The splitting was for "Purification" (Takhliya), where Gabriel extracted the "black clot" (Satan's share) from his heart, to cleanse and protect him from sins.
  • The Second Time (Al-Isra'): The splitting was for "Adornment and Filling" (Tahliya). He washed his heart with Zamzam water, then brought a golden basin filled with wisdom and faith, emptied it into his chest ﷺ, and then closed it.

This operation was "spiritual and psychological preparation" to withstand the horrors of the celestial journey, to witness the Great Signs of Allah, and to comprehend the "obligation of Prayer" which would be the direct connection between the servant and his Lord. It is a lesson in the necessity of "cleansing the vessel" (the heart) before "filling" it with Light.

The Journey of "Al-Isra'": From the Sanctuary to the Farthest

Al-Buraq (a white beast, tall, larger than a donkey and smaller than a mule, which places its hoof at the limit of its sight) was brought, and the Prophet ﷺ mounted it accompanied by Gabriel. The earthly journey (Al-Isra') launched from the Sacred Mosque (Al-Masjid Al-Haram) in Mecca to the Farthest Mosque (Al-Masjid Al-Aqsa) in Jerusalem.

“Exalted is He who took His Servant by night from al-Masjid al-Haram to al-Masjid al-Aqsa, whose surroundings We have blessed, to show him of Our signs.” [Al-Isra': 1].

Significance of Linking the Two Mosques:

  1. Unity of Messages: The link between the "Qibla of previous Prophets" (Al-Aqsa) and the "Qibla of Final Islam" (The Kaaba) is an indication that Islam is the legitimate heir to all heavenly messages, and the trustee of their sanctities.
  2. Sanctity of Al-Aqsa: Affirmation of the status of Jerusalem in Islamic creed, and that it is the sibling of the Meccan Sanctuary, and no Muslim is to forfeit it.

Leading the Prophets: Handing Over the Banner of Leadership

In Jerusalem, Allah gathered for His Prophet ﷺ the souls of the Prophets and Messengers (and it is said their bodies and souls) in a majestic scene that had never occurred in human history. Gabriel stepped forward, took the hand of the Prophet ﷺ, and presented him to lead them all in prayer.

Muhammad ﷺ prayed as Imam leading Adam, Noah, Abraham, Moses, Jesus, and all the Prophets.

This prayer was an official "handover ceremony" for the banner of guidance. The era of messages specific to certain peoples ended, and the era of the "Universal Final Message" began. By his leading them, they all acknowledged his virtue and leadership, and that he is the "Master of the Children of Adam" as he informed about himself. It was a message of reassurance to him: "You are not alone; you are an extension of this great luminous procession, and you are their leader."

7. In the Realm of High Heavens: Al-Mi'raj and Details of the Celestial Journey

After the Prophet ﷺ led the Prophets in Al-Aqsa Mosque, the "Mi'raj" (Ascension - like a ladder or luminous elevator in which the souls of Adam's children ascend) was set up for him, and he ascended in it accompanied by the Trustworthy Spirit, Gabriel peace be upon him. It was not an ascent into open space without a monitor, but an ascent into a "guarded kingdom" with gates and guards, governed by strict heavenly protocols.

The Protocol of Permission: Heaven is not an "Agency without a Doorman"

At every heaven of the seven heavens, a majestic scene occurred indicating the greatness of the Divine System. Gabriel knocks on the door, and the Keeper (Guard of the Heaven) asks: "Who is this?" He replies: "Gabriel." The Guard asks: "And who is with you?" He says: "Muhammad." The Guard asks a fundamental question: "Has he been sent for?" (Meaning, has the High Command come for his ascension?). Gabriel replies: "Yes." Only then are the gates opened with a single welcoming phrase: "Welcome to him, what an excellent arrival has come."

Map of Prophetic Encounters: Details of Sightings in the Seven Heavens

In every heaven, Divine Care arranged special meetings for the Prophet ﷺ with his brothers among the Prophets, each carrying meanings and symbols, and the Prophet ﷺ described them accurately in authentic Hadiths:

  1. First Heaven (Adam and the Souls): He met his father Adam (آدم) peace be upon him, and described him as a tall man. To the right of Adam were "black shapes" (Aswidah - shadows or many souls) and to his left were "black shapes." When he looked to his right he laughed and rejoiced, and when he looked to his left he wept and grieved. The Prophet ﷺ asked Gabriel, who said: "This is Adam, and the black shapes to his right and left are his sons; the people of the right are the people of Paradise, and the black shapes to his left are the people of Hellfire." Adam welcomed his son saying: "Welcome to the righteous son and the righteous Prophet."
  2. Second Heaven (The Maternal Cousins): He met Yahya bin Zakariya (John) and Isa bin Maryam (Jesus) peace be upon them. The Prophet ﷺ described Jesus as "a man of medium height, reddish-white, with lank hair (not curly), as if he had just come out of a bathhouse (Dimas)" meaning water seemed to drip from his hair due to intense freshness. They welcomed him saying: "Welcome to the righteous brother and the righteous Prophet."
  3. Third Heaven (Joseph and Beauty): He met Yusuf (Joseph) peace be upon him, and the Prophet ﷺ described him with a phrase summarizing all meanings of beauty: "Behold, he has been given half of beauty." (Meaning half the beauty of mankind). He welcomed him and prayed for his good.
  4. Fourth Heaven (Idris): He met Idris (Enoch) peace be upon him, confirming the Almighty's saying: {And We raised him to a high station.} He welcomed him and prayed for him.
  5. Fifth Heaven (Aaron): He met Harun (Aaron) peace be upon him, who was beloved among his people; he returned the greeting and welcomed him.
  6. Sixth Heaven (Moses and Weeping): He met Musa (Moses) the One Spoken to by Allah. He described him as "a dark-skinned man (Adam), tall, with much hair, as if he were from the men of Shanu'ah (an Arab tribe known for the strength of their bodies)." Moses welcomed him saying: "Welcome to the righteous brother and the righteous Prophet." When the Prophet ﷺ passed him, Moses wept. He was asked: What makes you weep? He said: "I weep because a young man sent after me will have more of his nation enter Paradise than will enter from my nation." (This was weeping of envy [in a good way/Ghibta] and mercy, and keenness on the large number of his followers, not weeping of jealousy).
  7. Seventh Heaven (Abraham and the Frequented House): He met his father Ibrahim (Abraham) peace be upon him (the Friend of Allah), and the scene was majestic; he saw him leaning his back against "Al-Bayt Al-Ma'mur" (The Frequented House). The Frequented House is the Kaaba of the people of heaven (parallel to the Kaaba on earth); seventy thousand angels enter it every day to pray and never return to it until the Day of Judgment (due to the multitude of angels). The Prophet ﷺ said describing Abraham: "Behold, he is the one most resembling your companion" (referring to himself ﷺ). Abraham welcomed him saying: "Welcome to the righteous son and the righteous Prophet."

Sidrat al-Muntaha: Beauty Beyond Description

Then the Prophet ﷺ was raised to "Sidrat al-Muntaha" (The Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary), a great tree located in the furthest part of Paradise, where the knowledge of creatures ends. The Prophet ﷺ described it with precise visual description in the Sahih:

  • "Its fruits (Nabq) are like the jars of Hajar" (large jars made in the region of Hajar).
  • "And its leaves are like the ears of elephants" (in size and width).
  • It is covered by what covers it by the command of Allah, changing its colors. He ﷺ said: "No one of Allah's creation can describe it due to its beauty."
  • In another narration: "It is covered by moths of gold."

The Four Rivers and the Test of "Fitrah"

At the root of the Lote Tree, the Prophet ﷺ saw four rivers:

  1. Two hidden rivers: Gabriel said: "They are in Paradise" (Al-Kawthar and Salsabil).
  2. Two visible rivers: Gabriel said: "The Nile and the Euphrates." (This is an indication of the blessing of these two rivers, and glad tidings of the spread of Islam in their lands).

Then three vessels were brought to the Prophet ﷺ: a vessel containing wine, a vessel containing milk, and a vessel containing honey. The Prophet ﷺ took the vessel of milk. Gabriel said to him: "It is the Fitrah (natural instinct/innate nature) upon which you and your nation are; had you taken the wine, your nation would have gone astray."

The Scratching of Pens and the Obligation of Prayer (The Greatest Gift)

Then he ﷺ ascended to a level reached by neither human nor angel, until Gabriel was cut off from him, and he heard the "Scratching of the Pens" (the sound of the pens of Decree writing what is to be). There, the Prophet ﷺ drew near to his Lord, a drawing near of honor and nobility, and Allah spoke to him without an intermediary, imposing upon him the greatest obligation of Islam: Prayer (Salah).

  • The First Obligation: Fifty prayers in the day and night.
  • Moses' Advisory Role: The Prophet ﷺ descended and passed by Moses peace be upon him, who asked: "What did your Lord impose upon your nation?" He said: "Fifty prayers." Moses said (being experienced with human nature): "Return to your Lord and ask Him for reduction; for your nation cannot bear that, for I have tested the Children of Israel and tried them."
  • Negotiating Reduction: The Prophet ﷺ returned to his Lord asking for reduction, so He removed ten, then he passed by Moses who told him to return, and he kept going back and forth between his Lord and Moses, until they became five prayers.
  • Five for Fifty: Moses said: "Return to your Lord and ask Him for reduction." The Prophet ﷺ said: "I have become shy before my Lord, but I am satisfied and I submit." Then a caller called out from Allah Almighty: "I have confirmed My obligation and alleviated the burden on My servants; they are five in action, and fifty in reward." {The word will not be changed with Me.}

Thus, the Muhammadan Nation returned with the gift of Heaven: a prayer small in number, great in reward, the daily ascension of the believer to connect with his Lord just as his Prophet connected on the night of Ascension.

8. Return to Mecca: The Battle of "Verification" and the Birth of the Title "Al-Siddiq"

On the way back from the celestial journey, and before entering Mecca, the Prophet ﷺ passed by a caravan of Quraish returning from the Levant, and they were sleeping. He recognized them and their luggage, found a vessel containing water covered, uncovered it, drank what was in it, and then covered it again as it was. These precise details would act as the "Black Box" he would later use as irrefutable physical evidence.

The Morning of Challenge: (Would that I had died before this!)

Morning dawned upon the Prophet ﷺ in Mecca while he was sitting alone in the Hijr, distressed and sad. He knew with certainty that what happened last night was a matter "beyond the level of the human mind," and telling Quraish about it would be tantamount to missionary suicide in the view of logical calculations. How could he tell people who take a month to travel to Jerusalem and a month to return, that he crossed it, ascended to the sky, and returned, all before his bed had cooled?

Worry squeezed his noble heart for fear that people might apostatize from the religion due to lack of comprehension, or that Quraish would increase in denial and mockery.

Abu Jahl's Trap: Luring the Victim

The "Pharaoh of this Ummah," Abu Jahl (أبو جهل), passed by him and saw the Prophet ﷺ in a state of brokenness and isolation. He sat with him mockingly and said: "Is there any news?"

The Prophet ﷺ said with the honesty of Prophethood that knows no evasion: "Yes, I was taken by night (Isra') tonight."

Abu Jahl was stunned and said with disapproval: "To where?"

He said: "To Bayt Al-Maqdis (Jerusalem)."

He said: "Then you woke up here among us?!"

He said: "Yes."

Here, Abu Jahl's eyes gleamed with intense malice. He saw in this news the "Golden Opportunity" to destroy Muhammad's ﷺ call forever, not by the sword, but by mockery and proving insanity before everyone. He did not deny him to his face immediately, fearing the Prophet ﷺ might retract his words; rather, he said cunningly: "Do you see if I call your people, will you tell them what you told me?"

He ﷺ said confidently: "Yes."

The Farce and the Magic Turning on the Magician

Abu Jahl called out at the top of his voice: "O assembly of Bani Ka'b bin Lu'ay! Come here." The gatherings rushed to him, and people came running.

Abu Jahl said: "Tell your people what you told me."

The Prophet ﷺ stood tall and dropped the "Bomb of Truth": "I was taken by night tonight to Bayt Al-Maqdis."

The assembly exploded with hysterical reactions; some clapped (in mockery), some placed their hands on their heads (in amazement and disapproval), and Quraish reverted to its limited material logic saying: "We beat the livers of camels (travel) to it for a month, and return in a month; yet you go and return in one night?!" Matters reached such a point that some of weak faith who had recently converted turned back on their heels as a trial and test.

The Visual Miracle: (So Allah Displayed Jerusalem to Me)

The polytheists wanted to embarrass the Prophet ﷺ in front of everyone, so they said: "O Muhammad, describe Bayt Al-Maqdis to us, for some of us have traveled to it and seen it."

Here, the Prophet ﷺ fell into severe distress; he had gone at night, entered for prayer and worship, and was not a tourist examining columns, decorations, and windows, nor did he memorize the details of the architectural structure.

He ﷺ says describing that difficult moment: "I was distressed with a distress the like of which I had never felt."

But, because Allah does not forsake His allies, the "Visual Miracle" occurred. Allah ordered Gabriel to lift the image of Jerusalem before the Prophet's ﷺ eyes (as if looking at a display screen or live broadcast), so he began looking at it and describing it to them door by door, place by place.

He ﷺ says: "So Allah displayed Jerusalem to me, and I began informing them of its signs while looking at it."

The polytheists were stunned by the accuracy of the description and could not find a single loophole, so they said compulsively: "As for the description, he was correct."

The Physical Evidence: The Caravan and the Ashen Camel

When the Prophet ﷺ saw their arrogance, he moved from the evidence of "Description" to the evidence of "Reality," informing them of matters known only to someone who was truly there. He told them:

  1. News of the First Caravan: I passed by a caravan of such-and-such tribe at "Al-Rawha," and they had lost a camel and were searching for it, and I drank their water which was in a covered bowl. (When the caravan arrived, they asked them, and they confirmed the truth of the lost camel and the missing water).
  2. News of the Incoming Caravan: He told them about another caravan coming from "Al-Tan'im," led by an "ashen camel" (gray like ash) carrying two sacks (Gharartan).

They said: "When will it arrive?" He said: "It will rise upon you at sunrise."

Quraish went out rushing to "Al-Thaniyah" (the entrance of Mecca) waiting, their eyes watching the disk of the sun, wishing Muhammad would lie. When the sun rose, a shouter cried: "Here is the sun risen," and before they could complete their joy, another shouted: "And this, by Allah, is the caravan approaching led by an ashen camel, just as Muhammad said!"

Narrative, rational, and physical proofs combined, but, as is the habit of arrogance, they said: "This is manifest magic!"

The Stance of "Al-Siddiq": Establishing the Methodology of Belief

In the midst of this trial, the polytheists rushed to Abu Bakr (أبو بكر) may Allah be pleased with him, thinking they had finally found what would shake his faith in his companion. They said to him with gloating: "Have you heard what your companion says? He claims he was taken by night to Jerusalem and then returned!"

Abu Bakr's response is taught in the colleges of Creed and Faith Logic. He did not ask for details, nor did he ask for evidence; rather, he asked one question to verify the "Source":

He said: "Did he indeed say that?"

They said: "Yes."

He said his immortal words: "If he said that, then he has spoken the truth."

They marveled and said: "Do you believe him that he went to the Levant and returned in one night?"

He replied with the logic of "Mental Priorities":

"I believe him in what is further than that; I believe him regarding the news of Heaven, coming to him in the morning or evening."

(Meaning: If I believe him that Gabriel descends upon him from above seven heavens in moments with Revelation, how can I find it strange that he goes to Jerusalem in a night? He who has the power for the first has the power for the second).

From that moment, Abu Bakr deservedly earned the title "Al-Siddiq" (The Truthful/The Confirmer of Truth), because he presented a model of "Absolute Submission" based on trust in the Source. This is the most important lesson concluding this phase of the Seerah: Belief in the Unseen is the real test that distinguishes the ranks before entering the phase of empowerment and state-building.

1. In the Tent of Umm Ma'bad (أم معبد): The Passing Encounter Preserved by History

In those defining days of human history, while the desert of the Arabian Peninsula witnessed the greatest strategic transition for the Islamic call—represented by the "Prophetic Migration" (Hijrah)—a story was being woven in a simple Bedouin tent. This story would later become the most important and accurate document describing the Prophet ﷺ. It was not merely a fleeting rest stop for travelers; it was a moment where "Muhammadan Beauty" and "Prophetic Blessing" manifested before the naked eye of a woman who knew nothing of Islam and did not realize she was standing before the Prophet of the End Times.

The Scene: Dry Land and Hardship

On the rugged road between Makkah (مكة) and Madinah (المدينة), specifically in the area of Qudayd (قديد), the noble Prophetic caravan was moving hastily. It included the Prophet ﷺ, his companion Abu Bakr (أبو بكر), their guide Abdullah bin Uraiqit (عبد الله بن أريقط), and Abu Bakr’s servant, Amir bin Fuhayra (عامر بن فهيرة). The heat was intense, provisions had run out, and the search for Qira (hospitality) and food became a necessity to continue the journey.

A tent appeared to them belonging to a woman from the Khhuza'a tribe named Atikah bint Khuwaylid (عاتكة بنت خويلد), known as Umm Ma'bad (أم معبد). She was no ordinary woman; narratives describe her as "a strong, resilient woman" (Jildah), and "visible" (Barzah—meaning she sat in the courtyard of her tent, accessible to people), chaste, and generous, accustomed to feeding and giving water to passersby.

The caravan stopped to ask her to purchase meat or dates. However—by sheer misfortune—it was a time of drought and famine. She told them in frank Bedouin dialect: "By Allah, if we had anything, you would not have needed to search or ask." All the sheep had gone out with her husband, Abu Ma'bad (أبو معبد), to search for any grass in that barren land, and nothing significant remained in the tent.

The Miracle of the Sheep: When Blessing Turns Nothingness into Existence

In the corner of the tent, the Prophet ﷺ spotted a solitary, secluded sheep. He asked, "What is this sheep, O Umm Ma'bad (أم معبد)?"

She replied with sorrow: "A sheep kept behind from the flock by exhaustion" (meaning it was too weak and emaciated to go out for grazing).

The Prophet ﷺ asked a question that seemed strange given its emaciation: "Does it have any milk?"

She replied, ruling it out completely: "It is too exhausted for that."

Here, with the utmost Prophetic politeness, he asked her permission: "Do you permit me to milk it?"

She agreed, thinking it a futile attempt, saying: "By my father and mother, if you see any milk in it, then milk it."

The Prophet ﷺ approached the sheep. In that moment, the laws of nature submitted to the station of Prophethood. He placed his noble hand on the sheep’s udder, mentioned the name of Allah, and prayed for blessing. Suddenly, what stunned Umm Ma'bad occurred; the sheep spread its legs (to be milked) and milk flowed abundantly, filling the udder that had been dry just moments before.

The Prophet ﷺ asked for a large vessel sufficient for the group. He milked until froth rose to the top. Then, the lessons of the "School of Muhammad" in altruism and etiquette began:

  1. He gave her drink first: He presented the vessel to Umm Ma'bad (أم معبد) until she was satisfied, honoring her as the hostess.
  2. He gave drink to his companions: Then he gave drink to Abu Bakr (أبو بكر) and those with him until they were all quenched.
  3. He drank last: He ﷺ drank last, applying his eternal ethical rule: "The one who serves water to the people is the last of them to drink."

He did not stop there; he milked a second time until he filled the vessel again and left it with her, then they departed.

The Testimony of the "Blind" Regarding Identity
