Sa'd stood over them with the stance of a confident, obeyed leader, and began a decisive dialogue aimed at settling the matter without equivocation or long gradation.
He said: "O Banu Abd al-Ashhal, how do you know my status among you?"
They said with one voice: "Our master, the best of us in opinion, and the most auspicious in nature."
Then Sa'd dropped his resounding bombshell, utilizing this massive reserve of trust and loyalty: "Then the speech of your men and women is forbidden to me until you believe in Allah and His Messenger."

Sa'd placed them before a difficult choice with no escape: either Islam or total severance from their beloved leader. Because they trusted his mind and knew he would not lead them to ruin, and because the light of Allah had begun to seep in, the response was amazing and unprecedented in Arab history.
Historians say: "By evening, there was not a man or woman in the dwellings of Banu Abd al-Ashhal who was not a Muslim."
Only one man among them, Al-Usayrim [الأصيرم] (Amr bin Thabit), delayed his Islam slightly, then converted on the day of Uhud, fought until he was martyred without prostrating a single prostration to Allah. The Prophet ﷺ said of him: "He worked little and was rewarded much," and he entered Paradise.

Thus, with a sincere word from a loyal leader, an entire tribe converted in one day, and the houses of Banu Abd al-Ashhal (from Aws) became among the greatest fortresses of Islam in Medina, competing with the houses of Banu an-Najjar (from Khazraj).

Second: The Lesson is in Sincerity and Impact, Not Length of Life

The biography of Sa'd bin Mu'adh confronts us with an astonishing fact calling for contemplation; this great master lived in Islam for only six years.
He converted at age 30 and died a martyr from his wounds in the Battle of the Trench (Al-Khandaq/Al-Ahzab) at age 36.
Only six years! But what did he achieve in them?

  1. He established Allah's law among his people: They all converted by his word.
  2. He supported the Prophet ﷺ at Badr: With his immortal stance saying: "By Him who sent you with the Truth, if you were to cross this sea and wade into it, we would wade into it with you..."
  3. He defended the Messenger at Uhud: He was among the steadfast around him when people fled.
  4. He judged by Allah's judgment at the Trench: When the Jews of Banu Qurayzah [بنو قريظة] agreed to his judgment, he ruled to execute their fighters and take their progeny captive. The Prophet ﷺ told him: "You have judged among them with the judgment of Allah from above seven heavens."

Third: The Shaking of the Throne and the Funeral of Angels

Allah, the Majestic, the Appreciative, the Forbearing, rewarded this truthful servant with a reward He granted to no other human after the Prophets.
In the Sahihayn, the Prophet ﷺ said when Sa'd died: "The Throne of the Most Merciful shook for the death of Sa'd bin Mu'adh." [اهتز عرش الرحمن لموت سعد بن معاذ].
This great Throne, the heaviest and greatest of creations, shook in joy at the arrival of Sa'd's soul, or shook due to the greatness of his death, indicating this man's status with Allah.
Not only that, but seventy thousand angels witnessed his funeral, descending to escort him, to the point that the hypocrites said mockingly: "How light is his bier." The Prophet ﷺ replied: "The angels were carrying him."
Furthermore, the Prophet ﷺ was gifted a silk robe from Paradise, and when his companions marveled at its softness, he said: "By the One in Whose hand is Muhammad's soul, the handkerchiefs of Sa'd bin Mu'adh in Paradise are softer than this."

In Conclusion:

The story of Sa'd bin Mu'adh is not just history to be told, but a message to every Muslim: "Do not belittle your short life, nor your limited capabilities. The value is not in how long you lived, but how you lived."
Truthfulness with Allah folds time, blesses deeds, and makes six years weigh as much as the life of Noah (PBUH) in the sight of Allah. Sa'd bin Mu'adh is the practical proof that one truthful man, possessing a living heart and burning determination, is capable of changing the course of history, and that his death can shake the pillars of Heaven.

We ask Allah to grant us the truthfulness of Sa'd, the steadfastness of Sa'd, and to resurrect us in the company of Sa'd, with the Prophets, the Truthful, the Martyrs, and the Righteous; and excellent are those as companions.
